- The power P comes from the table in the Layout section, at the index for the hero row and a Mote id. It is read from the table at start-up.
- P is not 1.0 in three cases only: Tor's melee hits in the power step (sub-state 10, with a Mote in word 2; key = that Mote), the hits of a Mote power-attack object (key = the object's own Mote id), and Roberta's full-release shots (key = the Mote in word 2). Every other hit uses P = 1.0, including all of Roberta's melee hits (Damage).
- The attack id is the item id of the Mote in equipment word 2 (SPEC-0021). An empty word 2 gives no attack id.
- The table is data, not code. A clean implementation must read it, not hard-code it.

### Hero target pick (stick-aligned)

Confidence: High for the structure, the three windows, the stick-length gate, the failure return and the cooldown; Medium-High for the intent of the neighbour-link terms; High for the stick divisor (taken from SPEC-0033).

The routine looks for a target in front of the hero and stores it in the hero's target field (+0x9D0). It is a third target finder, separate from the actor pick of SPEC-0033 Behavior 19 and the predicate of SPEC-0033 Behavior 22 (Conflict C5, closed below). It walks the nearby-object list and considers only entries whose kind byte (at +0x4C of the entry) is -2. Kind -2 records are climbable panels: this pick is the climb-panel grab from the ground, and a success enters climb sub-mode 1 (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51, state 21) (Medium-High).

Cooldown: when the hero's cooldown word at +0x9CC is 1 or more, the routine returns 0 at once and leaves the target unchanged.

Otherwise each candidate is tested in the order below. The first candidate that passes every test becomes the target.
1. Facing: two dot products must both be strictly below -0.8. One uses the candidate direction and the motion record's forward basis vector; the other uses the candidate direction and the heading vector stored in the hero's locomotion block. They are distinct vectors and both tests must pass (High for the returned fields and comparison; Medium for their semantic labels in this picker).
2. Range (first window): let p be the hero's position, the four-lane vector at +0x50 of the record at +0x16C (its fourth lane is 1). The signed distance of p from the panel's plane, d = n . p with the plane offset in n's fourth lane, must lie strictly between minus R and 1.25 R, where R is the hero radius (the float at offset +8 of the record at +0x16C). This is a band on the distance from the panel's face, not a height test.
3. Second window (along the panel's side axis): u = U . p, the hero's position along the panel's side axis U (the record's +0x20 vector, offset in its fourth lane, so u is measured from the panel's start), must lie between a lower limit and an upper limit. The lower limit is +1, or -1 when the panel's first neighbour link (+0x54) is non-zero. The upper limit is the panel's length LU (+0x40) minus 1, or LU plus 1 when the panel's last neighbour link (+0x5C) is non-zero. So the hero must stand at least 1 unit inside a panel edge, and may stand up to 1 unit beyond an edge that has a neighbouring panel linked (the neighbour links are those of the record layout in SPEC-0006, type 10; the hero's own position is the reference vector, the same one as in the first window).
4. Third window (along the panel's up axis): v = V . p, the hero's position along the panel's up axis V (the record's +0x30 vector, offset in its fourth lane), must lie between -1 and the panel's height LV (+0x44) minus 1. This window has no neighbour-link term.
5. Stick: the stick vector is the two dead-zoned stick values of SPEC-0033 Behavior 2, divided by the stick scale 87.5 (word 0x319524). Its length must be above 0.5. The candidate's direction is not compared with the stick. A stick length of 0.5 or less skips the candidate.

Success: the candidate becomes the target, the climb and ladder sub-mode word (+0x9BC; SPEC-0033 Behavior 51) is set to 1, and the routine returns 1.
Failure: when no candidate passes, the target is set to none and the routine returns 0.

Conflict C5 (closed, High): this pick, the SPEC-0033 Behavior 19 actor pick and the SPEC-0033 Behavior 22 predicate are three separate routines. The ground-input caller tries them in that order and short-circuits on success. If Behavior 19 fails, this pick runs; if this pick succeeds, it clears the pick cooldown and sends event 0x15 (state 21), then does not call Behavior 22. Behavior 22 runs only when both earlier picks fail. If it returns false, the caller continues into later near-object and interaction checks; if it returns true, those checks also continue using its stored target. The later consumer of a successful Behavior 22 target is not established by the data and needs a runtime check (Default (rewrite choice): the stored target is used only by the later near-object checks named above). The earlier interpretation that this pick compares the candidate's direction with the stick belongs to Behavior 22, not to this pick. Synthetic vector: actor pick false, this pick true => target becomes the passing kind -2 panel, stance sub-mode 1, cooldown 0, state-21 event sent, third pick skipped.

Conflict C6 (closed, Medium-High for the intent): the word +0x9BC is the climb (state-21) and ladder sub-mode, and this pick writes 1 to it on success. The write is deliberate: a successful pick enters climb sub-mode 1, the ground entry of the climb. The hero's per-tick driver runs the cascade before its state switch, so in a tick where the hero is in state 21, the pick's write comes before that tick's state-21 dispatch (High for the order). In the cascade branch that calls the pick with its flag set, a success is followed by the hero's entry into state 21 after a helper (High for the write; the other call site's outcome needs a runtime check (Default (rewrite choice): same entry); see Open questions).

### Hero clear-path predicate (clearance test)

Confidence: High for the blocker same-link skip and footprint call; Medium-High for the other steps; Medium for the blocking-rule edge cases (see Open questions).

The predicate returns 0 when a blocker lies on the way and 1 when the path is clear. It is not the actor-to-actor clear path of SPEC-0018 (High). It has four steps:
1. Height: the height of the query point must be within 0.25 of the ground height at that point (a code constant). The ground height is read by the ground helper with a ceiling of 8.0. If the height test fails, the result is blocked (0).
2. Blockers: the hero keeps a list of sub-lists at +0x174. Each object in it is probed with the footprint family of SPEC-0018 (its one-face form), with the hero radius R and an extent of 2 times the hero's +0x16C+0xC value. One hit blocks. A blocker probe is skipped only when both the blocker and the hero's current target have nonnegative kind bytes, both have a non-null +0x48 link, and the linked records' +0x64 values match; otherwise the normal footprint probe runs (Open questions for the semantic meaning of the shared link value).
3. Nearby objects: for each object of kind -3 in the nearby-object list, the three window tests apply in order. The range window is strict: the projection must be greater than minus R and less than 1.25 times R. The second window is inclusive from minus R through the object's extent at +0x40 plus R. The third window is inclusive from -1 through the object's extent at +0x44 minus a margin. The margin is the hero's +0x16C+0xC value plus 1.25, except that it is 0 when the helper returns 30. A nearby object blocks only when it passes all three windows. If any window fails, that object is ignored and the scan continues; a later object can still block.
4. Result: blocked (0) if any step blocks; otherwise clear (1).

Caller: the hero state 0x17 handler (the sidle, state 23) calls the predicate. Clear (1) does nothing. Blocked (0) raises the hero's event 0x50 through its state table. The query point is the hero's position plus a multiple of the motion-record side basis (row at +0x290): minus 0.25 times it while the current sidle clip is 79, and plus 0.25 times it while the current clip is 81 (when neither clip is current the routine does not call the predicate). The predicate's only caller is that handler, and state 23 is never entered with shipped data (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51), so the predicate does not run in play and a clean build may omit it; which of the two directions the player calls left or right therefore does not matter. The blocker height step, the footprint probe and the same-link skip are as stated above. The shared link value in the skip rule is the part-record index of the model-set entry that the blocker's frame record is bound to (SPEC-0028, part record, index into the type 5 entry table): a blocker that belongs to the same model-set entry as the hero's current target does not block.
Test vectors (synthetic; the predicate is dormant in play): clip 79 current, hero position (0, 0, 0), side basis (1, 0, 0): the query point is (-0.25, 0, 0). Clip 81 current: (0.25, 0, 0). Blocker and target both with kind byte 0, both linked, links with equal entry index 5: the blocker is skipped; entry indexes 5 and 6: the footprint probe runs; blocker kind byte -1: the probe runs.
Synthetic vector for the nearby-object pass: let R = 1, the hero margin term be 3.25, and a candidate's extents at +0x40 and +0x44 both be 4. A candidate with range projection 0, second-window projection 0, and third-window projection 1 passes the first two windows but fails the third because its upper bound is 0.75; it is ignored. If the helper returns 30, the margin becomes 0 and that same third projection passes the third window (upper bound 4), so this candidate blocks. This vector exercises the branch rule; it does not assign a gameplay meaning to helper result 30.

### Hero collision-kind contact tests

Confidence: High for the caller, the gate order and the effect; Medium-High for the geometry of each shape test; Medium for how it feels in play. These tests are the hero's landing break on world breakables (SPEC-0048). They are not a visibility test and not a target-directed attack test.

Caller and order. In the hero's motion step the world-breakable walk of the hero's grid cell (the cell's breakable list at hero +0x174, the same list that Hit detection step 6 walks for a swing) offers every entry whose state word is 1 or 3 and that has a volume record to the hero's own landing test first. When the landing test reports a hit, the entry is handled by the breakable contact rule below and the normal solid response of the motion step (SPEC-0018) is skipped for that entry on that tick. When it reports no hit, the solid response runs as for any other character.

Gate (all volume kinds). The hero must be falling (the vertical component of its motion record's velocity is below 0) and in one of the jump states 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 43, 44, 45 or 46 (state 11 is included). Otherwise there is no hit.

Shape test, by the volume's kind byte (+0x10). R and H are the hero motion record's radius (+0x08) and height (+0x0C); the hero's reference point is its position plus this tick's pending step.
- Kind 2 (cone): never a hit.
- Kind 1 (sphere: centre +0x30, radius in the fourth lane): the hero's capsule (base at the reference point, axis world up, radius R, height H) overlaps the sphere. Top of the shape = centre y + radius.
- Kind 3 (cylinder: base +0x30, radius in the fourth lane, length +0x24, axis +0x40): the static overlap of SPEC-0018 (Finite cylinder tests), where the first shape is a solid round cylinder and the second acts as a flat card, between the cylinder (centre = base + half the length along the axis) and the hero's box (radius R, length H, centre = reference point + half of H along the hero's up row, axis = that row, the second row of the hero's frame block). Top of the shape = base y + axis y x length.
- Kind 4 or 7 with the float at +0x2C of 0 or below: the landing test of SPEC-0018. Top = (base +0x30 + the vector at +0x60 x the length at +0x24), y component.
- Kind 4 or 7 with +0x2C above 0: the one-face footprint query of SPEC-0018 (One-face test of a moving body) with radius R and the reference point; the query's normal-flip allowance is off. Top as for the line above.

Height gate. After the shape test hits, the hit counts only when top - 1.5 is strictly less than the hero's position y. So a falling hero breaks a breakable only while its position is within 1.5 units below the top of the shape or above it (and its box still overlaps the shape).

Effect. A hit runs the breakable contact rule of the hero route (Contact routines and cues, hero melee route): script hook, then the element gate (the entry's element byte is 0, equals the element kind of the Mote in equipment word 2, or is 7), then the break with a 0.25 piece push and the cue chosen by the material word (+0x5C). On an element mismatch the entry stays intact, the hero's value at +0x1560 is set to 4 and, for Tor only, the attack sub-state becomes 13 (the recoil). The landing test reports a hit in every one of these cases, including the hook case and the mismatch, so the solid response is skipped on that tick even when nothing breaks (reproduce).

Disc data. Of the 2,073 breakable entries, 2,030 have a volume: 1,915 cylinders (kind 3) and 115 rectangles (kind 4) with +0x2C above 0. No sphere, cone, kind 7 or non-positive rectangle occurs, and all entries start in state 1. So on disc only the cylinder and the positive-extent rectangle tests can fire.

Test vectors (synthetic; R = 1.0, H = 3.0, cylinder base (0, 0, 0), axis (0, 1, 0), radius 1.2, length 2.4, element 0, hero over the axis with no pending step): state 8, velocity y = -2, position y = 2.0: boxes overlap, top 2.4 - 1.5 = 0.9 is below 2.0, hit: the entry breaks and the solid response is skipped. Position y = 0.9: boxes overlap but 0.9 is not below 0.9, no hit; the solid response runs. Velocity y = 0 or above, or state 13 or 2: no hit. Same hero over an entry of element 5 with a Mote of kind 2 equipped: hit, no break; Tor goes to attack sub-state 13 and Roberta does nothing; the entry is not pushed out on that tick.

### Target marker

Confidence: High for the release gate, the state-to-kind map and the position; Medium-High for the effect that is shown; Low for how it looks on screen. The marker is a ground effect under the hero, not a lock-on mark on a target. It uses one shared marker object that starts effect definition 401 of the shared effect group (SPEC-0034), a definition that exists in the table: four emitters, each showing a child model with no sprite material (start sizes 1.3 x 1 x 1.3, 2 x 1 x 1.3 and 1.3 x 1 x 1.3 for the first three, one particle every 0.6, 0.1 and 0.1 s with lives 1.5, 1.0 and 0.8 s, and for the fourth a single burst of 30 particles of life 0.75 s that start at size 0).

Each tick the marker updater does one of two things. It releases the current marker object when the marker-enable word (hero +0x17C, the ground entry the ground probe selected this tick; it is 0 at creation) is zero, or when the release gate below is 1. Otherwise it makes sure the marker object exists, puts it at (hero x, ground reference height + 0.02, hero z), where the ground reference height is the hero motion record's value at +0x140, orients it from the hero's frame, and selects the kind: 1 for hero state 3, 2 for states 4 and 6, and 0 for every other state. A change of kind releases the object and starts it again.

Release gate. Let s1 be the motion record's ground gap (+0x24, position y minus ground height) and s2 its reference height (+0x140). The gate is 1 exactly when s1 > 0.1 and s2 > 0.1 and not (the hero is in a jump state, 7 to 11 or 43 to 46, and its mode word +0x1508 is 6, 7, 9 or 10). In every other case it is 0. So the marker is shown while the hero is on or within 0.1 of the ground, is hidden in the air, and stays shown in the air when the ground reference height is 0.1 or lower (for example ground at height 0 or below) or when a jump state carries mode 6, 7, 9 or 10.

Test vectors (synthetic): s1 = s2 = 0.2: state 13 (not a jump state) with any mode: gate 1, released. State 7 with mode 8: gate 1, released; with mode 9, 10, 6 or 7: gate 0, kept. s1 exactly 0.1, or s2 exactly 0.1: gate 0, kept. s1 = 0.2, s2 = 0.05: gate 0, kept. Marker-enable 0: released whatever the gate. Position: hero (10, 5, -3) with s2 = 4.0 puts the marker at (10, 4.02, -3).

### Right-stick aim mode

Confidence: High for the entry, exit, reticle rules and the retained R2/Circle selector. The hero's gameplay reads of +0x14B0 gate aim-mode start and aim-view mode during state 13. A generic selector-based accessor also copies the 64-byte block beginning at +0x14B0 when selector 8 is requested, but no combat consumer reads that block, so it has no gameplay meaning for the rewrite (D). The aim mode values go to a separate aim view object, not to the camera (SPEC-0027, "Right-stick aim mode and the camera").

The hero's aim mode word (+0x1490) and aim counter (+0x1494) run once per tick in the hero's update, for a hero with a pad. The right stick values are x = −S(byte +0x12B) and y = −S(byte +0x127) of the pad record, where S(b) is the stick conversion of SPEC-0033 (b − 127.5, a dead zone of 40, the rest shifted by 40, so −87.5 to +87.5).

- During state 13, if R2 or Circle is held, the hero stores 1 when R2 is held and 0 when Circle is held; R2 takes priority if both are held. If neither is held, the stored value is retained. Right-stick aim in attack sub-states 1, 9 and 14 requires the stored value to be 1. The value therefore retains the last R2/Circle choice made during the attack, though its other consumers are not fully characterized.
- Every tick the objects of the world list that the aim scan reads have their highlight word cleared.
- Off: the aim reticle words are set to (0, 0, 0) (hidden). Aim mode starts (counter 0) when y is not 0 and either the hero is in state 1, or the hero is in its attack step sub-state 1, 9 or 14 with the flag word +0x14B0 set. The right stick X alone never starts it.
- On: the counter goes up by 1. From the 61st tick (counter above 60) the reticle is set: with both stick values 0 it keeps its current value and is centred; else it is set to (180, trunc(x × 0.125), trunc(y × 0.125)), so the offset is −10 to +10 pixels on each axis. When the reticle value is below 1, the counter is set back to 0.
- On: aim mode ends when the hero is in neither state 1 nor state 13. Otherwise the aim-target scan (the rope-gun aim scan of SPEC-0033) runs, and its result (0, 1 or 2) is used below. The stored right-stick aim point that Roberta's shot uses comes from this scan (Roberta shot projectile, Launch).
- The reticle HUD element moves its screen position 1 pixel per tick on each axis toward its base position plus the offset, and is drawn with the reticle value (180) while that value is at least 1 (SPEC-0023).
- Aim view mode (called the camera mode value; it is not a camera mode). It is computed only while the camera mode (the camera lock word) is 0, and when the hero may steer the camera (or Roberta is in state 13 with +0x14B0 set): 4 when the scan returned 2; else 2 for Tor in aim mode; else 3 when the scan returned 1; else 1 in aim mode; else 0. Without those conditions the mode is 0. The mode, the two stick values and the aim counter go each tick to a separate aim view object, which uses only the mode (it stores the counter and ignores both stick values; SPEC-0033, Behavior 23) and would draw a small marker model of the common-object registry (id 312) in front of the camera; no resource on the disc supplies that model, so the marker is never drawn (High for the disc survey, Medium-High for the absence of any other source); the camera record is not changed (SPEC-0027, "Right-stick aim mode and the camera").

Test vectors (synthetic): Tor in state 1, right stick Y byte 0 (y = 87.5) and X byte 128: aim mode starts; on the 61st tick after the start the reticle is (180, 0, 10). Right stick Y byte 167 or 88 (inside the dead zone): no start. Tor in aim mode entering state 7 (jump): aim mode ends that tick. During state 13, held R2 sets the flag to 1; held Circle alone sets it to 0; both held sets it to 1; neither held preserves the previous value.

### Hero shot slots in enemy hit tests

- A slot is armed when its armed word is non-zero. An unarmed slot is skipped by every test.
- Point test (sphere against point): given a test point and a radius R from the caller, a slot touches when the distance between the slot position and the test point is at most R plus the slot radius. The Spyder nest's not-attacking branch uses R = 3.0 (SPEC-0017); the Black Knight's weak-point test and the Spyder nest's attacking branch use R = 5.0 (SPEC-0012, SPEC-0017). The routine returns a direction and a depth as well (the depth is R plus the slot radius minus the distance).
- Disarm on hit: a slot that touches a target in the weak-point test of SPEC-0012 and in the attacking branch of SPEC-0017 has its armed word set to 0 at once, and that test returns a hit. The Spyder nest's not-attacking branch does not disarm the slot.
- Synthetic vector: a slot with radius 0.5 whose position is 5.4 units from the test point touches under R = 5.0 (5.4 is less than 5.5) and is armed; at 5.6 it does not touch.

### Hero body cylinder in the enemy hit tests

Two different shapes stand for the hero on the enemy side (High).

1. Melee weapon volumes of the common enemies (class 17), and every other monster weapon test that uses the weapon cylinders of the monster row (SPEC-0040 Behavior 12A; SPEC-0029 Behavior 10.2): the hero's bone capsules, exactly as for any other target (Hurt shapes). Tor has nine capsules, the same in all eight outfits: eight of length 0.367 and radius 0.367 on bones 0, 1, 2, 3, 24, 25, 27 and 30, and one of length 0.823 and radius 0.431 on bone 4 (the bone-local x of its base point is -0.004 in six outfits and 0 in two); base points are bone-local and are read from the bone table. Roberta has one capsule in all eight outfits: length 1.112, radius 0.436 on bone 0, base point (0, -0.217, 0.105). The capsules are posed from the hero's bone frames as the previous tick's animation pass left them. The hero's row radius and height, and the body cylinder below, play no part in these tests. The sizes are small: against a weapon cylinder of radius 3.0 the hero adds about 0.4 of reach.
2. The weak-point tests of SPEC-0012 and the Spyder nest's attacking branch of SPEC-0017 use a different shape, a finite cylinder with radius 1.0 (a fixed value in the routine; SPEC-0018 for the test). Its length is the equipped weapon's reach plus 2.0 (3.5 with no valid weapon, whose reach counts as 1.5; SPEC-0012, Weak-point test, which has the worked values), and its centre and axis come from the hero's stored vectors (Medium-High for the exact combination). The common-enemy strike never uses it.
3. Enemy pool projectiles test the body cylinder (radius 1.175, height 3.5 from the feet, both heroes, every outfit), not the bone capsules (SPEC-0029 Behaviors 9 and 13a).

### Hit detection

Confidence: High for the gates, the hit list, the attack window and the weapon length; Medium-High for the cylinder placement; Medium for the swept-test internals and for the lane mapping of the posed frames.

**Hurt shapes.** Three kinds of target shape exist, and each attack family tests one of them:
- Bone capsules (hero swing, Roberta's shots, monster weapons and boss reach tests). Each bone whose hurt length (bone record +0x40) is above 0 is a finite cylinder with flat ends. Each tick it is posed: P = the bone's current world frame applied to the base point (+0x30); Y = the second basis row of that frame (the bone's local Y axis in world). The cylinder has radius = the hurt radius (+0x44), full height = the hurt length, axis Y and centre P + 0.5 x length x Y, so it runs from P to P + length x Y. Bones are tried in index order and the first hit ends the test.
- Body cylinder (the Mote power-attack objects against characters): a vertical finite cylinder with the character's motion-record radius (+0x08) and height (+0x0C) (SPEC-0018), axis = the constant up vector (0, 1, 0, 0) at 0x3932E0 (written at boot). The character's position point is its feet, and the cylinder runs from the feet up to the feet plus the height: its centre is position + 0.5 x height x up (High). The enemy projectile pool tests characters with the same shape (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9).
- World breakable volumes (attack sweeps and the hero's breakable walk; Attack sweeps, Contact routines and cues).

**Attack window.** At the start of each hero update, before the action-state switch, the hero's attack-active word is set from event bit 0x1 of the current animation frame (table B; Layout). The event query has no layer selector and scans four playback records: main slots 0 and 1, sub-part and face. Shipped Tor action entries select main slot 0; generic inclusion of main slot 1 does not prove a Tor upper-body clip supplies a hit window. For each playing record with frame index f (the whole part of the cursor) and per-tick frame step s, the bits of frames f, f-1, ... (s frames back) are ORed when s is above 1, otherwise frame f alone is used; when f equals the frame count and the clip loops, the value is the first frame AND the last frame; a slot past its clip gives nothing. Any slot with the bit gives true (Medium for the multi-frame rule). Hero windows at play rate 1 (frames of the main-family clips; 4 ticks per frame): Tor action 0x0F clip 38 frames 0-3; 0x17 clip 39 frames 5-16; 0x18 clip 40 frame 0; 0x10 clip 41 frames 2-9; 0x13 clip 42 frames 2-11; 0x12 clip 43 frames 2-12; 0x0E and 0x1B clip 44 frames 2-5; 0x14 clip 45 frames 3-8; 0x11 clip 55 frames 1-6; 0x0C clip 56 frames 1-5; 0x1C clip 58 frame 2; 0x1D clip 61 frame 6; 0x1E clip 62 frame 9; 0x1F clip 63 frame 9. Roberta: 0x0C clip 0 frame 3; 0x0E clip 2 frames 7-9; 0x10 clip 4 frames 0-3; 0x13 clip 7 frames 3-5; 0x14 clip 8 frames 3-4.

**Windows at other play rates (High).** The hero's play rate P (the per-tick speed scale, which multiplies the animation playback rate of the hero's animation pass) is set to 1.0 at the start of every hero update. No attack step writes it. Only the hero's status routine (Status effects), the locomotion steps and the long fall change it later in the same update. During an attack the only change is slow (status 3): its second predicate is the hero's in-attack test, which is true for Tor in state 13 at sub-states 2 to 12, for Tor in the jump states 7 to 11 while the air-attack phase is 8 or 9, and for Roberta in state 13 at sub-states 5 and 12 (the swats). In those steps a slowed hero plays at P = 0.75; every other attack step (Roberta's shots, spins, leap and full release, and Tor's hold) plays at 1.0 even when slowed. Speed (status 5) applies only while moving, never in an attack. Stun sets P to 0, but it also sets the hero idle. At P = 0.75 a main-family frame lasts 16/3 ticks instead of 4, so every window and clip length above stretches by 4/3.

Play-rate test vectors (synthetic, frame cursor = 0.25 x P x ticks since the clip start): Tor action 0x10 (clip 41, window frames 2-9) at P = 1 is active on ticks 8 to 39; slowed (P = 0.75) frame 2 starts at tick 11 (cursor 2.0625) and frame 10 at tick 54, so the window is ticks 11 to 53; the clip's length 48 becomes 64. Roberta's 0x0C shot (frame 3) is released on tick 12 whether or not she is slowed.

**Tick order of the tests (pose timing; High).** In each pass the region character loops run before the selected hero (SPEC-0007 pass order). Each character runs its update hook, then its motion-record tick, then its animation pass, which first copies the current attachment frames to the previous ones and then poses the body again. The hero's update hook holds the attack-window update, the shot slot step (before the state switch) and the melee scans inside the attack states; the Mote objects run after the hero's animation pass. Hence:
- The hero swing and the attack window read the hero's pose of the previous pass: D1 and P0 come from the last animation pass and D2 from the pass before it. The enemies' bone capsules they test against are this pass's (the enemies were posed earlier in the pass).
- Roberta's shots move and test with the enemies' bone frames of this pass; the shot's own start point is the body anchor of the previous pass.
- Enemy hits on the hero (the monster weapon cylinders of the common enemies and other enemy-kind characters, which run in the region loops) read the hero's bone capsules of the previous pass (Hero body cylinder in the enemy hit tests, shape 1). The boss weak-point tests that use the body-cylinder shape read the hero's stored vectors of the previous pass.
- The Mote objects (Charm attack hits) are placed from the hero's pose of this pass.
So a swing is one tick behind its animation: the first tick of a window tests the weapon where the clip was on the tick before.


**Hittable predicate.** A character is a candidate for the hero swing, Roberta's shots and the Mote object walks only when its hittable query passes:
- base rule: its action state is not 16, 33, 36, 53 or 54, and its word +0x7F0 is 0 (Gnarfas and the Faerie Sisters set +0x7F0 to 1 once when their controllers start and never clear it; the Undead Draik sets it once before its loop (SPEC-0029, Section 6); the Cloud Beast sets it to 1 on itself and on its companion on every tick that is not in its reaction state and never clears it (SPEC-0010); the Black Knight sets it with base state 20 on every tick and writes base state 1 only on stun ticks where its gate passes; the Ixi Chief clears it every pass; the Gelert Assassin sets it at creation and never clears it (SPEC-0013); the Plague Serpent sets it on every tick (SPEC-0016); Juppie sets it at start (SPEC-0015); the Darkest Faerie sets it as its invulnerable flag (SPEC-0054); the Werelupe King sets it only on ticks of its flinch, catch, roll, jump and hold states (SPEC-0019).);
- a hero target: also not in states 17, 25, 57, 59 or 60, not busy (SPEC-0033 Behavior 3), and not in hero mode 1 with mode code 8 or 13;
- an enemy-kind target: not hittable when its class state (+0xA34) is 16 or when any of these six tests holds; otherwise the base rule (High for the tests):
  1. its +0xC54 word is set (this is the townsfolk flag, set exactly on class-6 characters, so every townsfolk character is never a hit candidate; the class-6 routine also sets +0x7F0 to 1 at its start);
  2. its agro outer sub-state (+0xAF8) is 5 or 6 (the melee dodge or the missile dodge, SPEC-0038 Behavior 44);
  3. it is hidden by the invisible-kind rule (below);
  4. it is the second actor (the run-time second actor record), or the pet type id of its spawn record (+0xAC) has a kind in the primary petpet table (SPEC-0047);
  5. that pet type id has a kind in the second petpet table (SPEC-0047);
  6. it is not script-held and its current animation is 74, 75 or 76 (the end and slide of hit-reaction kind 7, and the flyer re-launch; SPEC-0038).
- Invisible-kind rule (test 3). Nothing is hidden while the invisibility-off word (+0xE60) is set. For a row with Display in default (row 0x82) = 0: hidden when the class state equals the spawn record's behaviour code (u16 at +0x84), or is 21 while animation 82 is not current, unless the selected hero wears the Tattered Cloak (item 126; SPEC-0038 Edge cases, SPEC-0021). For a row with Display in default non-zero: hidden while the hold word (+0xE3C) is set; otherwise, while the emerge sub-state (+0xBC0) is 0, hidden when the spawn record word +0xC4 is 0 and the current animation is the hide pick of the selector word +0xC8 (0 gives 9, 1 to 4 give 128 to 131, other values 9); while the emerge sub-state is non-zero, hidden only in class state 14 (retreat home) at phase 1 (+0xC04). The spawn record's cue word therefore also decides whether a waiting hidden state-8 enemy can be hit or drawn: with cue word 1 it is drawn and hittable while it hides and a hit wakes it with doubled damage, and a hit never wakes a cue-0 enemy that is still hidden.
- Test vectors (enemy-kind hittable query): a class-17 enemy in agro, outer sub-state 6 (missile dodge): not hittable. The same enemy in outer sub-state 1 playing a gait clip: hittable. A Display-in-default-0 enemy in class state 21 playing animation 82: hittable; in class state 21 playing any other animation, with the hero not wearing item 126: not hittable.
A guarding target is still tested by the hero swing and by shots when it is not hittable (it can block). The swing resolver, Roberta's shot walk, the Mote power-attack objects and the random target finder never select such a target; those bosses test the hero's swing and shot slots in their own hit routines (SPEC-0011, SPEC-0016, SPEC-0020).

**Hit list.** Every character keeps a hit list of ten target ids at +0x400 (Layout). A target whose id is in the attacker's list is skipped. Adding takes the first -1 slot; when all ten are used a debug message is written and slot 0 is overwritten. One target is therefore hit at most once until the list is cleared. Some boss tests have no per-swing latch; the Juppie stun-loop melee test is one (SPEC-0015).

**Hit-list clear (High).** One clear sets all ten slots to -1. It runs at: every sub-state transition of both heroes' charge routines (Combo windows); entry to hero state 13; the attack-start reset; every tick of hero state 12; character creation; the enemy attack start (the start of an attack slot of the agro routine, SPEC-0029); the heroes' air attack routines (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50); and two Werelupe King routines (SPEC-0019). The default death also clears the list (Health and death).

**Hero swing (Tor and Roberta), per tick.**
1. Inside the attack routines (both heroes' state-13 tails and four more hero routines), when the attack-active word is true, the hero visits every entry of the global hittable list in order and skips targets already in its hit list.
2. Reach test. The target must be hittable or guarding. Let A be the anchor and D1 the axis (Z row) of attachment 0 of the hero's posed body this tick (SPEC-0005 pose entry 0), and D2 the same axis from the previous tick. Gate: the full three-dimensional distance between the two character positions must be at most 65.0 (a code constant). Weapon shape: a finite cylinder of radius 1.0, full length L, centre P0 = A + 0.25 x D1, axis D1, where L = W + 2.0 and W is the hero's weapon reach (SPEC-0021 Weapon reach: 1.5 with no weapon, otherwise the per-hero table value). For each target bone capsule: static overlap (SPEC-0018 finite-cylinder test); if it misses and the tip moved (P0 + L x D1 differs from P0 + L x D2), the swept step of Combat reach test runs with that capsule against the triangle P0, P0 + L x D1, P0 + L x D2. The first hit returns true.
3. On a hit the target id is added to the hero's hit list and the hero's on-hit hook runs. Swing hit cue (High for the branch, Medium for the material call): in sub-state 10 Mote-bank cue 2 plays (Tor's power step; Roberta's sub-state-10 clip has no attack frames). Otherwise Tor plays a material impact from the target, and when nothing played, weapon-bank cue 14 plays; Roberta always plays weapon-bank cue 14.
4. Block: if the target guards and the hit is not a power hit, let c = dot(target facing, hero facing). If c is below the target's monster-row Blocking Angle (row +0x10C; SPEC-0029) the hit is blocked: no damage, and a block effect plays. A blocked swing also sets the target's blocked word; when the hero is Tor and in attack sub-state 2, 3, 4, 6, 11 or 12, his sub-state becomes 13 (the recoil). Roberta never recoils. On disc the Blocking Angle is 0.924 on 30 rows, 0.707 on 4, 0.5 on 4, 0.966 on 3, 0.991 on 1 and 0 on 217. The rule is the same for every target that reaches this step (all characters on the shared hit path, bosses included), and a guarding target never takes damage from a non-power swing. When c is not below the Blocking Angle (for example a swing from behind), the swing has no effect at all: no damage, no take-hit, no block effect, no cue, no recoil, the target is not added to the hero's hit list, the on-hit hook does not run, and the swing does not count as landed (so Tor pays no magic for it). The same swing can still be blocked on a later window tick if the facing changes, because the target stays out of the hit list. Only a blocked swing counts as landed. A "power hit" is Tor's power step (sub-state 10) with a Mote in equipment word 2; Roberta never makes one, and Tor in sub-state 10 without a Mote is an ordinary swing. A power hit skips the block test: the target takes the normal damage and take-hit when it is hittable, and nothing happens when it is guarding but not hittable (the Black Knight, the dead Black Knight). A guarding target is whatever its guard query says: base state 20, and for the common enemies of class 17 also outer sub-states 7 and 8. Monster rows with Blocking Angle 0 (most rows, and the Werelupe King's) therefore block only swings with c below 0 (target facing roughly opposite the hero's) and ignore every other non-power swing; the Black Knight sets its row's Blocking Angle to 1.0 at fight start, so it blocks every non-power swing except one with c exactly 1.0. Roberta's shots do not use the Blocking Angle at all: a guarding target is judged by the shot rules (Roberta shot projectile), where a quick shot bounces or is deflected from any angle and a full-release shot ignores the guard. Test vectors: guarding target, Blocking Angle 0.0, non-power swing with c = -0.5: blocked; c = 0.3 (swing from behind): nothing, not landed, not in the hit list; the same swing as Tor's power step with a Mote: normal damage. The Undead Draik writes base state 20 every tick (1 in its states 1, 3, 26, 27 and in stage 5) and writes 2.0 into its row's Blocking Angle at setup (SPEC-0029, Section 6), so every non-power swing that touches it while it guards is blocked.
5. Damage (Damage): the take-hit runs when the damage is above 0 or the hit was absorbed.
6. After the character scan, the hero's breakable walk runs over the list at hero +0x174 (Contact routines and cues). It runs on every tick that the character scan runs, so a swing touches breakables on each window tick; it has no hit list and no once-per-swing rule. Kinds 1, 4 and 7 test only the edges of the swing triangle (no swept volume); kind 3 uses the cylinder-pair test when the tip has not moved and the swept capsule test when it has (below). Per volume kind: kind 1 (sphere, centre +0x30, radius in the fourth lane at +0x3C) hits when the sphere comes within its radius of any of the three edges of the swing triangle (the base point P0 and the two tips); kinds 4 and 7 use the static rectangle test when the float at +0x2C is 0 or below and the moving test otherwise, against the same swing shape; kind 3 treats the volume as a finite cylinder with flat ends, radius +0x3C, full length +0x24 along the axis at +0x40, running one way from the base point at +0x30 (so its centre is the base + 0.5 x length x axis, as for the hurt shapes above), and tests it against the swing shape; when the tip has not moved (the previous-tick axis D2 equals D1, compared exactly) the weapon is a finite cylinder of radius 0.5 and full length L (the reach of step 2) with centre attachment point + 0.5 x D1 and axis D1, tested with the cylinder-pair rules of SPEC-0018 (for parallel axes: the axial offset of the centres within (L + length) / 2 and the lateral distance within 0.5 + radius, both ends included); when it has moved, the volume is tested as a capsule by the swept step of Combat reach test against the triangle P0, P0 + L x D2 and P0 + L x D1; kind 2 never hits. Rectangles (kinds 4 and 7): +0x30 is a corner and the extents are full lengths from it (SPEC-0048, Breakable volume shapes). The swing shape is the reach of step 2: base P0 = A + 0.25 x D1 and tips at the reach length along the current and previous-tick axes. The third vertex is the previous-tick tip: P0 plus the reach times the previous-tick axis (the axis taken for the second tip, not scaled by 0.25 as the base point is), so the swing triangle has corners P0, the current tip and the previous-tick tip, and the three edges are P0 to each tip and the two tips to each other (Medium-High). Test vector (synthetic): base point P0 = (0, 1, 0), current axis (1, 0, 0), previous-tick axis (0, 0, 1), reach W = 2; the tips are (2, 1, 0) and (0, 1, 2). A kind 1 sphere with radius 0.5 centred at (1, 1, 0.5) lies 0.5 from the edge from P0 to the current tip (nearest point (1, 1, 0)), so the test hits. The edge from P0 to the previous-tick tip is 1.0 away (nearest point (0, 1, 0.5)), so it alone would miss. Resolved (R-0076): point and extent roles of kinds 3, 4 and 7 (High; worked tests in SPEC-0048, Breakable volume shapes). Medium for the pair and swept internals of kind 3 and the face set of the moving rectangle test; Medium-High for the kind 1 edge test; High for the per-tick timing.

Factor A of the damage is the weapon's stat A, not W: W changes only the reach. An unarmed hero still swings with W = 1.5 but deals 0 and lands nothing.

### Combat reach test (capsule and anchor points)

Confidence: High for the hero swing's anchors, the steps, their order and the margin; Medium-High for the inputs of the other callers.

Input b (High): the hero swing passes the second basis row of the target bone's posed world frame as b, as it comes from the frame, with no normalisation. The test is therefore exact when that frame row has unit length (a frame with no scale).

Users (the swept test has seven direct callers and no indirect user): (1) the shared actor reach routine, used by the hero swing (Hit detection, step 2) and by the incoming-hit routines of several bosses, among them the Ixi Chief, Juppie and Gnarfas, where the weapon is always the hero's and the boss is the target (SPEC-0029 Behavior 10.2); the fixed enemy-kind weapon length of 1.5 is only the routine's answer for a non-hero attacker, which no caller supplies in play, and the enemy attack volumes are the monster weapon cylinders of SPEC-0040 Behavior 12A, not this routine; (2) the Black Knight weak-point tests and the Spyder nest valve test, which sweep the hero's weapon against a fixed cylinder of the caller (SPEC-0012, SPEC-0017); (3) the Werelupe King's own copy (SPEC-0019); (4) a variant of the shared routine with an attacking gate, which nothing calls; (5) the test of an enemy weapon against the hero's body (Hero body cylinder in the enemy hit tests); (6) the bush cut test and (7) the reed cut test of SPEC-0060. The only callers that pass a capsule radius of 0.5 are (6) and (7): a vertical capsule of radius 0.5 and length 6.0 over the plant, tested against the hero weapon's triangle after the static cylinder test misses. The other callers take the radius and the length from +0x44 and +0x40 of a bone record, the hurt radius and hurt length of the target's bone capsule (Layout: Hurt shapes and hit list).

Anchors (hero swing): A = P0 (the weapon base point of Hit detection, step 2), B = P0 + L x D1 (the tip this tick) and C = P0 + L x D2 (the tip last tick). The swept test runs only when B differs from C. The capsule is the posed bone capsule: centre p = bone base + 0.5 x length x Y, axis b = Y, radius r = the hurt radius, length h = the hurt length (full length, flat ends).

Steps:
1. n = unit((C - A) x (B - A)); c = |n . b|; s = sqrt(|1 - c^2|); dist = n . (p - A).
2. Plane slab: L = r s + 0.5 h c. If |dist| > L: miss.
3. Margin d = 0.4 (r c + h s). Shifted point (quirk): q = p - b x (|dist| / L); when c is exactly 0 and dist > 0, q = p + b x (dist / L). The shift is the ratio |dist| / L along b, not a length.
4. Inside test: if q, projected on the triangle's plane, lies inside the triangle A, B, C: hit.
5. Edge gates, for each edge V_i to V_(i+1) (AB, BC, CA), with unit direction e, length l and in-plane outward normal m: miss if m . (q - V_i) > d; miss unless -d < e . (p - V_i) < l + d (this test uses p, not q).
6. Edge cylinder test: hit when any of the three edge segments meets the finite cylinder (centre p, axis b, radius r, length h, flat ends; the finite-cylinder test of SPEC-0018); otherwise miss.

The test returns hit or miss only. The sign of n matters only when c = 0. The slab and the margin use |dist| and c = |n . b|, but the shift then follows the sign of dist (above 0, the shift is + b x (dist / L); otherwise - b x (|dist| / L)). The c = 0 test vector below therefore holds only for n = unit((C - A) x (B - A)), the order used in step 1; with the opposite order the hit point is shifted outside the triangle and the test misses (High, by the vector arithmetic). The sweep's one side test only turns the three edge normals outward and does not flip n (High; SPEC-0018, Open questions, R-0014, has the vectors for both corner orders). The inside test and the outward normals come from the triangle itself. When c = 0 the slab limit is r; when c = 1 it is 0.5 h. For a capsule whose axis lies along the plane normal (c = 1), d = 0.4 r, which is smaller than r, so a capsule that overlaps an edge from outside by more than 0.4 r fails the first edge gate and is missed (quirk; reproduce).

Test vectors (A (0, 0, 0), B (2, 0, 0), C (0, 0, 2)):
- b = (0, 1, 0), r = 0.3, h = 1 (L = 0.5, d = 0.12): p (0.5, 0.49, 0.5) hit; (0.5, 0.51, 0.5) miss; (-0.1, 0, 1) hit; (-0.2, 0, 1) miss (the cylinder overlaps edge CA, but the edge gate fails).
- b = (1, 0, 0), r = 0.3, h = 1 (c = 0, L = 0.3): p (0.5, 0.29, 0.5) hit (the c = 0 branch of the shift); (0.5, 0.31, 0.5) miss.
- b = (0.6, 0.8, 0), r = 0.5, h = 1 (L = 0.7, d = 0.4): p (0.5, 0.35, 0.5) hit; (-0.3, 0.35, 1) miss; (-0.3, -0.35, 1) miss; (2.3, 0.35, 0.2) hit.

### Charm attack hits

Confidence: High for the damage rule, the stored scale, the spawn lists and the routine identity; Medium-High for the volumes; Medium for the family-to-Mote pairing of the volumes; Medium-High for the status applies.

These are the hits of the Mote power-attack objects. They belong to the hero's Mote power-attack and charm object set, which is built once at start-up, 22 objects in all (Attack family driver). Five of the Mote objects are plain effect objects: Tor's weapon effects for 160, 164, 166, 168 and 170, which deal no hits. A full-press release spawns the object of the Mote in word 2: Tor's power attack for any of the 14 Mote ids, Roberta's full release for ids 161, 162, 164, 165, 166, 169, 171 and 173 (Power attack and full release). The object is placed along the hero's facing using the weapon reach (position + facing x reach, with no step toward the camera; High, see Tor Mote object placement, item 6).

At spawn the object stores a scale: the hero's outgoing product A x D x B x G x E (Damage), with D taken at the release sub-state (Tor 10, so D = 1.0; Roberta 9, so D = 1.5). With no hero the scale is 1.0. On each hit of a target:

    x = scale * C(id)
    z = x - Defence ;  if x > 0 and z <= 0: absorb
    z = max(z, 0)
    damage = truncate(z * P(hero row, id))        (no minimum)

- C(id) = 1 + G x 0.01 of the object's own Mote id when Match(element of that id, target type) holds (Mote elements and type match), else 1. It does not read word 2.
- P(hero row, id) is the attack power table value for the hero row and the object's own id (Layout).
- The target's take-hit receives the damage, attacker id -1 and the flag values 0, 3 and 1 (High for the Tor 161 and Roberta 169 objects; the other objects differ in their flags and reactions, Medium).
- Some objects also apply a status to the target through the target's status-apply method: the Lava (161) objects of both heroes, Roberta's Leaf (164), Tor's Shadow (169) and Sun (171), and the Water bubble (163). Their request fields are listed under Status apply (Sources of statuses on an enemy from the heroes). An enemy whose element matches the status ignores it (SPEC-0029). The other objects start an effect at the target (effect kind 2 or 3). Roberta's Leaf object deals no damage and only applies its status (below).
- No magic and no Mote charge is paid at a hit: both are paid at release (Mote charge use by attacks).

Volumes. Each object runs once per tick from the attack family driver: first its character walk, then its breakable walk (Attack sweeps). In the character walk, every global hittable entry that passes the hittable query (Hit detection) is tested against the object volume and the target's body cylinder (Hit detection, hurt shapes); on a pass the object's hit runs. The beam and bubble objects first add the target to their ten-slot list and skip targets already there (Beam and bubble lists). The Nova and Wind volumes in the table below test only targets that pass the hittable query, so a target whose invulnerable word +0x7F0 is set is never hit by them; the Plague Serpent, Gnarfas and the Faerie Sisters are such targets (Medium-High; SPEC-0016).

| Object (filter kind; Mote ids) | Attack volume | Size source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sun/Light (3; 170, 171) | sphere | radius = object word +0x400, centre +0x410 |
| Shadow/Smoke (6; 168, 169) | sphere | radius +0x54, centre +0x100 (High; set at spawn and rewritten each tick from the attached effect) |
| Nova (unfiltered; 172, 173) | vertical cylinder | radius 4.0 (word 0x2DCBB0), height 2.0 (code constant), centre +0x80 |
| Wind/Fog (5; 166, 167) | vertical cylinder | radius 4.0 (word 0x2DCBB8), height 5.5 (word 0x2DCBBC), centre +0x80 |
| Water/Bubble (4; 162, 163) | cylinder along the beam | radius 1.0 (word 0x2DCB3C), height 5.0 (word 0x2DCB40), centre +0xC0, axis = unit(+0xA0) |
| Rock/Leaf (2; Roberta 165) | sphere (Rock skipping shot) | radius = the rock's effect scale (1.0 when there is none; it shrinks by 0.12 per bounce); centre = rock position; the first hit ends the walk |
| Family A (kind from owner +0x100; Roberta 161, Tor 163, Tor 172) | vertical cylinder | radius sub-object (+0xA0) word +0xB0, height 4.0, centre = the object's attach point (+0x40 for Tor Water 163, +0x120 for Tor Nova 172, +0x3F0 for the Tor Rock sub-shots) |
For the character hit test, this cylinder is checked against the target body cylinder, not the target bone capsules. Both axes are world-up. The hit volumes overlap when the horizontal centre distance is no greater than the sum of their radii and their vertical intervals touch or overlap. For a Family A centre at yA with its 4.0-unit height, and a target feet point yT with body height hT, the vertical intervals are [yA - 2.0, yA + 2.0] and [yT, yT + hT]. Boundary contact counts as a hit. Family A stores a particle from a paired effect emitter. The particle's +0xB0 size supplies the collision radius; Family A compares its age at +0x130 plus dt against its +0x14C word. This is the Family-A cutoff comparison; the generic particle updater also uses +0x14C as the denominator for curve progression, so this spec does not assign it a universal lifespan meaning. The emitter initializes these particle fields, then its birth callback stores the particle in the object's single association slot; each particle born by a hooked emitter overwrites it. Each object binds one effect definition of its own at set-up (the numbers 415, 416, 560 and 553 are effect definition ids, not archive resource numbers), so the stored particle is the last one born by that definition's first-listed emitter. Each object's hit radius follows its own particle's size, and the object ends when that particle's life runs out: Roberta Lava 161 about 0.8 s, radius 0.5 rising to 25; Tor Water 163 0.7 s, radius 0.5 rising to 15; Tor Nova 172 1.2 s, radius 0 rising to 18; each Tor Rock 165 sub-shot 0.6 s, radius 0 rising to 20 (values and test vectors under Family A users and the Mote object placement item).
| Family B (kind from owner +0x148; Tor 161, Roberta 173) | sphere orbiting the hero | radius +0x34 (2.5, a program constant), centre +0x10 |
| Family C (kind from owner first word; Roberta 169, 171) | horizontal beam | 20 units long from the hero's query point; 2.0 units wide (fixed collision-box width) |

Object pairing (High). The spawn dispatch gives each (hero, Mote) pair one object; the volumes above keep their sizes:

| Mote | Tor power attack | Roberta full release |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 160 Fire | weapon effect only (no hits) | no object (Fire shot) |
| 161 Lava | family B orbit, fire status | family A object |
| 162 Bubble | effect only, stopped when sub-state 10 ends (no hits) | Water/Bubble beam (filter 4) |
| 163 Water | family A object (bubble, stun) | no object (Water shot) |
| 164 Leaf | weapon effect only | expanding ring, poison only, no damage |
| 165 Rock | three family A sub-shots (filter 2), 16 ticks apart (word 0x2DCB94) | three Rock skipping shots (filter 2) |
| 166 Fog | weapon effect only | drifting fog cloud |
| 167 Wind | Wind/Fog cylinder (filter 5) | no object (Wind shots) |
| 168 Smoke | weapon effect only | no object (Smoke shots) |
| 169 Shadow | Shadow/Smoke sphere (filter 6) | family C beam |
| 170 Light | weapon effect only | no object (Light shots) |
| 171 Sun | Sun/Light sphere (filter 3) | family C beam |
| 172 Nova | family A object | no object (Nova shots) |
| 173 Supernova | Nova cylinder (unfiltered) | family B orbit |

- Tor's weapon effects for 160, 164, 166, 168 and 170, and the 162 effect, are stopped whenever Tor leaves sub-state 10. With these six Motes Tor's power attack spawns no hitting object: its damage comes only from the sub-state-10 melee swings, with P from the attack power table (Damage).
- A new power attack cannot start while any Mote object is active (Power-ready check).
- **Family B (orbit; Tor 161, Roberta 173):** a point circles the hero's query point at radius 2.5 (0x2DCB68 for Tor, 0x2DCBAC for Roberta) at 2π radians per second (one turn per second; a program constant), for 10 s (0x2DCB64 / 0x2DCBA8). Tor's orbit (161): the centre is the hero anchor raised by 2.0 (run-time offset word; High). Its angle starts at 0 at the first activation of the Mote set (an object constructor sets it once) and is not reset by a spawn, so each later orbit continues from the angle the previous one ended at (Medium-High). The angle is measured on the world axes, not from the hero's facing. The orbiting point is the centre plus 2.5 times (cos θ, 0, −sin θ): a horizontal circle with no height term. Test vectors: θ = 0 gives offset (2.5, 0, 0); θ = π/2 gives (0, 0, −2.5); θ = π gives (−2.5, 0, 0). Hit shape: a sphere of radius 2.5 against the target's body cylinder. Each hit target is listed for 60 ticks, so a target in contact is hit once per second. Damage per hit is the rule above. Tor's 161 orbit also sends fire (status 7) with D, A and T of (Tor, 161); Roberta's 173 orbit sends no status; it also fires a bolt at a nearby enemy every half second (Hit volume re-arm).
- **Family C (beam; Roberta 169, 171):** a horizontal beam from the hero's query point, 20 units along the hero's direction (a program constant). Its collision box has a fixed transverse half-width of 1.0, so the total width is 2.0 units; the collision reader does not take this width from effect definitions 0x222 or 0x224. The other box extent and its center and axis come from the live segment/effect object. Each beam lasts 5 s (0x2DCB98 / 0x2DCB9C). Each hittable target whose body cylinder meets the beam is hit and listed for 1 s (0x2DCB2C × 60 = 60 ticks), after which it can be hit again. The element passed to the damage is 6 (Shadow) or 3 (Sun). These lists are the 1 s lists of Ten-slot timed lists.
- **Family C has no volley (Roberta 169, 171; High).** The Shadow and Sun objects are the beams above and fire no shots of their own. The count each object holds is its life in ticks: a start sets it to trunc(5.0 × 60) = 300, each tick lowers it by 1, and the beam ends when it falls below 1, so the beam is tested on 299 ticks. Each live tick the beam is rebuilt from the hero's query point to 20.0 units along the normalized aim, then the object walks every hittable target and then the breakables (SPEC-0040 Behavior 17). A target whose body cylinder meets the beam is hit and entered in the object's ten-slot hit list for 60 ticks; while it is listed it cannot be hit again, and when all ten slots are taken no new target is hit. A target that stays in the beam is hit on beam ticks 1, 61, 121, 181 and 241. Both objects live in the hero's effect object group, so they use no hero shot slot, and they are not the hero projectile volley that ids 169 and 171 skip (Power attack and full release).
- **Family A (Roberta 161, Tor 163, Tor 165, Tor 172):** the vertical cylinder above. The fourth user is Tor's Rock (165): its three sub-shots are family A objects with filter kind 2 (High). The volumes and lives per object are Medium (Open questions).
  - Synthetic overlap vectors (radius values chosen for the example): Family A radius 2, centre (0, 0, 0), height 4; target body radius 1, feet at (3, 0, 0), height 2: hit at horizontal tangency. Moving the target to (3.01, 0, 0) misses. With horizontal centres coincident, target feet at y = 2 and height 2 hits at cap tangency; feet at y = 2.01 misses.
- **Tor Rock 165 (High for the timing and the damage; High for the sub-shot motion):** the Rock object stores the release point and a countdown of trunc(60 × 0.0) = 0 ticks (word 0x2DCB90). Each tick while it is live: when the countdown is 0 and fewer than 3 sub-shots have started, the next sub-shot starts at the stored point with an empty ten-slot list, and the countdown is set to trunc(60 × 0.25) = 15 (word 0x2DCB94) unless that was the third; a positive countdown goes down by 1. So the sub-shots start on the Rock object's ticks 1, 17 and 33. Each sub-shot hit deals trunc(F × P(Tor, 165)), where F is the charm rule's value for the object's stored scale (10.0 until a spawn writes it), with take-hit flags (attacker −1, 0, 2, 0), and starts effect kind 2 at the target. Each sub-shot's position is set to the stored release point when it starts; its family-A tick does not advance that position, so it remains stationary while active. Its active state depends on an associated-particle link: the shared update clears the link when that particle's age at +0x130 plus dt is equal to or greater than its +0x14C word, after which the sub-shot is inactive. This states the Family-A comparison without assigning a universal semantic name to +0x14C. Resolved (High for the particle, life and radius curve; Medium-High for the tick counts): each sub-shot binds definition 553 and its linked particle is that definition's single particle, with life 0.6 s (36 ticks, so the sub-shots end about 36 ticks after their starts); its radius is 20 x age / 0.6 (a straight line from 0, with age in seconds, 1/60 s per tick), so it grows by 0.5556 per tick to 20.0 at the end. Each sub-shot keeps its own empty ten-slot hit list and a listed target stays listed for 60 ticks, longer than the sub-shot lives, so a target is hit at most once per sub-shot and at most three times by one Rock attack (once by each sub-shot whose volume meets it while it is alive). Test vectors: age 18 ticks (0.3 s): radius 10.0; age 30 ticks: radius 16.67; a body whose nearest point is 12 units from the release point (same height band) is first met at age 22 ticks (radius 12.22); the second sub-shot starts 16 ticks after the first, so at that moment the first has radius 8.89. The Rock object stays live while its countdown is 0 or more or any sub-shot is live, and plays effect mode 4 at its point when it ends.
- **Roberta Leaf 164:** a vertical cylinder around the spawn point whose radius starts at 1.0 (0x2DCB70) and grows by 1.0 unit per second (0x2DCB78) up to 5.0 (0x2DCB74), with half-height 1.5 (0.5 × the word 0x2DCB80), for 10 s (0x2DCB7C). Each target is hit once per object (a ten-slot list with no expiry). Its damage is trunc(word 0x2DCB84 × word 0x2DCB6C) and is dealt only when 0x2DCB84 is above 0; on the disc 0x2DCB84 is 0.0, so the hit deals no damage and calls no take-hit. It only sends poison (status 2) with D, A and T of (Roberta, 164).
- **Roberta Fog 166:** the cloud effect is the Aimed timed object (cycle 6 s, height = ground + 3.5). Its launch direction is zeroed during setup, so it does not drift horizontally on the documented spawn/tick path (Behavior below; High). Each pulse tests the hittable targets within 10 units (squared radius 100.0, run-time word 0x378B44) and sends each one the message of Attack spawn (the first routine); the look-up arguments 0xA6 and 0xAD of that message are the Mote ids 166 and 173, used for the element and the attack power table (Medium-High).

Test vectors (synthetic): a target standing in Tor's 161 orbit for 3 s is hit 3 times. A target inside Roberta's 169 beam for 5 s is hit 5 times. At any fixed position along the beam, its collision box spans transverse offsets -1.0 to +1.0, for total width 2.0; this width is independent of effect definitions 0x222 and 0x224. Roberta's Leaf ring 2.5 s after spawn has radius 3.5; after 4 s and later its radius is 5.0; a target inside it is poisoned once and takes no damage. Tor's power attack with Mote 164: no object hit; only the sub-state-10 swings deal damage.

Ids 162, 169 and 171 for Roberta: the full release spawns the object and fires no projectile volley, so all damage of these releases comes from the object's hits (Power attack and full release).

### Hit volume re-arm

Confidence: High for the owner, the timer, the test and the cue; Medium for what the bolt effect does on arrival (Attack spawn).

The owner is Roberta's Supernova (173) full-release object, the family B orbit (Charm attack hits). Besides its orbit hits, it fires a bolt at a nearby enemy every half second. Each tick while the object is live, after the orbit step:

- When the timer is above zero, it goes down by 1, and nothing else happens this tick.
- When the timer is zero, the bolt test runs. It passes when there is an active hero, a target is found and the effect host exists. The target is a uniform random choice among up to 10 characters of the global hittable list that pass their own hittable check, are less than 45.0 units from the hero, measured as in the shot finder (word 0x2DCBD0), and are visible along three parallel rays from the hero position + (0, 1.5, 0) to the character's aim point (the centre ray and rays offset 1.0 to each side); it is the finder of the Roberta shot modes 2 to 5 with 45 in place of 60.
- On a pass, the bolt is posted (the second Attack spawn routine): from the orbit point toward the target's aim point, duration trunc(d ÷ 2.0 + 10.0) with d the distance between the two, damage value trunc(F × P(Roberta row, 173)) with F the object's stored scale taken through the charm scale rule for Mote 173, effect type 23 with sub-type 7, a scale word of 1.0 (word 0x2DCBCC) and the definitions 1113, 1510 and 983 (three slots) of Attack spawn. The timer is then set to trunc(0.5 × 60) = 30 ticks (word 0x2DCBC8), and cue 1 of sound bank 1 plays at the orbit point. This cue is the "effect id 1" of older drafts.
- On a fail (no target in reach or sight), the timer stays at zero and the test runs again next tick.

So the first bolt leaves on the first tick with a target in reach, and later bolts leave at most every 31 ticks (30 ticks of countdown, then the test tick).

Test vectors (synthetic): a target 7.0 units from the orbit point gives a bolt duration of trunc(7.0 ÷ 2.0 + 10.0) = 13. With a target always in reach from the object's first tick, bolts leave on ticks 1, 32, 63, ... of the object's life: 20 bolts in its 10 s (600 ticks). With no target within 45 units, no bolt and no cue.

### Hit volume records

Confidence: High for the shot-slot index, cleanup path, shot-fill filter gate and child-entry gate. The records' geometry and any collision consumer remain open.

The hero record has six auxiliary records starting at +0x600, with a stride of 0x1C0 bytes, parallel to its six shot slots (Hero shot slots). When a shot build selects free slot k, it passes k to the auxiliary-record builder only if the shot-fill filter returns nonzero. The filter returns zero for every attack id when the hero word at +0x14C is zero. When that word is nonzero, it returns zero for attack ids 161, 162, 164, 165, 166, 169, 171 or 173 if the attack sub-state word +0xF90 equals 9; it returns 1 for every other input with +0x14C nonzero. On a permitted build, the slot-matched auxiliary record is initialized. The child-entry gate is whether the selected child effect id is not -1; a separate companion effect-definition lookup supplies its result when present and uses id 0x193 when it is absent. Child creation does not depend on that companion lookup succeeding. A shot update also has a path that clears the record with the same slot index when the shot is no longer active.

A character-update path also walks all six auxiliary records as child-state containers. For each record, it checks the matching shot-slot state; when that source is available, it updates active child entries using state and transform data from the corresponding shot slot and issues child start/update operations. When the source is unavailable, it clears the child entries. This establishes a non-collision use of the records in the shot-associated child update path. The known call paths still do not establish that Hit detection or Hit volume re-arm reads these records; their geometric content and any collision or re-arm use remain open.

### Beam and bubble lists

The object-hit callbacks of Roberta's family C beams (Shadow 169 and Sun 171) and of her Bubble beam (162) each keep a per-object list of ten target ids (see Layout). The beams own the first list (target id at object +0x7C, remaining ticks at +0x80, slot stride 8, used-slot count at +0x70); the Bubble beam owns the second (same layout at +0xD8, +0xDC, count at +0xD4). Adding a target:

1. If any slot already holds that target id, nothing is added and the callback skips the hit.
2. Otherwise the first free slot (id -1) takes the target id and an initial 60-tick lifetime in its parallel field. The lookup checks the id only; a separate per-object update decrements the lifetime once each active tick and clears the entry at zero. Thus a hit target can be hit again after about 1 second even while the attack object remains active (High for lookup, insertion and lifetime; Medium-High for mapping ids to attack names).
3. If no slot is free, nothing is added. A debug message is written; nothing is shown to the player.

The list starts empty when the attack object is initialized. The parallel field is a remaining-ticks counter, not a timestamp. The beam objects end after 5 seconds; their list entries expire independently after 60 active updates. The Bubble beam hit is the damage of Mote 162 at filter 4, one hit of that damage per listed target per 60 updates.

### Taking a hit (hero as target)

1. If the input lock word (game-state record +0x20) is set, the hit is ignored: no cue, no damage and no reaction (High). This word is the game-state box flag, set on each pass by the dialogue-box host and the list picker (SPEC-0007), so every hit on a hero is discarded while a dialogue box or a list picker is up.
2. The attacker is not added to the hero's hit list. The attacker hook that the take-hit routine calls does nothing in this build, so a hit leaves the hit list unchanged (Medium-High).
3. The hit cue and the hit effect always play, including on a hero in state 16. The hit is heavy when the hero's maximum health times 0.05 is less than the damage (the 0.05 is a code constant, compared in single precision), and light otherwise. Light plays sound event 19 and heavy plays sound event 20. The branch word is the hero variant word (+0xF80): Tor plays 19 and 20, Roberta plays 43 (light) and 44 (heavy) (High). The hit effect is one of the hero's two hit effects (bank +0x198, effects 0 and 1), each with chance 1/2 (one draw from the shared random stream). A separate sound cue is selected from the shared hit bank by the hit-kind argument: kinds 2 and 3 select cue 171 for either hero; other kinds select no cue. For kinds 0 and 1, Roberta selects cue 170; Tor selects cue 170 when its equipment-list-5 item id for the stored attacker is 0, cue 187 for kind 0 or 189 for kind 1 when that id is 1, and cue 186 for kind 0 or 188 for kind 1 for every other result, including no matching item. This is distinct from the threshold-selected light/heavy stinger and the random hero hit effect (High). The Black Knight's kick, slow and angry swings, charge impact, enraged high swing, floor strike and flying sword hit pass kind 0 (SPEC-0012). Cloud Beast shock, air-flow, lightning and bouncing-bolt paths pass kind 2, but that encounter is cut (SPEC-0010). Additional exact-kind callers are identified: the hero poison tick passes kind 3 with attacker id -2 and blocked flag 1; the Faerie Sisters projectile-list updates can pass kind 3 to the active hero; and a hero-attached companion state machine passes kind 1 for a one-point hit with attacker id -1 and blocked flag 0. The companion behavior is visible, but its character identity and gameplay label are not established. These are producer mappings, not additional cue rules. Other callers remain unclassified.
4. A hit on a hero in state 16 (the death state) then returns at once: no damage, no reaction and no stun clear. The hero stays in state 16. When the sound service is available, the hit-effect choice and its one shared random draw have already occurred before this return; a repeated hit on a dead hero therefore advances the shared random stream even though it does not damage the hero.
5. Reaction, for a hero not in state 16. The take-hit receives (value, attacker id, blocked flag). The attacker id is stored on the hero. The hero is busy when it is in the attack step at Tor's sub-state 1 or 10, or Roberta's sub-state 1 or 9 (charging, or the power attack or full release), and its equipment word 2 is not 0; an empty word holds −1, which also counts, so in practice these four sub-states are busy (Medium-High). With blocked flag 0 and the hero not busy, the hero is written to state 17 (the hit-reaction state, number 0x11; this is a state, not an animation) and plays request 0x20 when the first entry of the hero's weapon list (equipment list 1) exists and is not empty (a positive count and a non-zero item), else request 0xC0 (the unarmed flinch) (High). With blocked flag 1 the hero does not enter state 17; unless busy, it plays its block reaction instead: shield-bank cue 0 (bank +0x1A0), then request 64 (0x40) (High). The block reaction takes a float argument that it never reads (Medium-High). A busy hero is never interrupted: with either flag value nothing is written and nothing is played (High).
6. Damage is applied after the reaction, by the rule in Health and death: the value is subtracted from health unchanged, and the death step runs when health reaches 0. This holds for both values of the blocked flag; a blocked hit still costs its full value (example: a blocked Gnarfas whip still takes 15). The take-hit itself applies no shield block, armour, armour Mote, petpet 0.9 factor or attacker status scale (High). A lethal unblocked hit has therefore already written state 17 when the death handler runs. Hero defence is zero. The damage has already been reduced by the hero's shield block and armour only for the common enemies (class 17) and for enemy pool projectiles (Enemy attack damage to a hero); boss melee hits arrive unreduced.
   - Callers that want a block to cost nothing pass a value of 0 with the blocked flag set (the Ixi Chieftain's forward swipe, the Werelupe King's shared hit), or skip the take-hit entirely on a block (the Gelert Assassin, the Black Knight's two swings, the Plague Serpent's lunge).
7. After damage, if the hero is in state 25, the hero is written to state 1. This is the only state-1 write in the take-hit routine (High). The take-hit routine never writes state 16; the death handler does (Health and death).

When a hit takes effect inside the tick (High): the call does all of its work at once and nothing is queued. Health, the hurt or death state and the cue change on the tick of the call; the flinch clip starts in the hero's next animation pass. For each damage route (enemy melee, boss attacks, the projectile pool, ground rules, status ticks, level-script damage) the tick item, the first flinch frame and the lack of any invulnerability window are in SPEC-0007, When a hit on a hero takes effect.

Order of the whole routine (High): the box-flag test (step 1); a let-go when the hero is in the rope-gun state 27; the attacker step (step 2); the hit cue and effect (step 3; they play only when a sound emitter is available); the state-16 return (step 4); a hit flash when the damage is not 0; the reaction (step 5: state 17 with 0xC0 or 0x20, the block reaction, or the stop call); the weapon draw (skipped for attacker id -2 and when the party weapon-ready word is already 1; otherwise the first weapon-list entry, when present and not empty, sets that word to 1 with its cue, and the same step then sets the shield-ready word and starts the cloak removal of SPEC-0021; High for the skip rules, Medium-High for the shield word); the stun clear (status 0 is queried and cleared, and the status display is reset when it was active); the deduct (step 6); the state-25 write (step 7); a HUD refresh. A hit on a dead hero therefore returns before the stun is cleared.

A hit whose attacker id is -2 (poison and other environmental or status damage) does not change the weapon or the menu state. Other attacker ids do.

A guarding hero facing the attacker blocks with a shield, and armour reduces the hits of the common enemies and of enemy pool projectiles (Enemy attack damage to a hero). Boss melee hits are not reduced; how each boss treats a block is set by the boss (step 6). No parry or dodge is known for either hero, and there is no invulnerability window (High): the take-hit sets no timer and tests no hurt state, so repeated hits from one attacker are limited only by that attacker's hit list, which is cleared at each attack start (Hit detection, Hit-list clear). The one effect of the hurt state on enemies: while the hero is not hittable (Hit detection, Hittable predicate; hurt state 17, the knockback state 48 and the other busy states), common enemies pick no new attack and their attack cooldowns stop counting (SPEC-0038, Behavior 7), but an attack already picked still lands, and the counter-attack choice, bosses and pool shots are not affected (SPEC-0007, Hit reaction and invulnerability). No blink or alpha flicker exists; a hit with damage above 0 only starts the 14-frame reddish tint of SPEC-0046 (Medium-High). A hit (a poison tick too) on a hero not in state 16 ends an active stun (the stun timer is cleared) and stops the status effect visuals.

### Enemy attack damage to a hero

Confidence: High for the shared enemy class; Medium for the second attacker routine; High that boss melee hits do not use this rule.

This rule applies to the common enemies (the shared enemy class 17, which includes the class-17 minions and the spiders). Enemy pool projectiles (boss projectiles included) have their own hero-hit rule, which also applies shield and armour (SPEC-0029). Boss melee hits do not use it: each boss melee attack passes a fixed value or a boss tuning word straight to the take-hit, which subtracts it unchanged (Taking a hit).

When an attacker of the shared enemy class hits a hero, the damage is:

    d0 = the attack's damage value
    b  = shield block (0 unless the hit is blocked)
    d1 = Armour(d0 - b)
    y  = max(0, d1 * G)
    damage = truncate(y)                  (no minimum)

All products are single-precision with truncation toward zero (Damage).

- d0: the Damage Value of the attack in the attacker's monster row (row +0x34C + 4 x attack index; SPEC-0029). For a fixed special attack it is the value that the attack's own spec names.
- G: the attacker's status scale. For an attacker of the shared enemy class (class 17) it is the enemy's own scale: 0.5 under curse, 1.5 under strength, 0.75 with both, 1.0 otherwise (High; R-0034). A hero's scale (0.75 under curse, 1.5 under strength, 1.125 with both) is used for hero attacks only. The hero's own statuses do not change the damage it takes.
- Blocked: the hero guards and the attacker's own facing test passes. The hero guards when it is in action state 20, or Roberta is in attack sub-state 6 or 7, or the hero's guard timer (+0x1574) is above 0. In the attacker's hit routine, the facing test passes when dot(hero facing, attacker facing) is at most -0.5 (facing each other); attackers that pass a blocked flag of 0 are never blocked (SPEC-0029).
- Shield block b, when blocked: with no shield-family entry, b = 0. With a shield and word 1 empty, b = the shield's stat A. With a shield and an item in word 1 (the shield Mote), b = 1.25 x the shield's stat A; and when the attack's element is 0, equals that item's element kind, or is 7, word-1 charge is used (SPEC-0021 Mote charge: amount trunc(1 x (s x 0.01)) with a minimum of 1, where s is the shield's stat B for type 5, stat D for type 3, else 0) and 2 MP (odd Mote) or 1 MP (even Mote) is paid. When the element is nonzero, differs from the item's element kind and the item's kind is not 7, b = 1.25 x stat A with no charge and no magic payment (High; R-0034).
- Shields per hero (High): an equipment add puts types 4, 5 and 6 in Tor's entry of the family and types 1, 2 and 3 in Roberta's. Tor's shields are the type-5 shields 106 to 110; Roberta's are the type-2 books 176 to 180. So s is the type-5 stat B for Tor and 0 for Roberta (her charge amount is always the minimum, 1). Test vector: Roberta with book 178 (stat A 40), a blocked hit of 60, word 1 empty: damage 20; word 1 = Mote 161 with an attack element of 0: damage 10, 1 charge and 2 MP.
- Armour(d), when the hero has a body-family item:
  1. If word 0 holds a Mote (the armour Mote) whose power Q0 (6 odd, 4 even) is at most its remaining charge, the hero's MP is at least its cost (4 odd, 2 even), and the element rule of the shield block holds: d = (d x 0.01) x (100 - H), where H is that Mote's stat H (75 odd, 50 even). Then word-0 charge is used (amount trunc(Q0 x (body stat B x 0.01)), no minimum) and the cost is paid.
  2. Then d = d x (1 - A_body x 0.01), where A_body is the body item's stat A (a percent; 0 to 70 on disc).
- Petpet factor, always: d = d x 0.9 (one minus the word at 0x2DD8E0) when the active petpet's kind is 1 or 7 (SPEC-0047).
- The hero's class defence stays 0 and is not used in this intake.
- The result goes to the hero's take-hit (Taking a hit) with the attacker id and the blocked flag, and is applied to health by Health and death.
- Poison tick damage is not an enemy hit; it has its own 1 HP floor (Status effects).

### Guard timer and per-attacker block

Confidence: High.

- Guard timer (hero +0x1574): set to 40 ticks by the threat sense and reduced by 1 every tick in the hero update. The sense runs only in action state 1 (idle), and only with weapons drawn and a shield equipped. A threat is a live enemy projectile in front of the hero, or an attacking character (its attack-active query is true) within 10 units horizontally in front; "in front" means dot(unit horizontal vector from the hero to the threat, hero facing) > 0.5. The Darkest Faerie's four private projectile slots are not part of the enemy projectile pool, so her orbs and bolts never raise this timer (SPEC-0054).
