Confidence: High for the launch gate, the release frames, the launch direction, the homing steer, the rows, the block, bounce and deflect rules, the range rule, the ground-test threshold and ordering, the ground-surface cue lookup, the power clamp, the per-hit damage rule and the payment; Medium for the first tick of a release frame and the bounce frame quirk. Open points are under Open questions.

A Roberta shot is one object in one of the hero's six shot slots (Hero shot slots, which gives the slot layout).

Launch gate (High). Each tick in state 13, Roberta's arm routine calls the launcher. It fires when (a) the shots of this sub-state have not been fired yet, (b) the hero has at least 1 MP, and (c) the current animation frame carries event bit 0x20 (the release frame). Word 2 may be empty. After the burst the fired flag is set. Roberta's charge routine clears the flag at every sub-state change (Combo windows), so each sub-state fires at most once, and a full-release sub-state does not fire again while the hero stays in it. The launcher checks these gates before building any shot. Each shot build also requires a free shot slot (a slot whose armed word is 0); with none free, nothing is built. A quick shot (launch class 0, 1 or 2) pays nothing at launch and nothing while it is built: the build only stores the shot's MP cost and Mote charge amount (the power Q of the Magic table, taken from the Mote in word 2 and the launch class at that moment) in the slot, and both are paid when the shot hits a hittable target (Quick-shot payment). A quick shot that never hits costs nothing. Only a full-release volley pays at launch: the first shot of the volley pays its MP cost and its Mote charge amount (the rounded product of Mote charge use by attacks) when that shot is built, whether or not it later hits; the later shots of the same volley pay nothing when built or when they hit, and a full-release shot never pays at a hit. If the first shot finds no free slot, nothing is paid for the volley. The only MP requirement of a launch is the gate's 1 MP; MP is never compared with the cost, so a hero holding 1 MP fires, and a cost at or above the current MP sets MP to 0 (Magic) while the Mote charge amount is still used in full. Test vectors (synthetic): a full-release sub-state with a free slot and enough MP but no release event creates no shot and spends no MP or Mote charge; with the event present but all six slots occupied it also creates no shot and spends nothing; with the event present, enough MP and a free slot, a volley shot is built and the existing launch-payment rule applies even if that shot later misses. Quick shot, word 2 Mote 161 (cost 3, power 4), MP 50: launching leaves MP 50 and the charge unchanged; if the shot hits a hittable target for any damage (0 included) MP becomes 47 and the Mote loses 4 charge; if it misses, MP stays 50. Full release, Mote 161, MP 150: the first shot leaves MP 110 and charge 60 at launch; the later shots of the volley and every hit change nothing. Full release with Mote 161 and MP 1: the launch is allowed, MP becomes 0 and the charge falls by the full amount.

Release frames (High for the bits and the first tick). The release frame is the frame with event bit 0x20 of the clip that the arm table requests (main family, 4 ticks per frame at play rate 1; frame f covers ticks 4f to 4f + 3 after the clip start). The launcher reads the bit with the same four-record frame query as the attack window (Hit detection), so on the tick j after the request it reads frame floor(j / 4): the shot is built on tick j = 4f exactly. It is built inside the state-13 step, after that tick's shot-slot step has already run, so the new shot is first moved and tested on tick 4f + 1 (Hero battle timing table):
- Sub-state 2, action 0x0C (clip 0, 10 frames): frame 3 (about tick 12). Sub-state 3, 0x0D (clip 1, 15 frames): frame 4. Sub-state 4, 0x0E (clip 2, 17 frames): frame 7. Sub-state 10, 0x0F (clip 3, 6 frames): frame 2. Sub-state 11, 0x13 (clip 7, 6 frames): frame 3.
- Sub-states 5, 6, 7, 8 and 12 request clips with no 0x20 bit, so they never launch (5 and 12 are melee windows only). Their rows in table A are never used.
- Full release (sub-state 9), by the Roberta ids of Attack animation choice: 161 and 164: clip 12, frame 6; 167 and 173: clip 13, frame 8; 168, 170 and 172: clip 11, frame 4; every other id and no Mote: clip 10, frame 5. The group 162, 169 and 171 plays clip 20 (frame 0) in sub-state 14 with the fired flag already set, so it fires no volley.

Launch class. The hero's sub-state word (Charge completion) gives the class: 0 for sub-states 5, 6, 7 and 10; 1 for 2, 3, 8, 11 and 12; 2 for 4; 3 for 9 and 14 (full release); 4 for every other value. Classes 0 to 2 are quick shots and use table A. Class 3 is a full release and uses table B, keyed by the attack id. The launch class is also the level of the Magic table: class 0 is level 0, class 1 and the default row (class 4) are level 1, class 2 is level 2, and a full release (with a listed Mote, an unlisted Mote or no Mote) is level 3.

Table A, quick shots. Each launch makes one shot. No quick row carries a reaction (status request -1).

| Class | Sub-state values | Speed (units per second) | Radius (units) | Life (ticks) | Homing mode |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 5, 6, 7 | 60 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 |
| 0 | 10 | 30 | 1.0 | 120 | 1 |
| 1 | 2, 3, 8, 11, 12 | 60 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 |
| 2 | 4 | 90 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 |
| 4 (default row) | all other values | 60 | 3.0 | 120 | 0 (straight) |

The default row's homing test can be true only at sub-state 10, which has its own row, so the default row always flies straight (High).

Table B, full release. Keyed by the attack id, which is the Mote in slot 2.

| Attack id (Mote) | Speed (units per second) | Radius (units) | Life (ticks) | Homing mode | Shots | Reaction field |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 160 (Fire) | 75 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 | 1 | 7 |
| 161 (Lava) | 75 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 | 1 | none |
| 163 (Water) | 20 | 1.0 | 480 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| 167 (Wind) | 20 | 1.0 | 180 | 2 | 2 | 3 |
| 168 (Smoke) | 20 | 1.0 | 180 | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| 170 (Light) | 20 | 1.0 | 180 | 4 | 3 | 6 |
| 172 (Nova) | 20 | 1.0 | 180 | 5 | 6 | none |
| Other ids, not 162, 169 or 171 (the default row) | 60 | 3.0 | 120 | 0 | 1 | none |
| No Mote in word 2 (the class-2 row) | 90 | 1.0 | 180 | 0 | 1 | none |

The speed, radius, life, homing and reaction values are the shot rows of Where it lives (0x2DD6D0 to 0x2DD7A8). Life is given in ticks here, and the tick is 1/60 s (SPEC-0007). A full release with no Mote falls back to the class-2 row and still counts as a full-release shot (the full-release flag comes from the sub-state, 9 or 14): its cost, 10 MP (word 0x2DD484), is paid at launch, it uses no charge, and T = 1.0.

Launch, per shot (High; repeated for each shot of the burst):
1. Target, by the homing mode m of the row:
   - m = 0 or 1: the hero's current auto-target (the first entry of the hero's 15-entry auto-target list) when its valid flag is set; else none.
   - m = 2 to 5: scan the global character list in its stored order. Store the first ten characters whose actor eligibility method (+0xCC) passes, whose horizontal distance is less than 60 units, and whose aim point is visible along three parallel rays from the hero position + (0, 1.5, 0) (the centre ray and two rays offset 1.0 to each side). Once ten are stored, later qualifying characters do not replace them. Choose uniformly from the stored entries; return none when no character qualifies. For region characters, the eligibility method is the generic hittable predicate in Hit detection. This finder does not call the active-timed-record test used by projectile collision and does not require a positive-length hurt capsule, so a selected target can fail the separate collision prerequisite. The mode number 2 to 5 selects only this finder; modes 2, 3, 4 and 5 behave the same.
2. Aim point: the target's target-point method result when a target was found; for ordinary non-hero actors the shared default is render attachment point 7, unless a controller supplies an explicit override (SPEC-0040 Behavior 20). The Darkest Faerie appears to use the shared default; no override is known in its controller family. Else, when the hero is in right-stick aim mode, use the stored right-stick aim point (aim mode with m = 0 always uses it and drops the target); otherwise use hero position + 20 × facing + (0, 2, 0). A one-shot script override, when set, replaces the aim point with the position of a world locator (Where it lives, shot aim override).
3. Direction d = unit(aim point - body anchor). The shot starts at the body anchor.
4. Modes 2 to 5 only (fan): r1 = U(-1, 1) and s = unit(d × up), d1 = r1 s + (1 - |r1|) d; r2 = U(-1, 1), d2 = r2 up + (1 - |r2|) d1. d2 is not renormalised (its length is 0.6 to 1.0), so a fanned shot that is not steered flies slower than its row speed.
5. Slot frame: side +0x00 = unit(d × up), up +0x10 = unit(side × d), direction +0x20 = d (w = 0), position +0x30 = the anchor (w = 1). Up is the constant up vector (0, 1, 0, 0).

Launch test vectors (hero at the origin facing +Z, anchor (0, 1.5, 0.5)):
- Mode 0, no target: aim point (0, 2, 20), d = (0, 0.02563, 0.99967).
- Mode 0, auto-target aim point (10, 1.5, 0.5): d = (1, 0, 0).
- Mode 2, no target, r1 = r2 = 0.5: d = (-0.25, 0.50641, 0.24992), length 0.6176. r1 = r2 = -0.5: (0.25, -0.49359, 0.24992). r1 = r2 = 0: unchanged.

Status request (High for the words; Medium-High for the hit side). A full-release shot record carries a status request: (reaction field, D(Roberta, id), A, T), with D, A and T from the Mote status words (Layout). The duration is read for Motes 160, 163, 167, 168 and 170, and is -1 for every other id. Amount and period are read for Mote 160 only, and are -1 otherwise. A shot hit sends the stored request to the target's status-apply method (Status apply), and the shot ends. For a full-release hit with a nonempty reaction field, target damage runs before this request. The shared enemy status path ignores application when the target is already dead or dying, so a lethal hit suppresses this later request for that path (SPEC-0029); this does not establish the same behavior for other target classes or boss-specific hit paths.

Per tick, while the shot is alive:
1. Its arming counter drops by 1 when it is above 0, then its life counter drops by 1. When the life counter is then below 1 the shot is disarmed at once and does not move or test anything on that tick (High).
2. Homing runs (below); then the position moves by d x speed x dt, with dt = 1/60 s.

Arming counter (High): slot +0x7C, signed 32-bit; launch initializes it to 0. A bounce or deflect sets it to 15 ticks. On each live shot update, a positive counter is decremented before movement and character collision; guard handling and homing run only if it is 0 after that decrement. Thus a counter of 1 becomes 0 and permits both on that update, while 2 becomes 1 and still suppresses them. Test vector: counter 1 at update start allows homing and guard handling after the decrement; counter 2 does not.

Range (High). A shot with life L is built on its release tick and moves on the next L - 1 ticks; on the L-th tick after its build its life reaches 0 and it is disarmed without moving. With no hit it therefore covers speed x (L - 1) / 60 units: 179 units for a table A shot at 60 units per second with 180 ticks (not 180), 268.5 for the class-2 row, 59.5 for the rapid shot (30, 120), 119 for the default row (60, 120), 159.67 for Water (id 163, 20, 480) and 59.67 for the other 20-unit full-release rows; a fanned shot (modes 2 to 5) covers less, by its direction length.

Homing (High). Mode 0 never steers. In modes 1 to 5, each tick, when the shot's target (chosen at launch) exists, is alive, and the arming counter is 0: d = unit(0.75 d + 0.25 unit(target aim point - position)), the slot frame is rebuilt from the new d, and then the shot moves. Otherwise the shot moves along the stored d. All five modes steer the same way; modes 2 to 5 differ from mode 1 only in the target finder of the launch.

Homing test vector: shot at the origin, d = (1, 0, 0), speed 60, target aim point (0, 0, 10). After tick 1: d = (0.948683, 0, 0.316228) and position (0.948683, 0, 0.316228). Tick 2: d = (0.816441, 0, 0.57743). Tick 3: d = (0.639741, 0, 0.76859). With the arming counter at 5: no steer, a straight move.

Charge power (Medium-High). The launch takes the held power P = clamp(F / 120, 0.1, 1.0), where F is the held tick count, and stores it in the shot. The clamped value is copied into the shot power field, but no reader in the shot motion, life, hit or damage path uses it. Therefore P does not change speed, life, radius, shot count or damage; it affects only the stored field and the shot draw routine, which is gated by a slot word that nothing sets (Hero shot slots, +0x40 and +0x64; High for the motion, life, hit and damage paths, Medium-High for the draw; Stored charge power and Roberta shot: stored power reader).

Damage scale (High). The launch first writes max(20, 40P) to the shot's scale field, and then the same routine overwrites that field with the damage scale S. The value that remains is S, so max(20, 40P) never takes effect. S = A x D x B x G x E, multiplied in that order with the platform arithmetic of Damage, where:
- A is the stat A of the hero's weapon-family entry (SPEC-0021), 0 with no weapon;
- D is Roberta's step factor for the sub-state at launch (Damage: 2.0 at 4; 0.5 at 5 and 12; 1.5 at 9; 0.33 at 10; 1.0 otherwise);
- B = 1 + F x 0.01 of the Mote in word 2, using that Mote's stat F (Mote elements and type match); B = 1 with no Mote;
- G is the hero's status scale (the hero class method at offset 0x13C; curse 0.75, strength 1.5);
- E = 1.25 when the active petpet's kind is 0 or 7, otherwise 1.0.
The per-shot scale T is 1.0 for a quick shot. For a full-release shot, T is the attack power table value P(Roberta row, the Mote in word 2) (Layout).

Element. At launch the shot stores the element kind of the Mote in word 2 (kind 1 to 7; 0 with no Mote), from Mote elements and type match. Weapons store nothing: only the Mote sets the element.

Hit on characters (one per shot; Medium-High). For each entry in the global character list, collision proceeds when the actor eligibility method passes, or when the actor guards and the shot arming counter is 0. It then requires the target's animation object (actor +0x170) to have at least one skeleton bone record whose hurt-capsule length at +0x40 is positive. This early check scans the same bone records used by the later hit test; it is not a status-timer check. The collision next rejects a target that the invisible-kind predicate marks hidden (Hit detection, Hittable predicate, test 3), then tests each positive-length bone capsule using its length (+0x40) and radius (+0x44) against the shot sphere (centre slot +0x30, radius slot +0x48; Hit detection, hurt shapes; cylinder against sphere), stopping at the first hit. The random target finder does not use this positive-capsule prerequisite; its first-ten selection and list-order rule are stated in Launch, per shot.
- Quick shot meets a guarding target (High). With the arming counter above 0 the shot passes through: no effect, no damage, no disarm. Otherwise, when the target's deflect flag (character +0xE5C) is 0, the shot **bounces**: d and the side axis are negated; r1 = U(0, 0.5) with a random sign (fair coin), d1 = r1 side + (1 - |r1|) d; r2 is drawn the same way, d2 = r2 up + (1 - |r2|) d1 (not renormalised, so the speed falls to 61 to 100 percent); the arming counter becomes 15, so for 15 ticks the shot ignores guards and does not steer. When the deflect flag is 1, the shot is **deflected**: d = unit(current hero's aim point - shot position), the arming counter becomes 15, and the shot turns hostile: once the counter is 0 it steers toward the current hero with the homing rule above, and it tests the hero instead of enemies. No damage is dealt to the guarding target either way.
- Deflect flag: the Black Knight's and the Gelert Assassin's deflect (guard) stance routines set it (SPEC-0012, SPEC-0013), and so does the Black Knight's parry state, not only its stance strike (SPEC-0012). Only character creation clears it, so after one use of the stance it stays set for the rest of the fight (reproduce).
- Hostile shot against the hero: the shot is tested against the hero's body cylinder (motion radius and length, axis up). A guarding hero bounces it again (it stays hostile). Otherwise the hero's take-hit receives trunc(S x C x T) (hero defence 0), with the shooter's id as the attacker and blocked flag 0; a shot with a reaction field also sends its status request; the shot is disarmed. Such a hit also pays the quick-shot cost below (quirk; reproduce).
- Full-release shot meets a guarding target: no block. A target that is also hittable takes the damage below; otherwise the shot is disarmed with no damage.
- Bounce frame quirk (Medium): after a bounce the stored side and up axes are rebuilt from the wrong operands (up equals side, or is zero when r = 0), so a second bounce uses a degenerate up axis. Reproduce it when exact paths matter.
- Bounce test vectors: d = (1, 0, 0), side (0, 0, 1), up (0, 1, 0). r1 = r2 = +0.3: d = (-0.49, 0.3, -0.21), length 0.6117, arming counter 15. Both signs negative with 0.5: (-0.25, -0.5, 0.25). r = 0: (-1, 0, 0).
- Otherwise: C = 1 + G x 0.01 when the target's type matches the stored element kind (Mote elements and type match), otherwise C = 1. G is the stat G of the Mote in word 2 at the moment of the hit, not at launch. Kind 0 never matches, and kind 7 matches every type.
- Damage = truncate(max(0, S x C x T minus the target's defence d)), with no minimum. If S x C x T is above 0 and the result before the clamp is 0 or below, the absorb effect plays and the hit still counts. The take-hit runs when the damage is above 0 or the hit was absorbed.
- The shot is then disarmed, so it hits once.
- Quick-shot payment (High): when a quick shot is counted against a target by the shared shot-damage routine, the MP cost (Magic, at the shot's launch class) and the word-2 Mote charge (exactly Q: 2 for a lesser Mote, 4 for a greater one, 0 with no Mote; Mote charge use by attacks) are paid at once, before the take-hit decision, whatever the damage (0 included). The generic shot walk asks the routine only for hittable targets; the boss-own hit routines of the Faerie Sisters, the Gelert Assassin, the Ixi Chief and Juppie also ask it for their counted touches, so those touches pay although the boss is not hittable. The Black Knight, Plague Serpent, Darkest Faerie and Undead Draik remove the shot slot themselves and never pay; Gnarfas clears the slot first, so his query returns 0 and pays nothing. A no-Mote quick shot therefore pays 3, 5 or 10 MP per hit and no charge. A guarding target that is not hittable (bounce or deflect) and a miss pay nothing. A shot that hits Gnarfas goes through his own check (SPEC-0020), which clears the slot before asking for the damage: the damage is 0, the flinch still starts, and nothing is paid. A full-release shot pays nothing at a hit (its cost and charge were paid at release or by the first shot).
- **Last-charge quick-shot hit (High):** the hit's damage and element-match bonus are calculated before its quick-shot MP and Mote-charge deductions. Thus, if that charge use removes the last charge and clears the Mote from the hero's equipment, it does not change the already-calculated damage on that hit. The shot keeps the element kind saved at launch, but a later hit computes the matching stat-G bonus from the then-current word-2 item; with the Mote cleared, that bonus is 0. Do not apply this ordering to full-release shots, which do not pay on hit.

Ground test and ordering (High). On each live update the shot first applies its motion and then probes the current world at the shot x/z, with a probe ceiling of shot y + 3.0. The ground sampler chooses the highest accepted patch at or below that ceiling; if it samples candidates but accepts none, its fallback is the last rejected sample, which can lie above the ceiling (SPEC-0018, Ground probe). If no candidate is sampled, it returns the miss value −1.0e9 and no entry. The shot ends only when its centre y is strictly below the returned height; equality does not end it. This ground test runs before character collision and world contact. On this end path, a returned entry supplies its surface code for the ground-cue lookup; with no entry, no ground cue is selected. A ground-ended shot is disarmed and its cleanup and any resulting cue are processed before the tick continues to the next shot slot. The probe offset is a ceiling, not a fixed distance to the floor. Whether a last-rejected above-ceiling sample occurs during normal play depends on the level geometry a shot crosses and needs a runtime check (Default (rewrite choice): follow the rule as written). Test vectors (synthetic): shot centre y = 4.0, sampled height 4.0 -> continue; y = 3.99, sampled height 4.0 -> end; no sampled candidate returns −1.0e9, so a shot at y = 0 continues.

World contact (High for the order, class bits, disarm, and W-lane dataflow; Medium for the inside of each shape test). Each tick after the move, the shot tests the world-grid cell at its x and z (SPEC-0028). The shot's shape is its sphere: centre slot +0x30, radius slot +0x48 (the row radius, 1.0 or 3.0). The point has W=1. A survey of shipped world resources 532–1433 found that all 6,518 unique first-list kind-4/7 records carry at least one nonzero W among the vectors their box helpers consume. In the second list, 237,497 helper-eligible records were found; 237,454 carry a nonzero W among the three vectors read by the surface helper, while 43 have all three W lanes zero. The helper reductions use these raw four-lane record vectors; no homogeneous transform cancels or resets W before the projection. For a concrete kind-4 vector from resource 678, let the synthetic query point equal the record's stored point `P≈(-858.3405, 48.9700, 1051.1857, 1)`, and use its +0x40 vector `A≈(0.3542007, 0, 0.9351695, -679.012)`. The helper's four-lane dot is approximately `-0.000008`, while an XYZ-only dot is approximately `679.012`. The four-lane value is the signed distance from the record's plane, because the plane's offset is stored in the fourth lane of its normal vector: a point on the plane (W = 1) gives about 0, while the XYZ-only value 679.012 is only the plane offset, not a distance. A displacement carries W = 0, so it picks up no offset. Rule for a rewrite (Resolved, High): every rectangle and surface-record projection is n . p + d for a point and n . v for a displacement; an XYZ-only version is not a variant of the original and must not be used. Test vector: plane normal (0, 1, 0) with offset -5 in the fourth lane: the point (0, 5, 0, 1) gives 0, the point (0, 7, 0, 1) gives 2, and the displacement (0, 1, 0, 0) gives 1.
- Collider shapes in the first list, by kind byte (+0x10): kind 1 is tested as a sphere (centre +0x30, radius +0x3C); kind 2 by the cone test; kind 3 by the static overlap of SPEC-0018 (Finite cylinder tests), where the first shape is a solid round cylinder and the second acts as a flat card (the collider cylinder: radius +0x3C, length +0x24, axis +0x40, base +0x30); kinds 4 and 7 as a box, with a static box test swept along this tick's motion d x speed x dt when +0x2C is 0 or below, and the moving-box test otherwise. Other kinds are skipped.
- Class bits (the collider's +0x12 word, SPEC-0018). A collider with its trigger bit set (class 3 for kinds 1, 2 and 3; class 8 for a static box; class 5 for a moving box) is a trigger: on contact it runs that collider's world trigger with Roberta as the entity (SPEC-0030) and counts as no contact, so the shot flies on with no cue. Otherwise a collider with class 0 (solid) set is tested and can make a contact. A collider with neither bit is ignored.
- First collider list: the first solid contact disarms the shot (armed word 0), ends the walk of this list, and chooses a cue by the collider's surface byte (+0x11): 2 gives cue 8; 4, 8, 11, 16 and 18 give 7; 5 gives 15; 6 gives 14; 7 gives 13; 9 and 14 give 25; 12 and 13 give 20; 15 gives 19; 17 gives 26; 1, 3, 10 and any other value give 6. The cue is placed at the collider's +0x30.
- Second list: the cell's surface records (the same records the camera eye pass walks; SPEC-0018), in groups, are tested next. Records whose byte +0x4C is 6 are skipped. Each other record is tested against a small sphere of radius 0.25 (a code constant) at the shot position, swept along this tick's motion d x speed x dt. A contact disarms the shot as well and chooses the cue by the record byte +0x4D instead: 1, 13, 14, 15 and 17 give cue 15; 2 gives 13; 3 and 4 give 8; 5 gives 10; 7 gives 12; 8 and 11 give 14; 9, 18 and 19 give 7; 10 gives 11; 0, 6, 12, 16 and any other value give 6. The last match wins.
- The chosen cue plays once from the shooter's weapon bank (+0x19C).
World contact test vectors (synthetic): a shot whose sphere touches a kind-3 collider with flags 0x0001 (class 0 only) and surface byte 2 is disarmed and plays cue 8. The same contact with flags 0x0008 (class 3 only) runs the collider's trigger, keeps the shot armed and plays no cue. A shot whose small sphere meets a surface record with byte +0x4C = 1 and byte +0x4D = 5 is disarmed and plays cue 10; with byte +0x4C = 6 nothing happens.


Hit on world breakables (High). When the shot's shape meets a breakable, Roberta's shot route of Contact routines and cues runs: script hook (with the argument words (0, 1)), element gate (the entry's element byte is 0, equals the shot's element kind, or is 7), break with a 0.25 piece push, and always cue 144. The shot is disarmed at the shape hit either way (Medium-High).

Spawn point. The shot starts at the body anchor, which is attach point 0 of the hero's posed body: the translation of that attach point's frame, as written by the render update of the current pass (High; Launch, step 3). It is not a fixed offset from the hero position. Attach point 0 is bone 19 with its stored offset for both heroes (SPEC-0004), so the anchor moves with the hand and the stance. The launch vectors above use (0, 1.5, 0.5) only as a stand-in anchor.

Visual record and effect ids (Medium-High; R-0120). Each slot owns one effect record in the effect host, six records of 0x1C0 bytes starting at host offset 0x600 (record k for slot k). The launch starts the slot's effect with the shot's Mote id and launch class, and the record follows the shot position on every tick. This effect-host record is the shot's independent flight visual path; it does not require a projectile model or the attack-clip step selector described in SPEC-0034. The effect id comes from a boot table:
- full-release shot with a Mote in word 2: a 14-entry table keyed by the Mote id (ids 160 to 173); a full-release shot with no Mote: the table of the slot index;
- quick shot: a table of the slot index, chosen by the launch class (one table each for classes 0, 1 and 2; class 3 starts no effect).
A companion 14-entry table keyed by the Mote id supplies a second value for quick shots with a Mote. The selected ids resolve to effect definitions in resource 9 (SPEC-0034). Table values are disc data that the rewrite reads from the user's files; they are not listed in this spec. The separate attack-clip step selector remains invalid for shipped Roberta clips, so the other table-backed flight and impact effects remain absent (SPEC-0034; R-0114). Independent Mote attack effects can also appear during full-release attacks, but they are not the slot-following shot visual.

Slot word +0x64 is read by a second shot draw path that would show a visual with that id. No writer of that word was found, so this path draws nothing (Medium).

### Attack spawn (two routines, same shape)

Confidence: Medium-High for the duration formula and the data words; Medium for the message fields; High for the two senders (the Fog cloud and the Supernova orbit).


- Inputs: the attacker's aim point (its vector field at 0xA0), and the target object that the caller passes in. The target's aim point is taken from the target's class routine (the vector at +0x294 of its class table).
- Distance d: the length of the vector from the attacker's aim point to the target's aim point.
- The message is posted to an object found through a run-time lookup (the effect host), not to the target.
- Duration: D = d ÷ k + c, with k = 2.0 and c = 10.0 for both routines (the words listed under Where it lives). D is converted to a whole number by truncation toward zero and stored as the pooled object's lifetime in ticks. At the start of an update, a negative counter releases the object; otherwise it steps and the counter drops by one. A nonnegative starting value D therefore yields D + 1 active updates at counter values D through 0 (High; SPEC-0029, Behavior 9).
- Scale: the product of two run-time scale values (one from a scale look-up on the attacker, one from an item look-up), truncated to a whole number. The look-up arguments, 0xA6 in the first routine and 0xAD in the second, are the Mote ids 166 (Fog) and 173 (Supernova); they select the element and the attack power table record (Medium-High).
- Use: the first routine is the hit of Roberta's Fog cloud. Each pulse of the cloud sends this message to every hittable target within 10 units (Charm attack hits, Roberta Fog 166). The second routine is the bolt of Roberta's Supernova (173) orbit, posted at most once per 31 ticks toward one random target within 45 units (Hit volume re-arm; High).
- Effect message: 160 bytes, effect type 23, sub-type 5 in the first routine and 7 in the second. It carries the id field at 0x14C of the object passed in, the attacker's aim point, the target's aim point, the whole-number duration and the scale. The message is posted to the effect host.
- Sub-type (Resolved, High): the sub-type is the element number of the attack, the same element kind as the Mote element table (5 for Fog 166, 7 for Supernova 173). The pooled object stores it and compares it with the element of the Mote a hero carries in the same way as the elements of the common enemies' own missiles, which pass the element number of their monster row in the same field. Type 23 is not one of the pool kinds that replace the first and fifth definition slots, so the message's own definitions stand.
- Definitions shown (Resolved, High): the message carries six definition slots of the shared effect table (SPEC-0034); a slot of -1 starts nothing. Fog 166 (first routine): slot 1 is definition 450, which does not exist in the table, so it shows nothing; slot 2 -1; slots 3, 5 and 6 are definition 982 (three emitters of single bursts, 3 particles each, lives 0.9, 0.7 and 1.0 s); slot 4 -1. Supernova 173 bolt (second routine): slot 1 definition 1113 (five emitters, the big flash with a light), slot 2 definition 1510 (two emitters, life 0.6 s), slots 3, 5 and 6 definition 983 (three emitters of single bursts: 5, 20 and 3 particles, lives 0.9, 0.3 plus or minus 0.7 and 0.7 s), slot 4 -1. So a Fog pulse puts three copies of the 982 burst on each target and a Supernova bolt plays 1113, 1510 and three copies of 983 along its path. The duration of the object is the lifetime of the message, D + 1 updates (above).

### Three-spoke spawner

Confidence: High for the geometry, the count and the speed spread.

Use (High): this is the spawn of Roberta's Rock (165) full release (Charm attack hits, Object pairing). It does nothing while any of its three rocks is still live.

- It creates the three rocks of Rock skipping shot, objects 0x180 bytes apart, all from the same start point (the release point).
- Rock 0 uses the given direction p unchanged (blend fraction 0, no draw).
- Rock 1 uses a blend fraction f drawn uniformly from +0.2 to +0.25; rock 2 uses f drawn from −0.25 to −0.2. Each rock's direction is (1 − f) × p + f × n, with n = unit(p × U) as in the bounce; the result has its height part removed and is renormalised to unit length.
- Each rock's speed is 0.6 + u units per tick, with u uniform from −0.3 to +0.3 (the words 0x2DCB88 and 0x2DCB8C; the lower bound is the negation of the spread), so 0.3 to 0.9 units per tick (18 to 54 units per second).
- Draw order per rock: the blend fraction (rocks 1 and 2 only), then the speed, then the setup's contact limit.
- Each rock then runs the setup with its speed, the start point and its direction.

The two side rocks are not mirror images: rock 1 turns 14.0 to 18.4 degrees toward n and rock 2 turns 9.5 to 11.3 degrees away from n (the angles of 0.2/0.8 to 0.25/0.75, and of 0.2/1.2 to 0.25/1.25).

### Aimed timed object (spawn and tick)

Confidence: High for the control flow and the zero motion field on the Fog path; Medium-High for the ground-follow interpolation.

Use (Medium-High): this object is the cloud effect of Roberta's Fog (166) full release. Each countdown pulse (step 3 of the tick) tests the hittable targets within 10 units and sends each one the Attack spawn message (Charm attack hits, Roberta Fog 166).

Spawn (given a motion direction and a rate):
1. Cycle counter: 6.0 × the time base (run-time word 0x368FA0, 60 ticks per second) as a whole number of ticks, so 360 ticks at 60 per second.
2. Countdown: trunc((0.5 + u) × the time base), with u uniform from −0.1 to +0.1, one single-precision multiply truncated toward zero. The spread bounds are the word at 0x2DCB50 and its negation, so the second bound is +0.1 (High). The countdown runs from 24 to 35 ticks, each value with chance about 1/12: at u = +0.1 the sum 0.59999996 times 60 is 35.999996, which truncates to 35.
3. Ground reference: the height found by a ground probe 8.0 units above the object's point (the common foot probe offset, SPEC-0018). If that probe result is far from the object's own height (difference of 1e8 or more), the object's own height is used.
4. Motion field: the input direction is normalised and stored with its rate, then the vector field is overwritten with zero before the spawn routine returns. The tick multiplies this zero vector by the rate before adding it to position, so this path contributes no translation. Height-follow movement remains as described in tick step 1.
5. Start effect in mode 5 at the object.

Tick:
1. While the cycle counter is above 0: subtract 1. Apply the stored motion vector times its rate (1/60 s); for the Fog spawn this vector is zero, so X and Z do not change. Probe ground at the resulting point, using the same fallback as the spawn. Form the movement target's Y as trunc(ground height + 3.5) (word 0x2DCB60), then interpolate toward that target as part of the movement step. The cached ground reference remains the full floating-point probe result. This differs from spawn, which places the object at the full floating-point ground height plus 3.5.
2. When the cycle counter reaches 0: clear the cycle and countdown, release up to four attached effect objects and clear their stored handles, then play effect mode 4 at the position.
3. Countdown: when the cycle counter is 0 or below and the countdown is below 1, scan the global actor list. An actor qualifies when its class-specific eligibility query is nonzero and its position is within the configured 10-unit radius of the timed object's position (strict squared-distance comparison). For every qualifying actor, submit a target-specific attack-effect request through the effect host; the scan does not stop after the first match. This request uses the first Attack spawn routine (type 23, sub-type 5) and the Fog attack id 166. The test passes if at least one actor qualifies. On a pass, reset the countdown as in step 2 of the spawn and play effect mode 1. This request creates a visual effect-pool object; its active update runs effect and attachment upkeep without a target contact or damage step. It does not itself apply damage. The effect's visible appearance remains open, and any separate damage source is not established. If the countdown is 1 or more, reduce it by 1.

### Oscillator

Confidence: Medium-Low for the phase rule; High for the sine and cosine (Resolved, proactive (open close-out)): they are the shared folded series of SPEC-0034 section 12 (the same five stored constants, with the phase reduced by flooring), which matches the ideal sine and cosine to about 5e-8, so the clean side may use exact sin and cos.

- A phase advances each tick by a rate times the time step (1/60 s, run-time word 0x368FA8). If the result is above 6.283186, subtract 6.283186 once; if it is below -6.283186, add 6.283186 once. This is a single correction per tick, not a general modulo reduction.
- An offset equal to amplitude × sine(phase) (and the matching cosine term for the second axis) is added to the object's base position each tick.
- A time counter advances by the time step and is compared with a limit. Whenever it is at or above the limit, the stored callback is invoked. The oscillator routine itself has no one-shot latch or reset; whether later callback behavior prevents repeat calls is caller-specific and remains open. The known direct callers are two effect-object paths, but their object identities and callback effects are not established.

### Anchor sockets, nearest object and follow

Confidence: Low to Medium-Low.

- Up to four anchor transforms are taken from the rows of the object's matrix (rows at offsets 0x298 to 0x2CC of the other object) and stored at owner offsets 0x220 to 0x22C, with ids at 0x260. The owner's id is at 0x14C.
- The nearest-object pick returns the object with the smallest distance from the owner to a transformed point.
- Follow: the 0.5 blend is in the shared per-frame hero rendering/interaction path. That path invokes it only for the active hero and only when the hero's attach-mode flag is 1; it blends the selected target position halfway toward the current point before updating the effect transform. The blend is therefore pinned to this presentation/interaction path, not to hero locomotion or an attack displacement. The stored target id selects/reset its effect state when it changes.
- Aim command: a distance is computed for the command, and the target position is stored at 0x120. A random draw then decides whether a second command (code 7, with three values) is also sent. The draw is a uniform whole number from {0, 1}, so the second command is sent with chance 1/2.

### Mode-code and flag multipliers

Confidence: High for the values and the meanings.

- Table A is Tor's step factor D (Damage), keyed by the attack sub-state at hero +0xF90: 1.0 by default and for sub-states 0, 1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 9, 10 and 11; 2.0 for 4 and 12; 0.75 for 5 and 6. The power step (sub-state 10) is 1.0.
- Table B is Roberta's step factor D, keyed by the sub-state passed in: 1.0 by default and for 2, 3, 6, 7, 8 and 11; 2.0 for 4; 0.5 for 5 and 12; 1.5 for 9; 0.33 for 10 (the single-precision value of about 0.33).
- Flag factor: the hero's status scale G (Damage): start at 1.0, multiply by 0.75 when curse (status 1) is active and by 1.5 when strength (status 4) is active. The enemy's copy of this scale uses 0.5 in place of 0.75 (SPEC-0029); the hero damage path uses the 0.75 copy (High).

## Hero growth

Neither hero has an experience count or a level counter. Maximum health and maximum magic are the only growth values, and they rise only when a stat-up item is added to the party. No experience or level value is stored or derived (Medium-High: no writer for either is known).

**Boot data words.** The growth values are twelve signed 32-bit words in the boot program's data. Read them at runtime; do not copy them.

| Value | Tor address | Tor | Roberta address | Roberta |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Start health | 0x319BC0 | 150 | 0x319BC4 | 120 |
| Start magic | 0x319BC8 | 90 | 0x319BCC | 150 |
| Health step per stat-up | 0x319BD0 | 25 | 0x319BD4 | 20 |
| Magic step per stat-up | 0x319BD8 | 15 | 0x319BDC | 25 |
| Health cap | 0x319BE0 | 500 | 0x319BE4 | 400 |
| Magic cap | 0x319BE8 | 300 | 0x319BEC | 500 |

Confidence: High for every value.

**Caps.** The health and magic caps are applied when a save is loaded (SPEC-0025, Hero maxima and restore). The stat-up add path does not check them.

**Stat-up items.** When an item is added to the party (the add path, not a drop), three flags are tested. A flag passes when the item's type is 0, or 7 or higher except 17, and the stat named by the flag is at least 1. The stats are the A, B and C fields of the item's record in the item config table (archive resource 529; SPEC-0021).

1. Stat A flag: for each hero, the maximum health rises by that hero's health step, and current health is set to the new maximum.
2. Stat C flag: for each hero, the maximum magic rises by that hero's magic step, and current magic is set to the new maximum.
3. Stat B flag: luck rises by stat B times the item count, then is clamped to 0 through 24.

Each flag applies once per add, whatever the count; only the luck gain uses the count. The flag effects reach both heroes, not only the one who received the item. A successful stat-up plays sound cue 0x9F. Confidence: High for the flag tests and the boot words; Medium-High for the effects on both heroes and the refill; High for the luck meaning (SPEC-0021 pickup gate); Medium for the cue.

**Implicit level.** A pool's level is (maximum minus start) divided by its step, with integer division. Each pool reaches its cap at 14 steps: (500 - 150) / 25 = 14, (400 - 120) / 20 = 14, (300 - 90) / 15 = 14, (500 - 150) / 25 = 14. The level is never stored. The game tests it in four checks whose callers are not identified (Open questions). Confidence: Medium-High.

### Hero level tests

Confidence: High for the formula, start and step words, front-end caller, and argument values.

Four tests ask whether a hero's upgrade level is above an argument n. Each test belongs to one hero and one pool.

1. The hero must be the active hero (index 0 for Tor, 1 for Roberta). Otherwise the test is false.
2. The level is trunc((maximum minus start) / step), where the maximum is the hero's current maximum for that pool. The division truncates toward zero.
3. The test is true when n is less than the level.

| Test | Hero index | Start (boot word) | Step (boot word) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tor health | 0 | 150 (0x319BC0) | 25 (0x319BD0) |
| Tor magic | 0 | 90 (0x319BC8) | 15 (0x319BD8) |
| Roberta health | 1 | 120 (0x319BC4) | 20 (0x319BD4) |
| Roberta magic | 1 | 150 (0x319BCC) | 25 (0x319BDC) |

The test reads the maximum, not the current value. It measures how far the maximum has grown through stat-up items, and it does not depend on how hurt the hero is. These tests are not low-health checks.

Test vectors (synthetic, from the start and step words):

- Tor health maximum 175: level (175 minus 150) / 25 = 1. With n = 0 the test is true.
- Tor health maximum 150: level 0. With n = 0 the test is false.
- Tor health maximum 140: (140 minus 150) / 25 = -0.4, which truncates to level 0. With n = 0 the test is false.

### Hero build guide: Tor's sword and the guard (shared hit rules)

Confidence: High for the clip numbers, windows and timings (action tables, clip banks, boot words and the routines that read them), including the pose timing below. Use with Attack router, Combo windows, Hit detection, Damage, Guard timer and per-attacker block, and SPEC-0033 Behavior 53 (ground clips). Tick numbers count from the tick on which the step's animation is requested (call it T); the clip's first key is shown on T and a clip with S = 2 shows one key per 4 ticks. A hit window of frames a to b is open on ticks T + 4a to T + 4b + 3 (from T + 1 when a is 0, because on T itself the window word still reads the clip that played before; see Hero battle timing table, transition-tick scan). Resolved: the open test reads the frame the clip is about to show on that tick (the clip's cursor was already advanced at the end of the previous pass), so the window is not delayed; what lags is the geometry. The blade position and the last-tick blade position come from the hero's pose of the pass before (one tick behind the drawn frame: on the first open tick the blade is where the clip was three quarters of a key earlier), while the enemy's bone capsules are this tick's (enemies are posed earlier in the same tick). So the first tick of a window can only reach what the blade had reached one tick before (Hit detection, Tick order). The swing is also tested only while the open test is true, never on the tick after the window closes.

**Tor's attack states (state 13).**

| Sub-state | Entry | Action (clip, ticks) | Hit window (ticks after T) | Root travel in the facing direction | Exits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 hold | attack press, no gesture | 22 (clip 52, 36), then loop 13 (clip 54, 40) | none | 0 | release before F = 50 to 2; at 50 or more to 10 (power-ready check each tick from 50) |
| 2 swing 1 | release | 14 (clip 44, 12 keys, 44) | 8 to 23 | 3.73 (keys 1.403, 0.908, 0.494, then about 0 until 0.31, 0.23, 0.19, 0.19) | press at F 20 to 33 to 3; below 20 to 6; above 33 closes the chain; done to 0 |
| 3 swing 2 | press from 2 | 15 (clip 38, 16 keys, 60) | 1 to 15 (on its entry tick the word still reads swing 1: a chain press at F 20 to 23 of swing 1 gives one more scan on that tick) | 3.51 (0.793, 1.121, 0.705, 0.589, then about 0, ending 0.06, 0.10, 0.05) | press at F 17 to 30 to 4; below 17 to 6; above 30 closes |
| 4 swing 3, finisher | press from 3 | 16 (clip 41, 13 keys, 48) | 8 to 39 | 3.82 forward and 0.31 to the left (1.371, 1.172, 0.346, 0.206, 0.035, 0, 0, 0, then 0.13 to 0.15) | done to 0 |
| 6 chained variant | early press in 2 or 3, or a repeat | 17 (clip 55, 14 keys, 52) | 4 to 27 | 3.19 (0.295, 1.252, 0.354, 0.149, 0.045, then 0 to 0.23) | a press at F 11 to 19 repeats it (at most 3 repeats) |
| 7 clockwise gesture attack | gesture mode 2 at the press | 19 (clip 42, 17 keys, 64) | 8 to 47 | 0 from the clip; the hero moves 0.25 x L per tick along the stick while the window is open | done to 0 |
| 8 counter-clockwise gesture attack | gesture mode 3 | 18 (clip 43, 17 keys, 64) | 8 to 51 | as 7 | done to 0 |
| 9 flick lunge | gesture mode 1 | 20 (clip 45, 11 keys, 40) | 12 to 35 | 13.38 forward, most of it in keys 3 to 5 (0.60, 3.15, 7.09, 2.32); held on the last clear point after a contact | done to 0 |
| 10 power step | release at F 50 or more | the Mote animation: 0x1B (clip 44, 44 ticks), 0x1C (clip 58, 64), 0x1D (clip 61, 132), 0x1E (clip 62, 100), 0x1F (clip 63, 128) | 0x1B 8 to 23; 0x1C 8 to 11; 0x1D 24 to 27; 0x1E and 0x1F 36 to 39 | 0x1B 3.73; the others 0; stick step as 7 | done to 0 |
| 12 plunge (air) | attack input in the air | 23 (clip 39, 17 keys, 64), then 24 (clip 40, 8 keys, 28) | 23: from 20 until 24 is requested (the last key, 16, carries the bit and the one-shot clip holds it through the whole fall); 24: its request tick (still reading 23) and 1 to 3 | 23 rises 2.98 in the first 16 ticks and the hero then falls: the vertical velocity is raised to -8.0 only on ticks where it is above -8.0, and the 0.3 gain then adds 0.32 per tick, so the fall starts at about 0.28 units per tick and speeds up (8.0 / 30, then 8.32 / 30, ...) until it is within 1.0 of the ground | 24 ends to state 1 |
| 13 recoil (blocked swing) | the target blocked a non-power swing from 2, 3, 4, 6, 11 or 12 | 26 (clip 57, 10 keys, 36), voice cue, rumble 0.8 s at 0.6 | none | backward 4.58 (0.593, 0.614, 0.689, 0.683, 0.657, 0.502, 0.278, 0.212, 0.178, 0.178 per key) | done to 0 |

Notes: the damage factor D (Damage) is 2.0 in sub-states 4 and 12, 0.75 in 6 (and 5), 1.0 otherwise. The first swing and the power step 0x1B share a clip. The window is open on a tick when event bit 0x1 is set on the current frame; a target already in the hit list is skipped, and the list is cleared at every sub-state change, so each of the three swings of a chain can hit the same enemy once. Reach: the swing is a cylinder of radius 1.0 and length W + 2.0 starting 0.25 along the weapon axis from the weapon attachment (W = 1.7 for sword 98, 2.5 for sword 101, 2.87 for sword 105, 1.5 unarmed), tested against the target's bone capsules, plus a swept test from last tick's blade position. Resolved: facing during a swing. While the hero is in state 13 its facing is set every tick to its stored heading, without any turn rate, and the stick never updates that heading in state 13 (nor on the tick of the press, because the press changes the state before the heading is refreshed). So the swing faces the heading the stick gave on the tick before the press (camera-relative, instantly, however far the previous facing was from it); with a centred stick that heading equals the facing. The same-tick stick is not used. Then the attack turn rewrites the heading (SPEC-0040 Behavior 20 item 7: instant turn toward a held target nearer than 4.0 and in front, otherwise a 0.25 blend toward a held target, or toward the nearest breakable in front within 12.0); the stick does not turn a swing except through the displacement of sub-states 7, 8 and 10.

**Input windows in ticks (F counts ticks inside the sub-state; the press edge is R2 or Circle).** F is 0 on the tick the sub-state is entered and goes up by 1 at the start of each later tick's machine step, before the tests, so a press on the tick n ticks after the entry tick has F = n. The tests are: below the low bound is the early variant, from the low bound up to and including the high bound is the chain, above is a closed chain; the bounds (20 and 33 for swing 1, 17 and 30 for swing 2, 50 for the power release, 0 for the unused quick tap) are fixed run-time values set at hero start.
- Hold: release at F below 50 is a swing, 50 or more a power step (Tor's quick-tap path never runs). The held counter only matters at release; the stored power is unused by Tor's damage.
- Swing 1: a press at F 20 to 33 chains swing 2. The swing clip lasts 44 ticks, so the chain window ends 11 ticks before the clip does; the hit window (ticks 8 to 23) is still open for its last 4 ticks when the chain window opens at 20.
- Swing 2: a press at F 17 to 30 chains the finisher. Swing 3 has no further chain.
- Early presses (F under 20 in swing 1, under 17 in swing 2) start the chained variant, which can repeat at F 11 to 19, three times at most.
- A press after the window closes the chain until the next attack start.

**Worked example: three swings on one standing enemy in reach (sword 98, stat A 4, no Mote, enemy defence 0, enemy in front).** Attack press on t0 (request 22, sub-state 1). Release on t10: sub-state 2, request 14. Swing 1 window opens on t18 and closes on t33; the first overlapping tick hits for 4 x 1.0 = 4 and the target is now in the hit list. Press on t35 (F 25): sub-state 3, request 15, hit list cleared; window t35 to t50; hit for 4 on the first overlapping tick. Press on t61 (F 26): sub-state 4, request 16, list cleared; window t69 to t100; hit for 4 x 2.0 = 8. Done on t110 (49 ticks after the request), then state 1. Total 16 with a sword of stat A 4, or 160 with sword 105 (stat A 40). With an odd Mote in word 2 each landed swing also pays 3 MP and uses Mote charge (1 MP for an even Mote); with no Mote nothing is paid. The hero moves forward about 3.7, 3.5 and 3.8 units across the three swings if nothing blocks it, so a close enemy is overrun unless its body stops the root step.

**Worked example: a block against an enemy strike (class 17 enemy, Tor with a shield in slot 2).** Tor holds L1 in state 20 (request 63; no travel). The enemy strikes with damage value 20, flags 0, facing Tor: the dot of the two facings is -0.9 (at most -0.5). The shield stat A is 5 and word 1 is empty: block b = 5, armour none, damage 15. Tor plays shield-bank cue 0 and request 64 (16 ticks, slides back 3.42 units), is added to the enemy's hit list and loses 15. If the enemy's knockback K for that slot is above 0 and damage was above 0, Tor's velocity becomes K x unit(9 x enemy facing + (0, 12, 0)) and he enters state 48. A strike from the side (dot above -0.5) takes full damage with the flinch (request 32 with a weapon) and no block. A strike with the unblockable flag 0x01 ignores the guard. With an idle hero (state 1, weapon out, shield equipped) a threat in front within 10 units sets the 40-tick guard timer, which counts as guarding for 40 ticks and raises the shield (request 63).

**Moving while guarding.** State 20 writes no travel and plays a still shield clip: the hero turns to the stick at once (rate 1.0) and plays the turn clip 119 when the stick is more than about 18 degrees off, but does not walk. The attack selector comes first in the button chain (SPEC-0033 Behavior 1A), so an attack press while the hero is not busy leaves state 20 for state 13 (Resolved, High). The order inside the press tick is: the button chain runs in the hero's input step before the state switch; its first step sees the press edge of R2 or Circle (or the button merely held, with Square not pressed) and, because the hero is guarding (state 20, or the 40-tick guard timer running), enters state 13 at once, without the check for an equipped weapon that the unguarded case makes; the attack start picks the sub-state from the stick gesture mode; the walking tiers and the heading update are skipped for the tick; the state switch then runs state 13's routine on the same tick, and state 20's routine never runs on that tick (so the L1-release exit is not reached). Releasing L1 returns to state 1 (state 32 when L2 is held and the previous state was 32). There is no stamina, no guard break (Resolved, Medium-High: a blocked hit never writes a hero state, so no hit strength, flag or timer ends the guard; the only forced exits are a knockback into state 48 when the attacker has a knockback value and damage got through, and the unblockable and forced-block flags of the strike) and no shield that wears out; a Mote in word 1 loses charge on elemental blocks and the block falls back to stat A alone when it is gone.

**What a port most likely gets wrong (Tor's sword and the guard).**
1. Giving swing 1 a long window. It is open on ticks 8 to 23 only, and the chain window (F 20 to 33) opens after it closes.
2. Moving the hero with a velocity during swings. Forward travel is clip root motion, front-loaded (three quarters of swing 1's travel is in its first 12 ticks); only sub-states 7, 8 and 10 add a stick displacement of 0.25 x L per tick.
3. Hitting the same enemy more than once per swing, or only once per chain. The list clears at each sub-state change, so one hit per swing.
4. Dealing damage with an unarmed swing, or with a swing from behind a guarding enemy (the swing then does nothing at all and is not recorded).
5. Applying the damage factor D wrongly: only the finisher and the plunge are doubled; the chained variant is 0.75.
6. Blocking by facing the attacker's position. The rule is the dot of the two facing vectors at most -0.5, per attacker; a guard from the side or back takes full damage.
7. Applying armour or the shield to boss melee hits (bosses subtract their value unchanged) or letting a blocked hit cost nothing (it costs the shield-reduced damage; callers that want nothing pass 0).
8. Forgetting the recoil: a blocked non-power swing throws Tor back 4.6 units for 36 ticks, and a power-step swing is never blocked.
9. Starting the shield only from L1: an idle armed hero also raises it for 40 ticks after a threat, which blocks projectiles and strikes that arrive in that time.
10. Turning the swing toward the stick of the press tick. The swing faces the heading of the tick before the press and then only the attack turn changes it; a port that reads the stick on the press tick, or turns at a rate, differs by up to 180 degrees.
11. Delaying the hit window by a tick. The window opens on its nominal tick; only the blade geometry it tests is one tick old.
12. Exiting the guard on an attack press only after the guard animation ends. The press leaves state 20 on the same tick and state 13 runs that tick.

### Hero build guide: Roberta (wand shots, swat, spin, leap, full release)

Confidence: High for the tables and numbers (they restate the sections above and the disc clip data); High for the first tick of a release frame (the shot is built on j = 4f and first moved on 4f + 1); Medium for the air-shot tail; the sources of each rule are in the sections named. Roberta's walk, run, reverse, sidestep, jump, fall, ledge and ladder numbers are the ones of SPEC-0033 (Behavior 53 and the sections it names); her differences are listed at the end of the tables. "j" below is the number of ticks after the tick on which the clip was requested (the clip starts in that tick's animation pass); a frame f of a four-ticks-per-frame clip is read from tick j = 4f, and a window on frames a to b is read on ticks j = 4a to 4b + 3 (j = 1 to 4b + 3 when a is 0). All clips are in the main family and play at rate 1 unless a swat is slowed (rate 0.75, every length and window times 4/3).

**State 13 for Roberta, sub-state by sub-state** (ids and clips from her action table; "keys" is the frame count; "root" is the total root travel of the clip in units, forward positive, applied by the ordinary root step, not by any velocity):

| Sub-state | Action id (clip, keys, ticks) | Release frame (shot leaves at j) | Melee window (j, step factor D) | Root travel | Exits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 hold | 17 (5, 23, 88) then 25 (6, 10, loop 40) | none | none | about 0 | release F < 80 to 2; F of 80 or more to 9 (14 for Motes 162, 169, 171); failed check cancels |
| 2 first shot | 12 (0, 10, 36) | frame 3 (12) | frame 3 (12 to 15), D 1.0 | 0 | press F < 20 to 10; 20 to 40 to 3; above 40 closes; done to 0 |
| 3 second shot | 13 (1, 15, 56) | frame 4 (16) | none | +4.68 (1.41 of it in key 0, 0.7 in key 1) | same windows, to 10, 4 or closed |
| 4 third shot | 14 (2, 17, 64) | frame 7 (28) | frames 7 to 9 (28 to 39), D 2.0 | +3.36 (keys 4 to 7 carry 3.0) | done to 0 |
| 5 swat | 16 (4, 13, 48) | none | frames 0 to 3 (1 to 15), D 0.5 | +1.12 forward and +0.52 sideways, front-loaded in keys 0 to 2 | done to 0 |
| 6 / 7 spin | 22 (18) / 21 (16): 4 keys, loop 16 ticks per cycle; end 148 (19) / 147 (17): 12 keys, 44 ticks | none | none (a spin never hits) | 0 (the stick step moves her) | see Spin below |
| 8 leap | 23 (9, 18, 68) | none | none (the leap scan is off) | +29.2 | contact or done to 13 |
| 9 full release | by Mote: 0x1B (10, 20, 76) default and no Mote; 0x1C (11, 30, 116) for 168, 170, 172; 0x1E (12, 25, 96) for 161, 164; 0x1F (13, 35, 136) for 167, 173 | frame 5 (20); frame 4 (16); frame 6 (24); frame 8 (32) | none | -4.68 for 0x1B, +0.28 for 0x1C, 0 for 0x1E, -0.16 for 0x1F | done to 0 |
| 10 rapid shot | 15 (3, 6, 20) | frame 2 (8) | none | 0 | press F 11 to 20 repeats (to 10 again, cap 5); done to 0 |
| 11 air shot | 19 (7, 6, 20) | frame 3 (12) | frames 3 to 5 (12 to 20), D 1.0 | 0 | done to 0 |
| 12 air swat | 20 (8, 10, 36) | none | frames 3 to 4 (12 to 19), D 0.5 | 0 | done to 0 |
| 13 landing | idle picker id | none | the melee scan runs | 0 | counter above 10 or facing within dot 0.98 to 0 |
| 14 release, Motes 162, 169, 171 | 0x1D (20, 16, 60) then 63 (21, 16, loop 64) | none (fired flag preset) | none | 0 | no Mote object active to 0 |

Notes on the table. The first-shot window values (F of 20 to 40) count ticks after the sub-state entry, with the done test first, then F + 1, then the press test (Combo windows); The melee windows of the quick shots are real: the wand cylinder of the swing (reach + 2.0, radius 1.0; Hit detection) damages any hittable target in front of her on those ticks, once per target per sub-state, with factor D, and pays no MP and no Mote charge (only Tor's melee pays). Sub-state 4 therefore has a doubled wand strike at the moment the 90-units-per-second shot leaves. The release frames only launch a shot when the launch gate holds (Roberta shot projectile). The shot speeds per tick are: 60 units per second is 1.0 per tick, 90 is 1.5, 75 is 1.25, 30 is 0.5, 20 is one third.

**Input windows (ticks).**
- Hold: threshold 80; cue and power-ready check from F = 7 and 8; the stored power F / 120 is unused.
- Chain: press at F of 20 to 40 of sub-state 2 or 3 goes forward; F below 20 gives the rapid shot; above 40 ends the chain. Rapid shot: press at F of 11 to 20 repeats it (the clip is done on tick 21), at most 5 times per attack.
- Gesture entries: flick then press (mode lasts 15 ticks) is the leap; clockwise (10 ticks) sub-state 6; counter-clockwise (10 ticks) sub-state 7; a locked target nearer than 5.0 with no gesture starts the swat.
- Spin: the replay press counts on 13 of each 16 ticks (below); the stick moves her at 0.25 L units per tick while the spin clip plays (L about 1.46 at full push), vertical velocity kept.

**Spin (sub-states 6 and 7).** Guard from the first tick to the last tick of the sub-state, including the end clip: a class-17 enemy strike from any direction does nothing (no damage, no take-hit, no hit-list entry, a block spark per overlapping tick). It does not stop boss hits, which follow their own rules, and it does not block flagged unblockable strikes. Timeline with n replays: the cycle clip loops every 16 ticks; on the first tick of a cycle's end (j = 16 (n + 1)) with the replay flag clear the end clip is requested; the end clip is done on its 45th tick; the sub-state ends and she is in state 1. A press sets the replay flag only on ticks j with j mod 16 from 3 to 15; the flag is consumed (and the cycle restarted, count + 1) at the next boundary while the count is below 10. Maximum: 11 cycles, 176 ticks, plus 45 for the end clip.

**Shots, quick reference** (details in Roberta shot projectile). Quick shots (sub-states 2, 3, 4, 10, 11): sub-states 2, 3 and 11 are class 1 (60 units per second, radius 1.0, life 180 ticks, straight); sub-state 4 is class 2 (90, 1.0, 180); sub-state 10 is class 0 homing (30, 1.0, 120, mode 1, step factor 0.33). Payment at a hit on a hittable target: no Mote 5 MP (class 1), 10 MP (class 2) or 3 MP (class 0); with an odd Mote 3 MP and 4 charge, with an even Mote 1 MP and 2 charge. Full release: the Mote table (Table B) with the Mote object of Charm attack hits; paid at release or at the first shot (40 MP and 40 charge for an odd Mote, 20 MP and 25 charge for an even one, 10 MP and no charge with no Mote). A launch only needs 1 MP.

**Magic pool.** Start 150 (maximum rises with stat-up items); health 120. Trickle toward a floor of int(0.1 x max) + 1 only, at one point per 59 ticks (Regeneration); spending past the pool sets it to 0. Nothing refuses a shot for lack of MP except the 1 MP launch gate; a Mote release also needs its release cost in the power-ready check at the start of the hold.

**Hit reaction and death.** A hit enters state 17 unless she is in sub-state 1 or 9 with a Mote in word 2 (those are never interrupted; the Mote word counts as non-empty even at -1). She never recoils from blocked swings and has no invulnerability window. The flinch clip is id 32 or 192 chosen on Tor's weapon entry (State 17), a looping 44-tick clip ended by the 43rd-tick progress gate; blocked hits play the block reaction instead (request 64, clip 23, 12 keys, 44 ticks, sliding her back 3.16 units; sub-states 1 and 9 skip it). Knockback from class-17 strikes puts her in state 48 with velocity K x unit(9 x attacker facing + (0, 12, 0)). Death: state 16, clip id 34 (17 keys, one-shot 64 ticks, sliding back 1.92 units), voice 45; the hand-over is in SPEC-0055.

**Differences from Tor** (everything not listed is shared): a wand or rod (items 111 to 118; reach table of SPEC-0021) instead of a sword; books as shields (types 1 to 3); her air attack is a shot or swat chosen by the locked-target distance (8.0), with no plunge and no strafe-jump air attack; her jump voice is cue 56; her climb-off-top step forward is 0.05 (SPEC-0033); her spin guards from any side where Tor needs L1 and facing; her swats are the only attack steps slowed by Slow; her gesture windows are 10 ticks (Tor 25); her hold is 80 ticks (Tor 50) and her quick release launches shots instead of swinging; the Rope Gun entry is shared (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).

**Worked example: quick-shot chain.** Press at t0 (state 13, sub-state 1, requests 22 then 17). Release at t10: sub-state 2, request 12, weapon cue 2, voice 4. Wand A = 5, no Mote, enemy 15 units ahead and hittable. Frame 3 is read at t22: the shot is built at the hand anchor (needs 1 MP); the wand also strikes anything within about reach + 2 units on t22 to t25 for 5. The shot moves 1.0 per tick and reaches the enemy near t36: damage 5 (A x 1 x 1 x 1 x 1 = 5), MP 5 paid, no charge. Press at t35 (F = 25): sub-state 3, request 13 (she slides forward 4.68 in all, 3.0 of it in the first 16 ticks); shot at t51. Press at t61 (F = 26): sub-state 4, request 14, weapon 4 and voice 6; strike at t89 to t100 for 10 and a 1.5-units-per-tick shot at t89 paying 10 MP at its hit; done at t126, sub-state 0, state 1.

**Worked example: leap.** Flick, then press at t0 inside the 15-tick window: sub-state 8, request 23, her position stored as the leap point. From the first tick the step clears her body-collision flag, sets the fired flag and stops the auto-facing, and weapon cue 17 plays once on that tick. Nearly still until t24, the dash covers 29.2 units from about t28 to t56 (6.8, 7.7 and 6.4 units in keys 8, 9 and 10, which are t32 to t43). If the body-contact flag rises at t40 she is put back on her last clear point and the leap ends at once; otherwise it ends at t69. Landing: sub-state 13 for at most 11 ticks while she turns at rate 0.25 toward the reverse of her heading; the melee scan runs.

**Worked example: full release with Mote 161 (Lava).** Hold from t0 with MP 150 and the Mote at charge 100 (the press tick already counts: F is n + 1 on tick tn while the button stays held, and F at the release tick equals the number of held ticks). t6 (F = 7) starts the power-ready check (passes: no Mote object active, wand held, charge above 0, MP at least 40). t7 (F = 8) the weapon cue 5 loop; t80 (F = 81) the Mote cue 0 loop; t89 clip 17 is done and clip 25 loops. Release at t100 (F = 100): sub-state 9, fired flag cleared, request 0x1E, weapon cue 0, Mote cue 1, the Lava object spawns at her position + facing x reach (hits through its own rules). Frame 6 is read at t124: the launch needs 1 MP and a free slot; the first shot pays 40 MP and 40 charge (MP 110, charge 60) and leaves at 1.25 units per tick (range 223.75 units: 179 moves), radius 1.0, damage S x C x T where S = A x 1.5 x 1.25 (stat F 25) and C about 2.0 against a matching type (stat G 100), times the table value T. Done at t196 (a 25-key clip passes the 0.99 gate one tick before the 4(F - 1) + 1 rule; Hero battle timing table).

**Worked example: spin against a strike.** Clockwise gesture and press at t0: sub-state 6, request 22; the stick is pushed forward at full length so she moves 0.25 L per tick. A press at t5 sets the flag. At t16 (progress 0.19, F = 16 above 4) the flag restarts the cycle (count 1, F 0). No press follows. At t32 the flag is clear: request 148, the spin step stops. A class-17 enemy hits at t20 (any facing): no damage, no hit-list entry, a spark. The end clip is done at t77 and she returns to state 1.

**Resolved in this pass.** The air attack is chosen by locked-target distance, not by the Mote or the attack id. Her hurt clip is selected on Tor's weapon entry. The spin test uses the clip's key count. Her flinch clips are 44 ticks (a looping 11-key clip and a one-shot 23-key clip at speed byte 1), not 40 and 88. The leap carries 29.2 units of root travel. Quick shots, the second shot, the third shot and the default full release move her by their clips (see the table).

**What a port most likely gets wrong (Roberta).**
1. Charging MP at launch for quick shots: they pay at a hit on a hittable target (zero damage included), never at launch or on a miss; only a full release pays at release or at its first shot.
2. Standing still during shots and swats. Her clips move her: +4.68 in the second shot, +3.36 in the third, +1.1 in the swat, -4.7 in the default full release, +29.2 in the leap.
3. Forgetting the wand strike inside quick-shot clips: sub-states 2, 4 and 11 hit nearby targets in melee at the release frame (sub-state 4 doubled) without paying MP.
4. Letting her spin hit, or blocking only in front. The spin never damages; it blocks class-17 strikes from any direction and lasts through its end clip, not only while the cycle loops.
5. Reading the replay press at any tick: it counts only on 13 of each 16 ticks and is spent at the next boundary; up to 10 replays.
6. Choosing the air attack by the equipped Mote. The Mote plays no part: no target or one at 8.0 or more is a shot, a nearer lock is a swat; no wand means no air attack at all.
7. Thinking a quick release at F below 80 can be a full release: the threshold is 80 ticks of hold, and a failed power-ready check at F = 7 or later cancels the whole charge.
8. Using the stored power for damage or speed: it is unused.
9. Making her recoil, or applying the target's Blocking Angle to her shots: neither exists for Roberta.
10. Ending the hurt state at 40 ticks: with a weapon on Tor she flinches 43 ticks (a looping clip), and the clip choice follows Tor's weapon, not hers.
11. Letting the busy rule cover sub-state 14 or the spins: only sub-states 1 and 9 with a Mote in word 2 are never interrupted.
12. Rounding the damage: the product chops toward zero in single precision (sword and wand totals can be one lower than exact arithmetic).

### Hero battle timing table (tick reference)

Confidence: High unless a row or item says otherwise. This section gathers, per action, the tick on which each phase starts and ends, so that a rewrite can match the original to the tick. The rules behind each number are in the sections named; where an older sentence elsewhere in this spec gives a rounder figure, the figure here is the exact one.

**Units and counting rules (read these first).**
- One tick is one main-loop pass, 1/60 s (SPEC-0007). Every count in this section is in ticks. The original stores seconds only for status durations and a few timers; those become ticks as seconds x 60, with no rounding issue for the whole-second values used here.
- T is the tick on which a step's animation is requested inside the hero update. The clip starts in the animation pass of the same tick, so its key 0 is drawn on T. j is the number of ticks after T. All hero attack clips have speed byte 2: one key every 4 ticks at play rate 1.
- Frame-bit reads (the attack window, Roberta's release bit, the state-20 exit flag) are made at the start of the hero update. On tick T + j with j of at least 1 they read key floor(j / 4) of the new clip. On j = 0 they still read the clip that was playing before the request. A window on keys a to b is therefore open on j = 4a to 4b + 3, and a window that starts at key 0 opens on j = 1 for a fresh clip.
- Done ticks (0.99 gate, SPEC-0005 Clip progress): a one-shot clip of F keys is done on j = 4(F - 1) + 1 when F is below 25, and one tick earlier, on j = 4(F - 1), from 25 keys on, because the progress adds the stored fraction (exactly at the threshold for 25 keys: Medium-High there, since it rests on the divide rounding to nearest); a looping clip of 3 to 24 keys on j = 4F - 1; Roberta's one-shot flinch 192 (speed byte 1) on j = 2(F - 1) + 1. The 0.95 gate of the death state passes on j = 63 for the 17-key death clip.
- Slow (status 3) multiplies the play rate by 0.75 only in Tor's attack sub-states 2 to 12, Tor's plunge and Roberta's swats (sub-states 5 and 12). There the cursor is 0.1875 j: key k starts on the first j with 0.1875 j at least k, and every length grows by 4/3.
- The melee test of a window tick uses the blade pose of the previous tick and the target's bones of this tick (Hit detection, Tick order of the tests).
- F is the sub-state counter: 0 on the entry tick, so a press made n ticks after the entry is tested with F = n. Bounds are inclusive. The hold is the exception: the attack start sets F to 0 and the charge step of the same press tick already adds 1 for the held button, so F is n + 1 on the n-th tick after the press while the button stays held, and F at the release tick (the first tick with neither R2 nor Circle held) equals the number of held ticks. Tor's power-ready check therefore first runs 49 ticks after the press, Roberta's 6 ticks after it, and a press held on 50 (Tor) or 80 (Roberta) ticks releases into the power step or the full release.
- Enemy-side readers (common-enemy strikes, the projectile pool, boss attacks) run before the hero in each tick. A state the hero enters on tick X is seen by them from tick X + 1; a hit they deliver on tick X is seen by the hero's own update on tick X (SPEC-0007, When a hit on a hero takes effect).

**Tor, state 13 (play rate 1).**

| Step | Clip keys (ticks) | Hit ticks j | Input window (F) | Done j | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 hold | 22: 10 keys (36), then 13: loop of 10 keys (40) | none | release with F below 50: swing 1; 50 or more: power step | none | 13 is requested from the tick on which 22 passes its gate (j = 37). The power-ready check runs on every held tick from F = 50 |
| 2 swing 1 | 12 (44) | 8 to 23 | press F 1 to 19: chained variant; 20 to 33: swing 2; 34 or more: chain closed | 45 | the chain window opens while the hit window is still open (F 20 to 23) |
| 3 swing 2 | 16 (60) | 1 to 15, plus the entry tick when entered at F 20 to 23 of swing 1 (transition-tick scan, below) | press F 1 to 16: chained variant; 17 to 30: finisher; 31 or more: closed | 61 | also arms the exit to state 20 (shield and L1) |
| 4 finisher | 13 (48) | 8 to 39 | none | 49 | damage factor 2.0 |
| 6 chained variant | 14 (52) | 4 to 27, plus its entry tick (below) | repeat press F 11 to 19, at most 3 repeats, each restarting the clip | 53 after the last (re)start | damage factor 0.75 |
| 7 clockwise | 17 (64) | 8 to 47 | none | 65 | stick displacement 0.25 L per window tick |
| 8 counter-clockwise | 17 (64) | 8 to 51 | none | 65 | as 7 |
| 9 flick lunge | 11 (40) | 12 to 35 | none | 41 | root travel 13.38, most of it on j = 12 to 23 |
| 10 power step | 0x1B 12 (44); 0x1C 17 (64); 0x1D 34 (132); 0x1E 26 (100); 0x1F 33 (128) | 8 to 23; 8 to 11; 24 to 27; 36 to 39; 36 to 39 | none | 45; 65; 132; 100; 128 | MP, charge and the Mote object on the release tick T itself |
| 13 recoil | 26: 10 (36) | none | none | B + 38 | B is the tick on which the swing was blocked: the block sets the sub-state inside B's melee scan, the arm table requests 26 on B + 1 (with the voice cue and the 0.8 s rumble), so the clip starts on B + 1 and is done on B + 1 + 37. F is not reset by the block |
| plunge (air phases 8 and 9) | 23: 17 (64), then 24: 8 (28) | 23: from j = 20 until 24 is requested (its last key carries the bit and the one-shot clip holds it); 24: its request tick and j' = 1 to 3 | none | 24: j' = 29, then state 1 | one hit list for the whole plunge; it rises for 16 ticks, then falls from a vertical velocity of -8.0 that keeps growing by 0.32 per tick (the -8.0 is written only while the velocity is above it) until the gap is 1.0 or less |

**Roberta, state 13 (play rate 1; the shot is built on the release tick and first moved one tick later).**

| Step | Clip keys (ticks) | Shot built on j | Melee ticks j | Input window (F) | Done j |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 hold | 17: 23 (88), then 25: loop of 10 (40) | none | none | release F below 80: shot 1; 80 or more: full release. Check from F = 7, loop cue from F = 8 | none (25 from j = 89) |
| 2 shot 1 | 12 (36) | 12 | 12 to 15 | press F 1 to 19: rapid shot; 20 to 40: shot 2; 41 or more: closed | 37 |
| 3 shot 2 | 15 (56) | 16 | none | same bounds: rapid, shot 3 or closed | 57 |
| 4 shot 3 | 17 (64) | 28 | 28 to 39 (factor 2.0) | none | 65 |
| 5 swat | 13 (48) | none | 1 to 15 (factor 0.5) | none | 49 |
| 6, 7 spin | loop of 4 (16 per cycle); end clip 12 (44) | none | none | replay press counts on j mod 16 = 3 to 15; at most 10 replays | end clip done 45 ticks after its request, which comes on j = 16(n + 1) for n replays |
| 8 leap | 23: 18 (68) | none | none | none | 69, or at once on body contact; then the landing, at most 11 ticks |
| 9 full release | 0x1B 20 (76); 0x1C 30 (116); 0x1E 25 (96); 0x1F 35 (136) | 20; 16; 24; 32 | none | none | 77; 116; 96; 136 |
| 10 rapid shot | 15: 6 (20) | 8 | none | repeat press F 11 to 20 (the done test of F 21 comes first), at most 5 repeats | 21 |
| 11 air shot | 19: 6 (20) | 12 | 12 to 20 | none | 21 |
| 12 air swat | 20: 10 (36) | none | 12 to 19 (factor 0.5) | none | 37 |
| 14 release of 162, 169, 171 | 0x1D: 16 (60), then 63 (loop) | none (fired flag preset) | none | none | 0x1D done on 61; then 63 until no Mote object is active |

**Shot flight (High).** Each shot slot is stepped in the hero update before the state switch: first the arming counter (when above 0) and the life go down by 1; a life below 1 disarms the shot on that tick without a move; otherwise the shot moves speed / 60 units (steered first in modes 1 to 5 when its arming counter is 0) and is then tested against the ground, characters and the world. A shot of life L built on tick X therefore moves and tests on ticks X + 1 to X + L - 1 and is gone on X + L. A quick shot of class 1 leaves the hand on j = 12, is first tested on j = 13 at 1.0 unit from the hand, and expires on j = 192 after 179 units. A target whose capsule surface lies D units ahead of the shot sphere along a straight path is reached on the first tick k after the build with k x speed / 60 of at least D.

**Guard and block (High).**
- L1 press in a free state: state 20 is written in the hero's input step and state 20's routine runs on the same tick G (request 63). Enemy strikes and pool shots test the guard from G + 1. On the L1 release tick R the routine writes state 1; enemy tests of tick R still see the guard, and from R + 1 they do not. There is no raise or lower time beyond this one-tick lag, and the guard does not depend on the shield clip's pose.
- Guard timer (idle, weapons drawn, shield equipped): a threat raises it to 40 inside the state-1 routine; it is lowered at the start of each hero update, so enemy tests on the next 40 ticks are blocked (the 40th with the timer still at 1). While the threat lasts the timer is set to 40 again every tick.
- Block reaction (request 64): Tor 5 keys (16 ticks), done on j = 17, sliding back 3.42; Roberta 12 keys (44), done on j = 45, sliding back 3.17. It starts on the block tick (the strike runs before the hero's animation pass). In state 20, and in state 1 while the guard timer is above 0, the routine does not replace 64 until it passes its gate, then requests 63. In state 1 with the guard timer at 0 the idle request replaces 64 on the same tick, so the reaction is cut. A second block while 64 is current does not restart it (same id), but the shield cue plays again.
- Attack press while guarding: state 13 starts on the press tick; enemy tests of that tick still see state 20.

**On the hero when hit (High unless marked).**
- Hit-stop: none. The take-hit, the common-enemy strike and the projectile pool change no world time scale, no tick rate, no play rate and no clip speed, and request no pause; the game runs on through every hit.
