The action-state switch routes state 16 to the death routine (SPEC-0033 Behavior 4A.6); the interaction reading is withdrawn.
- **Hero action states: start sources** (resolved): states 30, 35 and 47 are written by the in-air ledge chooser; state 19 by the Cross route and the air landing chooser; state 21 from the ground, the air and state 38; state 23 by the Square sidle prompt; state 27 by the attack button with the rope gun; state 37 by an edge contact while walking; state 41 by the ladder mount test; state 49 by the Square feed prompt; state 58 by pushing into a block face for 15 ticks (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51; SPEC-0047).
- **Attack-family starter order** (resolved, Medium-High): the starter is the in-air ledge chooser. Result 0 writes state 30; result 1 writes state 47 when rising and 35 when falling. There is no double write.
- **Take-hit animation** (Resolved, High): with blocked flag 0 and the hero not busy, request 0x20 plays when the first weapon-list entry exists and is not empty, else 0xC0; with the blocked flag the block reaction plays (request 0x40). The branch word of the 43 and 44 cue pair is the hero variant word (Roberta). A busy hero (Tor sub-states 1 and 10, Roberta 1 and 9, with equipment word 2 not 0) gets no reaction (Taking a hit).
- **Attack cue chooser** (Resolved, High): it is the hero footstep cue (SPEC-0031); no attack cue chooser exists.
- **0.5 direction cap (Answered, High; SPEC-0033 Behaviors 11 and 50).** The capped camera-relative direction helper is reached by the hero jump handlers for states 7–11 and strafe jumps 43–46. State-13 attack steps use a separate stick displacement path scaled by 0.25 (Attack router); the known state-13 route does not call the capped helper. The earlier attack-step attribution is withdrawn.
- **Landing into state 7** (resolved, High): state 7 is the standing jump and fall state, not an attack router. The walk-off and the state-47 flag branch enter it in a falling phase (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 9A, 50 and 51).
- **Cancel bit** (resolved, High): the "cancel" of state 41 is the jump off the ladder, on the Cross press edge (SPEC-0033 Behavior 13).
- **Sub-mode 3 threshold in state 30** (moved): open in SPEC-0033 Open questions (Traversal bodies).
- **Lunge and strike sub-mode meanings** (resolved): the seven sub-modes of state 30 are the ledge hang: grab, move to the edge, hang idle, hang, pull up and the two shimmy ends (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **Targeted approach (state 41): waypoints and predicates** (resolved, High): state 41 is the ladder. The waypoints 0.45 + 1.25 k are the rungs, the 0.6 window the rung search, 120 the mount from above, and 1.3 and 1.27 the top tests of the two rung clips (SPEC-0033 Behavior 13).
- **Targeted approach (state 41): effect and helper calls** (resolved): index 140 is sound cue 140, one per rung step and with the mount clip; the two helpers add the jump-off push (-0.5) and lift (2.0) (SPEC-0033 Behavior 13).
- **Attack mode value (states 13, 41)** (Answered, High): the value selecting Tor's or Roberta's attack table is the hero variant word +0xF80 (0 Tor, 1 Roberta); the 0.05 step of ladder sub-mode 8 applies to Roberta only (SPEC-0033 Behavior 13). The separate value written by a world press trigger is stored at +0x1560 and consumed as Tor's hero-voice cue index when state 13 runs sub-state 13. A valid local cue is 0–11; mapped values 12 or greater play nothing (SPEC-0031, Hero voice slot uses; Edge cases). The trigger press enters only through the Tor-gated volume handlers. This does not establish which trigger records are physically reachable during ordinary play.
- **Grab (state 23)** (moved): state 23 is the sidle; its open points are in SPEC-0033 Open questions (Traversal bodies).
- **Stance group (state 21): sub-routines** (moved): state 21 is the climb; its sub-mode table is in SPEC-0033 Behavior 51.
- **Staged special (state 27)** (resolved in part): state 27 is the rope gun; its phases and its entry are in SPEC-0033 Behavior 51 (the state-13 charge routine enters it when the aim mode is on, an anchor is selected and R2 is held). One behavior-record gate releases the rope when the active hero's selected behavior record has kind 0x7E. The hit callback also releases the rope while the hero is in state 27 when the global hit-processing gate allows the callback to proceed; it does this before attacker-specific processing, so release does not guarantee damage. The phase-3 landing flag and phase-4 action-selector path are also listed in SPEC-0033 Behavior 51; the phase-4 path is gated by a fresh Square press. A broad hero virtual-update method also calls the reset helper unconditionally within its fixed per-tick sequence, though the upstream dispatch gate and cadence are unknown. The behavior record type remains open (High for the release gates and Square input meaning).
- **Record approach (states 19, 47 and 58): height offset** (moved): open in SPEC-0033 Open questions (Traversal bodies).
- **Record approach (state 47): flag-set branch** (resolved, High): the hero goes to state 7 with air phase 0 while the vertical velocity is still above 0, and with air phase 5 (the descent of the jump states, SPEC-0033 Behavior 50) when it is falling above the ground.
- **Exits to state 4 (states 19, 30, 35, 47 and 58)** (resolved, Medium-High): they test event bit 0x40 and the stick pushed forward (the forward value of SPEC-0033 Behavior 2 positive), the same test as the boosted-jump exit (Stick-forward exit to state 4).
- **Targeted special (state 49)** (moved): state 49 is feeding a petpet (SPEC-0047).
- **Sub-kind mapping (state 37)** (resolved): the test on action ids 2 to 6 is the depth check of the drop from a walk (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).

- **Attack rotation playability** (Resolved, High): the table at 0x315C68 is the quick-item panel's candidate lists, and the test is whether the party owns the item (SPEC-0021); no attack animation rotation exists.
- **Stored charge power** (+0xFA4; Resolved, High): it is written once, at the release, as F x (1/60) x 0.5 with no cap in storage. The Roberta path passes it to the shot spawner, which clamps the copy it stores to 0.1 to 1.0 at both ends (Low-end clamp).
- **Charge resource check and second gate** (Resolved, High): the check is the power-ready check (Power-ready check). The charged loop is a looping sound: weapon-bank cue 5 from F = 8, and Mote-bank cue 0 once F is above 80 with a Mote in word 2 (Charge completion).
- **Tor fully-charged cue** (Answered): none on the held path at 50; no cue is stated at the threshold.
- **Conflict C4** (Resolved, High): Tor's arm table and Roberta's arm table run after the matching charge routine on the same sub-state word; the charge routine makes the transitions and the arm table the requests (State 13).
- **Consumable status test. Answered.** A consumable buff or immunity applies only when its status timer is 0 or below (inactive). See the consumable bullet under Consumable and slot-0 cross-references.
- **Conflict C1** (Resolved, High): the state-20 exit has no stick test. Sub-state 3 always arms it, sub-state 4 arms it in a frame with event flag 0x40, and it fires with a shield and L1 (0x0004) held or pressed. The state-30 exit to state 4 tests the stick pushed forward (Medium-High; SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **Conflict C3** (Resolved, High): the mode value of state 13 is the hero variant. The sub-state table is Tor's arm table and the mode map is Roberta's arm table, keyed by the sub-state (State 13).
- **Conflict C2** (Resolved, Medium-High): the placement given in State 30 is right.
- **State-30 action tests** (moved): open in SPEC-0033 Open questions (Traversal bodies).
- **Attack animation choice** (Resolved, High): six fixed ids in the program, with no disc location: 0x1B (the fallback, also with no Mote), 0x1C, 0x12, 0x1D, 0x1E and 0x1F. The flag word is the hero index; an id outside 160 to 173 gives 0x1B; the callers are Tor's sub-state 10 and Roberta's sub-states 9 and 14 (Attack animation choice, Attack router).
- **State-13 lookups** (Resolved, High): the two lookups of the state-20 exit test that the hero's shield-family entry exists and is not empty.
- **State-13 sub-state 13 helpers.** Resolved (High for method identity and request order): the effect step is a voice cue (the cue chosen from the hero's +0x1560 word, on the hero's voice slot), and the call with 0.8, 0.6 and 2 is a rumble request with preset 2, duration 0.8 s and intensity 0.6 (State 13, Tor's sub-state 13; SPEC-0033, Rumble). The float 10.0 call invokes the hero block-reaction method; that method ignores the float, may request shield-bank cue 0 when sound is available, and requests animation 64. The caller then requests animation 26. This is the request order; which animation is visibly presented is not established.
- **State 21 sub-mode 20.** Resolved: the state-21 map lists sub-modes 0 to 18, and sub-modes 19 and above do nothing; sub-mode 20 does nothing as well.
- **Bounce writes from state 21.** Resolved: the climb state 21 does not write state 56. Sub-mode 2 and sub-modes 13 to 16 request animation 56 as a plain request (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **General normal-attack conversion** (Resolved, High): truncation toward zero after the clamp at 0, with no minimum; the take-hit runs only for damage above 0 or an absorbed hit (Damage).
- **Charm routine identity** (Resolved, High): the charm routines are the Mote power-attack object hit handlers, with the rule in Charm attack hits. The step with Q x S x 0.01 and a minimum of 1 is the shield-block charge, not damage.
- **Charm returned value** (Resolved, High): the routine that returns 1.25 times an item value is the hero's shield block (1.25 x the shield's stat A with an item in word 1), run when an enemy hits a guarding hero (Enemy attack damage to a hero).
- **Shot record fields** (High). Resolved: the per-hit damage of a shot is the rule in Roberta shot projectile: damage = max(0, S x C x T minus the target's defence), with the damage scale S kept in the shot's scale field (slot +0x4C, Hero shot slots), the element kind stored at launch, and the Mote's stat G read at the moment of the hit. The launch first stores max(20, 40P) in the scale field and then overwrites it with S, so the first value never takes effect. The held power field (slot +0x40) is not read by the shot's motion, life, hit or damage rules (Roberta shot: stored power reader).
- **Low-end clamp** (Resolved, High): the low value 0.1 is set on every spawn that finds a free slot, before the clamp, so the clamp always covers both ends: P below 0.1 becomes 0.1 and P above 1.0 becomes 1.0. The spawner also forms 40 x P (at least 20) in the damage-scale field and then overwrites that field with the damage scale in the same spawn, so the product has no effect. Test vectors: stored power 0.05 gives P = 0.1; 0.5 gives 0.5; 1.3 gives 1.0.
- **Roberta spawn path** (Resolved: High): the launcher check runs at the common tail on every state-13 pass, but a launch requires its fired latch clear, at least 1 MP, an animation release event, and a free shot slot (Roberta shot projectile, Launch gate). The effective quick-shot sub-states are 2, 3, 4, 10, and 11: 2 and 3 select class 1, 4 class 2, 10 the rapid homing class-0 row, and 11 the air-shot class-1 row. Full release is sub-state 9, where the attack-choice/Mote id selects the class-3 volley record and count. Sub-state 14 selects the special full-release route but its fired latch is already set, so it makes no volley. Other sub-states do not launch because their clips lack the release event or their path has no release frame. See Roberta shot projectile, Release frames, Launch class, and tables A–B.
- **Charge time factor** (Answered, High): the charge routines read the run-time tick-length word dt = 1 / frame rate, and the frame-rate setter writes this same word from the selected rate-table value (SPEC-0007). The release thresholds are separately stored as 50 ticks for Tor and 80 for Roberta. The former factor-G description confuses the tick-length multiplier used to form stored charge power with a separate creature counter threshold.
- **Conversion mode** (Resolved, High): products and sums chop toward zero and the integer conversion truncates (Damage, Platform arithmetic). A clean build must reproduce this (SPEC-0008, platform rule).
- **Target pick: stick-length gate** (resolved, High): the candidate's direction is not compared with the stick; a scaled stick length above 0.5 is required, and 0.5 or less skips the candidate. A direction match above 0.5 belongs to SPEC-0033 Behavior 22, not to this pick. The sign convention of the stick pair follows SPEC-0033 (Medium-Low there too).
- **Target pick: facing vectors** (Resolved, High for the returned fields and strict tests; Medium for semantic labels): one candidate-facing dot test uses the motion record's forward basis vector; the other uses the heading vector stored in the hero locomotion block. Each dot product must be strictly below -0.8. The later position-window projections use the motion record's position point, not another direction getter.
- **Target pick: second and third windows** (Resolved, High; Medium-High for the intent of the neighbour links): the two words at +0x54 and +0x5C are the panel record's neighbour links (SPEC-0006, type 10), tested only for non-zero. The reference vector of all three windows is the hero's position (four lanes, fourth lane 1). The second window is the position along the panel's side axis, from 1 unit inside the start (-1 with a first neighbour) to 1 unit inside the length (+1 beyond it with a last neighbour); the third is the position along the up axis, from -1 to the height minus 1. Rules and test vectors: Hero target pick; Test vectors, Target pick windows.
- **Target pick: failure result** (Answered, High): this climb-panel pick clears the target before scanning, so its 0 result leaves no target. The separate SPEC-0033 Behavior 22 ladder finder also clears its target before scanning and leaves it cleared on failure (SPEC-0033 Open questions, Behavior 22).
- **Conflict C5** (Answered, High): the actor pick, climb-panel pick and Behavior 22 predicate are three separate routines. Their short-circuit order and outcomes are specified under Cascade call site and Hero target pick.
- **Conflict C6** (resolved, Medium-High for the intent): the word +0x9BC is the stance sub-mode, and a successful pick sets it to 1 (see Hero target pick). The intent is the stance approach entry.
- **Cascade call site** (Resolved, High for the branch order and climb-panel success; partial for the third-pick consumer): actor pick runs first; the climb-panel pick runs only after it fails, and a success clears the cooldown, sends event 0x15 (state 21), and skips the third call. Behavior 22 runs only after both earlier picks fail. The third-pick result then enters later near-object and interaction handling, whose ultimate consumer of the stored target remains open (Hero target pick).
- **Clearance test: height failure** (resolved): the result is blocked (0).
- **Clearance test: footprint call** (resolved, Medium-High): the clearance test probes each blocker with the footprint family of SPEC-0018.
- **Clearance test: radius, offset and same-link skip** Resolved (High for the rules; Medium for the meaning of the shared link value): the radius is the hero radius with an extent of twice the hero height value; the query point is the hero position minus 0.25 times the side basis (+0x290) while sidle clip 79 is current and plus 0.25 times it while clip 81 is current; the same-link skip needs nonnegative kind bytes, non-null +0x48 links and equal +0x64 values in the linked records, that value being the model-set entry index of the part the blocker is bound to. The predicate's only caller is the sidle handler, state 23 is never entered with shipped data, so the whole test is dormant in play and the left/right naming does not matter. It is not the actor-to-actor clear path of SPEC-0018 (Hero clear-path predicate has the rule and test vectors).
- **Clearance test: blocking rule** (Resolved in part, High): a nearby kind -3 object blocks only when all three windows pass for that object. Failure of any window skips that object and scanning continues; a later object can still block. The third-window comparison is inclusive, with effective upper bound equal to the object extent at +0x44 minus the margin described above. The helper result is a value from an object's +0x144 field; the generic setter for that field preserves the prior value at +0x7FC when it changes. The clearance call path does not establish which object's field is supplied here, so the gameplay meaning of value 30 remains open. [High for the field/accessor boundary; unresolved for the receiver and meaning.]
- **Combat reach test: vector roles** (Resolved, High): the test axis n is the unit normal of the triangle, and b is the axis of the target's bone capsule (Combat reach test). Whether the caller normalises b is open (see the input-units item).
- **Combat reach test: anchor points** (Resolved, High): the anchors are P0, the tip this tick and the tip last tick; the steps are the slab, the shifted point, the inside test, the three edge gates and the edge cylinder test, in that order (Combat reach test).
- **Combat reach test: distance output** (Resolved, High): 0.4 (r c + h s) is the margin d of the edge gates; the test returns hit or miss only.
- **Combat reach test: callers** Resolved (High for the caller list; Medium-High for the boss owners): the hero's swing runs the test as its swept step with a target bone capsule (radius +0x44, length +0x40 of the bone record) against the triangle P0, tip now, tip last tick (High). The single-weapon reach test of enemy-kind characters and several bosses uses a fixed weapon length of 1.5 (SPEC-0029). The radius of 0.5 (with length 6.0) belongs to the bush and reed cut tests of SPEC-0060. The full list of the seven direct callers, with no indirect user, is under Combat reach test, Users.
- **Combat reach test: sign of the three-point normal** (Resolved, High): the sign does not matter (Combat reach test).
- **Helper meanings (hero reference vectors and fallback)** (Resolved, High): the target pick's two facing tests use the motion record's forward basis vector and the locomotion block's stored heading, with a strict -0.8 threshold for each. Its three panel-window projections share the hero position point from the motion record. The ground-input updater has two direct call sites for this pick, both in its local input cascade; no other direct call site appears in the boot-program code. The actor pick runs before it, and the kind -3 window predicate runs only after both earlier picks fail. A successful panel pick stores its target and enters climb state 21; a failed pick clears its target. The separate clearance helper belongs to the sidle path and is dormant with shipped state routing (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 19, 22 and 51).
- **Combat reach test: input units** (Resolved, High): n is normalised inside the test; the hero swing passes the bone frame's second row as b with no normalisation (Combat reach test, Input b).
- **Event switch** (Resolved, High): it is the common-enemy agro outer-sub-state entry (SPEC-0038 Behavior 44), and its codes are the rush gait set; the hero has no such switch.
- **Attack cue chooser tiers** (Resolved, High): the tiers are the footstep gaits (run, walk, other) and the kinds are ground surface kinds (SPEC-0031).
- **Attack cue chooser gate** (Resolved, High): the footstep gate is exact integer equality of the frame cursor (SPEC-0031).
- **Low-health warning** (Resolved, High): the tests compare the active hero's current health with 0.15 of its maximum (cue 157) and its current magic with 0.15 of its maximum (cue 158), on every tick while below; the follow-up is the HUD change-detector re-arm (SPEC-0023 section 4). 157 and 158 are cue ids.
- **Timed attack-family group** (Resolved, High): the owner is one global object, not the hero; only the mote eater fills and starts it; it runs trunc(30.0 x R) + 1 = 1801 group steps with interval trunc(1801 / n), minimum 1; each spawned entry plays its Mote's effect and drains that Mote. Each Faerie Sister's thread contributes one group step on the terminal state-9 death-animation pass that changes her base state to 16, then stops stepping on later passes (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9; SPEC-0011; High).
- Resolved: **Ten-slot timed lists.** There are two lists, each with a 1 s lifetime (the words 0x2DCB2C and 0x2DCB38 both hold 1.0, so 60 ticks): the first is the hit list of the family C beams (Roberta 169 and 171), the second the hit list of Roberta's Bubble beam (162). Each suppresses repeat hits by target id for 60 active updates, then clears the entry (High; Ten-slot timed lists; Beam and bubble lists).
- **Attack-family exclusion test (predicate resolved, High).** The filter returns 0 for every attack id when the hero word at +0x14C is zero. When +0x14C is nonzero, it returns 0 for ids 161, 162, 164, 165, 166, 169, 171 or 173 when the hero attack sub-state word +0xF90 equals 9, and returns 1 for all other inputs. This is the same sub-state field used by the two hero attack tables in state 13; this filter checks the value 9 but does not itself test the hero's main action state. In the shot-fill path this result also gates the slot-matched auxiliary-record builder: the selected shot-slot index is passed to that builder only when the result is nonzero. The callback's gameplay meaning and runtime reachability of each builder input remain open. **Attack id 163 contact cue (resolved in part):** Roberta's full-release Water shot sets a slot flag. On target contact, the ordinary shot damage path runs and disarms the slot; the updater's cleanup then requests positional sound cue 5 on the active hero's Mote-bank slot. Water id 163 selects sound block 480 there, where cue 5 is the Mote move-loop cue (SPEC-0031). Same-pass natural lifetime expiry skips this request. The separate tuning lookup selects float-table index 40, whose value 8.0 is returned as integer 8 and stored in the shared submanager for an indirect actor callback; the value's meaning remains open. Cue 5 is a sound request, not a visual effect, resource id, or model/material binding, and is separate from the Water shot's damage to its target and the Mote's release damage. High for the call, slot and lookup; callback meaning and any further presentation remain open. Whether id -1 is reachable during play also remains open.
- **Attack animation tables** (Resolved, High): the boot tables indexed by Mote id are effect-definition id tables: ten of fourteen entries and six of eight entries (Attack-family effect tables). The animation choice is a fixed rule with no table, with six fixed ids (Attack animation choice).
- **Shot-associated child-effect records.** Resolved in part (High for the layout, child-state use and direct-use boundary): six 0x1C0-byte records at hero +0x600 parallel the six 0xA0-byte shot slots. Each has three 0x90-byte child entries; their transform state is updated from the paired shot when available, and child state is cleared when unavailable. The child-entry gate is a selected child effect id other than -1; a separate effect-definition lookup falls back to id 0x193 if no definition is found, without suppressing child creation. Cleanup clears the matching record. The known hit-detection and hit-volume re-arm routines neither read these records nor call the child updater. The generic child helpers propagate transforms and manage effect-instance lifecycle/proximity, with no character-hit, damage or re-arm query. Still open: whether an indirect path through some other child-object class or callback can consume these records as combat geometry.
- **Shot offset attribution** (answered): the modulo-20 helper is the enemy navigation goal fallback of SPEC-0029 Behavior 2b; no hero-shot use is established.
- **Factor 0.9** (Resolved, High): it applies to enemy hits taken by a hero when the active petpet's kind is 1 or 7 (Enemy attack damage to a hero); it does not apply to the hero's outgoing damage.
- **State 27 constants** (resolved, High): the 0.8 reel push is a fixed program constant and 0.35 gates the vertical part of the normalised aim (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51). The boot word 0x2DD7B0 is the 0.8-second rumble duration for Tor's attack sub-state 13 and is not used by the rope-gun push.
- **Routing of states 9 and 13** (resolved, High): states 7 to 11 run the air routine of each hero (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50); state 13 the attack step.
- **Force flag of the consumable use** (Answered, High; SPEC-0021, Consumable healing and the item-use dispatch): the quick-item panel and both item-screen paths pass force 1, so a heal applies whenever the missing amount is above zero, and the 50 percent items, the Silver Negg (152) and the full-restore items (58 and 59) are usable.
- **Word numbering for elements** (Resolved, Medium-High): word 2 is the weapon Mote (attack element), word 1 the shield Mote (the element of a shield block, which the Darkest Faerie orb block also reads) and word 0 the armour Mote. The second quick panel writes a Mote into any of the three words (SPEC-0021).
- **Target type meanings** (Answered; SPEC-0029 Layout 4 and 4b): T is the row's u16 at 0x3C; its per-row values and counts are High there, its labels Medium-Low.
- **Roberta shot value** (resolved). The launch first stores the larger of 20 and 40 times the clamped charge power in the shot's scale field, then overwrites that field with the damage scale S in the same routine. The max(20, 40P) value never takes effect (Roberta shot projectile). The shot's element is stored at launch and used by the hit tests.
- **Cost of a shot with no Mote** (Resolved, High): a quick shot with no Mote pays 3, 5 or 10 MP (launch class 0, 1 or 2) on every hit of a hittable target, even at 0 damage, and no charge; a miss pays nothing. A full release with no Mote fires one class-2 shot and pays 10 MP at launch (Roberta shot projectile).
- **Attack sweeps: family and callers** Resolved (High for the Mote caller map and sweeps A to F; Medium-High for reach in play): the family-A update calls sweep A after its active check and queries the world grid at its object position. The hero object driver maps it to Roberta Lava (161), Tor Water (163), Tor Nova (172), and three Tor Rock (165) sub-shot objects with filter kind 2; Tor Lava (161) uses family B. The common family-A update also runs for singleton item effect 57, so its function identity alone does not name a Mote. The shared family-B orbit update used by Tor Lava (161) and Roberta Supernova (173) calls sweep B over the hero-owned world-breakable list. Roberta Mote attacks 169 and 171 use the shared family-C beam update, which calls sweep C over the world-grid cell list at the beam position. Sweep D runs in Tor Mote 169's active timed-object update, sweep E in Tor Mote 171's activation path, and sweep F in updates of Roberta Mote 165's three full-release skipping rocks. Sweeps C to F query the world grid at their attack-object points. Sweep C's two attacks have identical geometry and the same runtime reach: one shared update and one breakable walk, a segment 20 units long from the hero's query point (Attack sweeps). Resolved (High): the first extra walk belongs to Roberta Mote 162, the second to Roberta Supernova 173, and the third to Tor Mote 167. Their attack-state spawn requests reach the corresponding active effect subsystems subject to the shared busy gate and the local active/progress gates. No sweep or walk has an authored-encounter gate; whether a swing or beam meets a breakable depends only on the Mote being held and on the level's breakable placement. On disc only cylinder and moving-rectangle breakable volumes exist (Attack sweeps).
- **Attack sweeps: hit gate** (Resolved, High): there is one gate, the breakable's state word +0x40 (1 or 3). The owner-state reading is withdrawn.
- **Attack sweeps: kind bytes** (Resolved, Medium-High): kind 1 sphere, 2 never hit, 3 finite cylinder, 4 and 7 rectangle (static or moving); other kinds run no test (Attack sweeps).
- **Attack-id spawn dispatch: targets** (Resolved, Medium-High): the dispatch spawns the Mote power-attack object for (hero, id); Roberta's table spawns nothing for ids 160, 163, 167, 168, 170 and 172. The effect definition each spawner binds is listed in Attack-id spawn dispatch.
- **Timed glow: source** (Resolved, High): event 8 is Roberta's attack sub-state 8 (the leap), read each tick; any other sub-state stops the effect and resets the accumulator. The bounds 0.5 s and 0.8 s and the effect id 0xF1 are program constants (Timed glow).
- **Timed glow and charge thresholds** (Resolved, High): the glow accumulator is its own timer, not the charge counter and not the entry timer of Timed attack-family groups.
- **Timed glow: owner** (Resolved, High): the Mote power-attack driver object holds the accumulator and the effect slot; it runs for the active hero only, so there is one glow, and only Roberta's helper set runs it.
- **Former attack and charge driver lead** (Answered, High; SPEC-0021, Item showcase): the routine selects full-showcase pose animations 163 to 166 and handles showcase text/stingers; it is not a hero combat or charge driver. Its nonzero request field is the showcase cue/text path, and the Tor/Roberta label is the speaker name. The broader meaning of the request state and timer unit remain open in the showcase analysis.
- **Stance sub-modes (state 21)** (resolved, High): the map is right. The second interpretation is the climb entry, in which the queued panel is promoted to the current target (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **Hero state 26** (resolved, Medium): state 26 turns a statue; its search finds an object of kind 1 within 3.0 (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **Tor chained-variant counter.** Resolved (High): a repeat of the chained variant needs the repeat count at 2 or less, so at most 3 repeats; the count is reset only by the attack-start reset (Combo windows).
- Resolved: **Unnamed parameter-block fields.** The fields +0x1008 (15.0 for Tor, 10.0 for Roberta), +0x1018 (20 and 40), +0x101C (5.0 and 10.0) and +0x107C (20 and 100) are written when the hero is created and never read by any load in the program, so they have no effect on play (High; a read through a whole-block copy is not excluded). +0x1540 (5.0, Roberta only) is the near-target distance of the near-target swat (Attack input, Roberta; High). Resolved for +0x153C (10 for Roberta): it caps spin replays after the initial cycle, allowing up to 11 total spin cycles when the chain flag is supplied at each boundary. Resolved for +0x1500 (10 in both): the plunge hold threshold in ticks (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50; High).
- **Variant word** (Resolved, High; SPEC-0040, section 18): +0xF80 selects Tor (0) or Roberta (1), and +0xF90 is the attack sub-state. SPEC-0040 uses both words in its hero attack-state test; the cross-spec relationship is confirmed.
- **Tor power-attack charge** (settled, High): truncation toward zero; Tor's power attack uses 39 or 24 charge.
- **Charge routine versus state-37 phases** (resolved, High): the state-37 "phases" are its phase word: the depth check (4.5), the step past the edge, the turn (0.99), then state 30 (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **State 58 animations** (resolved, High): the state setter requests 70 on entry, then the routine requests 134 (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **State 35 phase timers** (resolved, High): 0.95 is a progress gate and 0.75 a distance gate (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51).
- **State 48 choice** (resolved, Medium-High): state 48 is the long-fall landing; 0x88 (136) and 0x89 (137) are its falling and grounded clips (SPEC-0033 Behavior 9B).
- **Attack family routines** Resolved (High): states 7 to 11 and 43 to 46 are jump states, not attack routines; the stick step is the strafe-jump direction (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50). The chooser that returns -1, 0 or 1 is the in-air ledge chooser's class result: its only caller is the in-air ledge routine, where 0 gives the ledge hang (state 30), 1 gives the climb-up (state 47 when rising, state 35 when falling) and any other value offers nothing (In-air ledge chooser). It has no role in jump attacks. The combat that does happen in the jump states (Tor's plunge and air slash, Roberta's air shots, the landing break on breakables) is under Combat in the jump states and Hero collision-kind contact tests.
- **Incoming-hit routines** (Resolved, High for the order; Medium-High for the shot contact): the hero pre-step runs no incoming-hit sweep (Incoming hits before the state switch). The collider kinds and the two cue maps belong to Roberta's shot world contact (Roberta shot projectile). Resolved: the collider shapes, the trigger and solid class bits, and the second list, which holds the cell's surface records tested with a 0.25 sphere; a contact there also disarms the shot (Roberta shot projectile, World contact; Medium for the inside of each shape test).

- **Hero collision-kind contact tests and target marker.** Resolved (High for the caller, gate and effect; Medium-High for the shape tests). The collision-kind dispatcher is the hero's landing break on world breakables: the motion step's breakable walk offers each state 1 or 3 entry to it first, a falling hero in a jump state that overlaps the entry's volume within 1.5 units of its top breaks it (or recoils on an element mismatch), and the solid response is skipped on a hit. Rules, per-kind shape tests, the disc survey (cylinders and positive-extent rectangles only) and vectors are under Hero collision-kind contact tests. It is reachable in ordinary play by any falling jump onto a breakable. Target marker: the marker is a ground effect (definition 401, four child-model emitters) under the hero, not a target mark. The earlier statement of the release gate was wrong. The gate is 1 when the ground gap (+0x24) and the reference height (+0x140) both exceed 0.1 and the hero is not (in a jump state with mode word +0x1508 equal to 6, 7, 9 or 10). The marker is released when its enable word (hero +0x17C, the ground entry of the probe) is zero or the gate is 1; otherwise it sits at (hero x, reference height + 0.02, hero z) with kind 1 for state 3, 2 for states 4 and 6, 0 otherwise. Vectors are under Target marker; with both scalars 0.2 a hero in state 13 is released, a hero in state 7 with mode 8 is released and mode 9 keeps the marker.
- **Owner of the projectile setup** (Resolved, High): it is the setup of Roberta's Rock (165) skipping shots, not an item or drop routine (Rock skipping shot).
- **Owner of the three-spoke spawner** (Resolved, High): Roberta's Rock (165) full release spawns its three rocks through it (Three-spoke spawner).
- **Projectile aim step: random or fixed** (Resolved, High): the turn fraction t is a uniform real from −0.1 to +0.1, drawn at each bounce (Rock skipping shot, Bounce).
- **Projectile aim step: second vector step** (Resolved, High): the second step removes the height part of the blended direction and renormalises it, so the stored direction is a flat unit vector.
- **Projectile start offset** (Resolved, High): the word at 0x2DCBC4 is converted to a whole number by truncation (0), so the random part is always 0 with no draw, and the stored value −0.1 is the rock's gravity per tick.
- **Projectile spoke lifetime** (Resolved, High): the value passed by the spawner is the rock's speed in units per tick, used by every tick's move.
- **Spoke lifetime second bound** (Resolved, High): the rock speed spread is −0.3 to +0.3 (the word at 0x2DCB8C and its negation). Resolved for the aimed object's countdown spread (High): its bounds are the word at 0x2DCB50 and its negation, so the range is −0.1 to +0.1 (Aimed timed object).
- **Projectile lifetime table** (Resolved): the 6 to 10 draw is the rock's ground-contact limit K, not a tick lifetime; no lifetime table is used.
- **Aimed object motion value** (resolved for Roberta Fog 166, High): the dispatcher starts this object with a direction and rate, but setup overwrites the normalized direction with zero before the first tick. The tick consumes the zero vector, so the Fog cloud has no horizontal drift on this path. The height-follow step can still change Y; no claim is made about other object types that might share the initializer or an unobserved external writer.
- **Aimed object height** (resolved, High): on each active tick the target Y is the integer truncation toward zero of the new ground-probe result plus 3.5. The movement step interpolates toward that integer target. Spawn uses the full floating-point ground height plus 3.5, and the cached ground reference remains floating point.
- **Aimed object cycle-end and re-arm routines** (resolved for operational behavior, High). Cycle end clears both timers and releases the four attached effect objects, clearing their handles; effect mode 4 then plays. Once the cycle has ended, re-arm scans all actors and submits a target-specific attack-effect request through the effect host for every actor passing its class-specific eligibility query and the strict 10-unit distance test. The request uses the first Attack spawn routine (type 23, sub-type 5) with Fog attack id 166. Any qualifying actor is sufficient to reset the countdown and play effect mode 1. The target-specific request creates a visual effect-pool object whose active update performs effect and attachment upkeep, without a target contact or damage step; it does not itself apply damage. Its appearance is definition 982 in three slots (Attack spawn, Definitions shown; High).
- **Attack spawn: effect and sub-type choices** (Resolved, High): the effect shown for each attack id and hero is in Effect shown for each attack (both heroes, every Mote); the message's sub-type is the attack's element number (5 for Fog, 7 for Supernova), and its six definition slots are in Attack spawn. Resolved (High): the duration is the pooled object's lifetime in ticks; a nonnegative stored value D yields D + 1 active updates (Attack spawn). Resolved (Medium-High): the look-up arguments 0xA6 and 0xAD are the Mote ids 166 and 173, and the first routine is the hit of Roberta's Fog cloud (Attack spawn).
- **Time base** (resolved, High). The time base word at 0x368FA0 is run-time and not in the disc image. It is written once at start-up from the boot rate table at data address 0x30C598 (SPEC-0007, Rate selection), and holds 60 ticks per second.
- **Generator** (resolved, High). The generator is the shared Mersenne Twister (standard 624-entry generator and tempering). Its state object is at 0x39BE50 (624 entries in 8-byte slots; index word at 0x39D1D0, set to 625 = not seeded by a start-up initialiser). It is seeded on its first draw from the BCD date and time bytes of the console's real-time clock, so the sequence differs per boot and a clean build cannot (and need not) reproduce it. The words at 0x2EC9B8 are not state: they are the generator's two-entry twist constant pair in 8-byte slots (entry 0 = 0, entry 1 at 0x2EC9C0 = the standard twist constant 0x9908B0DF).
- **Mode-code meanings** (Resolved, High): table A is Tor's step factor D and table B Roberta's (Mode-code and flag multipliers).
- **Flag factor identity** (Resolved, High): the flags are curse and strength (statuses 1 and 4), and the hero damage path uses the 0.75 copy.
- **Anchor sockets and follow** (settled as far as the code goes; D for the rest). The socket refresh and nearest-object helpers write four candidate positions/ids into a separate owner record whose owner is not named by any caller in the code. The 0.5 follow blend is resolved to the active-hero per-frame rendering/interaction path and is gated by the hero's attach-mode flag. The randomized aim-command routine stores a target point and can send a second command with probability 1/2, but it has no direct caller in the known call graph; its owner and its relationship to the socket records are developer intent and not encoded anywhere (D). Default (rewrite choice): reproduce the documented four-slot selection, hero attach-mode gate, and half-way presentation blend only; do not infer a hero movement effect or bind the separate aim command to a particular attack or actor.
- **Oscillator** (resolved in part, Medium). The phase gets one ±6.283186 correction per tick, and the callback is invoked on every update whose elapsed counter is at or above its limit; there is no one-shot guard in the oscillator routine. Two effect-object paths call it directly. The sine/cosine kernel is settled (Resolved, proactive (open close-out), High): it is the shared folded series of SPEC-0034 section 12, matching the ideal sine and cosine to about 5e-8. Their object identities and callback effects remain open (needs a runtime check; default: invoke the callback as described above).
- **Shot slot position** Resolved (High for the point/vector W lanes, the 3D update and the collision-helper dataflow; the XYZ-only counterfactual is not a variant of the game: the rectangle and surface-record tests use the plane form n . p + d, see World contact). Launch writes the shot body anchor with W=1; each active position update adds a displacement with W=0 and restores the point W to 1. Enemy bone-capsule tests and first-list kind-1/2/3 world shape tests use XYZ-only distance/projection reductions. First-list kind-4/7 box tests and the second-list surface-record test include four-lane dot products; their raw record vectors are not transformed to cancel W before these reductions. The shipped type-11 kind-4/7 and type-5 second-list records carry nonzero W values in the vectors those helpers consume, so W contributes to their projected geometry. Concrete synthetic-query example: using resource 678's kind-4 stored point as `P≈(-858.3405,48.9700,1051.1857,1)` and its +0x40 vector `A≈(0.3542007,0,0.9351695,-679.012)`, XYZW gives approximately `-0.000008`, while XYZ-only gives approximately `679.012`. The four-lane value is the point's signed distance from the plane (offset in lane W), so the shot position needs W = 1 and the displacement W = 0 for the contact tests to be right. Test vector: plane normal (0, 1, 0) with offset -5: the point (0, 7, 0, 1) is 2 units from the plane; the displacement (0, 1, 0, 0) projects to 1 (World contact).
- **Shot slot scaled vector** (resolved, High). The vector and the scale that this item named are the launch direction (slot +0x20) and the speed (slot +0x44). The step helper returns direction times speed times the tick length, the per-tick displacement of the shot (Hero shot slots). Which callers use the helper is not identified (Low).
- **Shot slot radius and armed word writers** Resolved (High). The launch writes the radius (1.0 or 3.0 from the rows of Roberta shot projectile), the speed, the life and a nonzero handle to the armed word of the slot it fills. Nothing else ever changes the radius. The armed word becomes nonzero only at a launch. It is cleared by: the per-tick shot update itself (life reaching 0, the ground end, the world contact and the shot's own hits, all direct stores of 0), the single disarm routine that the enemy and boss hit tests call (eleven call sites, Hero shot slots in enemy hit tests), and the two clear-all routines that zero all six armed words (at hero creation and in the hero's reset). Every store to the slot area in the whole program and in all level modules was searched: there is no other writer. Test vector: slot 2 armed with life 1 at the start of the tick: the update lowers life to 0 and the word is cleared; a quick shot that touches a Spyder valve or a Black Knight weak point is disarmed once, by the disarm routine, and that test returns a hit.
- **Landing choice thresholds** (Resolved, High; Landings): both thresholds are tested in the same tick against the same fall height, so the 4.0 shake test (taken only after the 3.0 hard-landing test failed and the stick is centred) never passes in play. Test vector: fall height 3.0 (not above 3.0) with a centred stick is the soft landing with no shake; 3.01 is the hard landing. 3.0 (0x2DD828) is the hard-landing fall height and 4.0 (0x2DD814) the soft-landing camera-shake height. The flag at hero +0x1514 is never set to a non-zero value, so it does not affect the landing in play (Resolved, Medium-High; see Landing test of sub-kind 5). The writers of air phase 5 are the let-go and stop-climbing exits and the state-47 flag branch (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50).
- **Boosted-jump exit** (Resolved, High): the stick-forward state-4 branch is checked only while clip 0x85 is the current action and its progress is below 0.99; the near-completion branch requests clip 0x4E. Neither hero air routine checks clip 0x4E for the same exit. Ordinary landing transitions remain separate (SPEC-0033 Behavior 50).
- **Roberta shot: slot offsets** (Resolved, Medium-High). Slot k starts at hero offset 0x10D0 plus 0xA0 times k. The earlier base of 0x1100 is the position field of slot 0 (slot +0x30), so both descriptions name the same fields: the radius at slot +0x48 (hero 0x1118 for slot 0), the armed word at slot +0x58 (hero 0x1128) and the speed at slot +0x44 (hero 0x1114). The field once called the slot scale is the speed. The word at 0x1128 is the armed word of slot 0, not a live count (Hero shot slots).
- **Roberta shot: stored power reader** (Answered, High within the shot subsystem): the hero's held-power value is read at launch, clamped to 0.1–1.0 and copied to the shot's power field. The shot's motion, life, hit and damage rules do not read that field, so it has no effect on shot behavior (Roberta shot projectile; Charge completion). Test vector: 80 held ticks gives a stored power of about 0.667; 120 ticks gives 1.0 after clamping. Neither value changes the shot's motion, life, hit or damage rules.
- **Roberta shot: reaction and homing rows** (Resolved, High): the quick rows and the default row carry no reaction (-1), and every row except the class-0 row of sub-state 10 has homing 0, so the default row flies straight (Roberta shot projectile, table A).
- **Roberta shot: modes 1 to 5** (Resolved, High): all five modes steer with the same 0.25 blend; mode 1 uses the locked auto-target and modes 2 to 5 pick a random visible enemy within 60 units and fan the direction. The second per-shot value of the launch is the shot's target (Roberta shot projectile, Launch and Homing).
- **Roberta shot: launch direction** (Resolved, High): d = unit(aim point - body anchor), with the aim point from the auto-target, the right-stick aim point, or hero position + 20 × facing + (0, 2, 0), and the fan for modes 2 to 5 (Roberta shot projectile, Launch).
- **Roberta shot: ids 162, 169 and 171** (Resolved, High): their full release spawns the Mote object, sets the fired flag (no volley), enters sub-state 14 and pays MP and charge at once; their damage comes only from the object's hits (Power attack and full release).
- **Roberta shot: once-per-attack flag** (Resolved, High): the launch fires on the frame with event bit 0x20, once per sub-state; Roberta's charge routine clears the fired flag at every sub-state change, so the launch does not repeat inside one sub-state. Sub-states 5, 6, 7, 8 and 12 never launch (Roberta shot projectile, Launch gate and Release frames).
- **Roberta full-release gate** (Resolved, High): for the volley ids the first shot pays MP and charge at its launch, so a first shot that misses still costs both. The launcher first requires an unfired sub-state, at least 1 MP and the release-frame event; the shot builder then requires a free slot before payment. A failed gate or a full slot set means no shot initialization and no payment for that attempt (Roberta shot projectile, Launch gate).
- **Full-release scale table** (Resolved, High): T for a full-release shot is the attack power table value for (Roberta row, the word-2 Mote) (Roberta shot projectile).
- **Word-2 item hook** (Resolved, High): the hook is Mote charge use (SPEC-0021, Mote charge). It never deals damage; a quick shot's hit uses exactly Q of charge (Mote charge use by attacks).
- **Tor power-attack gate** (Resolved, High): the release pays charge and MP with no hit test and deals no damage itself.
- **Contact handler walks** (Resolved: High): the nine static walk-to-handler mappings and their Mote or attack-object callers are listed under Contact routines and cues. They resolve the dispatch chain, not encounter-specific activation.
- **Enemy hit rule coverage** (Resolved, High): the shield, armour and petpet rule of Enemy attack damage to a hero is used by the common enemies (class 17, including the minions and spiders); enemy pool projectiles use the pool's own rule, which also applies shield and armour (SPEC-0029). No boss melee hit uses either: boss melee values reach the take-hit unchanged.
- **Blocked flag per attacker** (Resolved, High; Medium-High for naming each fixed-flag call): the take-hit never looks at the attacker's kind; the flag comes from the caller. With the flag set the chain is: hit cue and effect, the state-16 return, then, unless the hero is busy, the block reaction (shield-bank cue 0, then request 64: Tor clip 51 for 16 ticks, Roberta clip 23 for 44 ticks; no state write and no state 17), the weapon draw, the stun clear and the deduction of the full value. Of the 65 call sites in the boot program, 53 pass a fixed flag of 0 (unblockable: the other boss melee, hazards and scripted hits), 7 pass a fixed 1 and 5 compute the flag from their own block test. The fixed 1 calls are the Ixi Chieftain's forward swipe and the Werelupe King's shared hit (value 0 with the flag), the Gnarfas whip and rapid strike (their full 15 on the blocked branch), the class-17 strike whose attack forces the flag, and the hero's poison tick (kind 3, attacker id -2). The computed flags belong to the class-17 strike, the enemy pool shots, the common-enemy contact resolution and one further boss routine. The Gelert Assassin, the Black Knight's two swings and the Plague Serpent's lunge skip the take-hit on a block. Test vector: a poison tick on an idle Tor deducts its value, plays shield-bank cue 0 and request 64 and writes no state; the same tick on a hero busy in the charge step plays nothing. The per-boss facing tests are in the boss specs.
- **Guard timer** (Resolved, High): the threat sense of the idle hero sets +0x1574 to 40 ticks when a shield is equipped, weapons are drawn, and an attacking enemy within 10 units or a live enemy projectile is in front (dot above 0.5); the hero then raises the shield (request 63) and counts as guarding (Guard timer and per-attacker block).
- **Shield types per hero** (Resolved, High). The equipment entry is chosen by type: types 4, 5 and 6 go to Tor's entry of their family and types 1, 2 and 3 to Roberta's. So Tor's shields are type 5 (ids 106 to 110) and Roberta's are type 2 (ids 176 to 180, the shield books). Roberta's shield-block charge stat is therefore always 0 and her shield Mote spends 1 per blocked hit (Enemy attack damage to a hero).
- **Hit list clear** (Resolved, High): the list is cleared at every sub-state change of both heroes' charge routines, at entry to state 13, at the attack-start reset, on every tick of state 12, at character creation and at each enemy attack start (Hit detection, Hit-list clear).
- **Pose timing** (Resolved, High). The hero swing, the attack window and the shot launch read the hero's pose of the previous pass, and test the enemies' bones of this pass; enemy hits on the hero read the hero's previous pose; the Mote objects use this pass's pose (Hit detection, Tick order of the tests).
- **Swept test internals** (Resolved, High): the steps and the three points are in Combat reach test.
- **Mote object pairing** (Resolved in part, High): the object per (hero, Mote) is in Charm attack hits, Object pairing; Fire 160 has no object for either hero and Lava 161 is family B for Tor and family A for Roberta. Family-A object counts are now established: Roberta Lava, Tor Water and Tor Nova each use one object; Tor Rock uses three stationary sub-shots. Each object's hit radius follows its own particle's size, and the object ends when that particle's life runs out (Lava 161 about 0.8 s, radius 0.5 to 25; Water 163 0.7 s, 0.5 to 15; Nova 172 1.2 s, 0 to 18; Rock sub-shots 0.6 s, 0 to 20; numbers in Family A users). Resolved: family C uses a fixed collision-box half-width of 1.0 (total width 2.0), rather than a width read from effect definitions 0x222 and 0x224. For Roberta's Shadow and Sun beams, activation also binds two visual effect instances (definitions 0x222 and 0x224) to the segment; each active tick rebuilds the segment from the hero's query point to 20 units along normalized aim. The beam ends when its 5-second count falls below 1, releases both visual instances and clears its target list. Exact mesh, material and particle appearance of those instances is not established here. **[High for collision, timer, segment update and release; visual asset appearance unresolved]**
- **Body cylinder origin** (Resolved, High): the position point is the feet, and the cylinder runs from the feet to the feet plus the height (Hit detection, hurt shapes). Test vectors: radius 1.0, height 3.5, feet at (0, 0, 0); a sphere of radius 2.5 at (3.0, 1.0, 0) hits with depth 0.5 (axial offset -0.75 from the centre (0, 1.75, 0), within 1.75; side distance 3.0 below 3.5); at (3.6, 1.0, 0) it misses. A sphere of radius 0.5 at (1.2, 3.0, 0) hits with depth 0.3 (axial offset 1.25, side distance 1.2 below 1.5); a cylinder centred on the feet would miss it.
- **Swing constants** (Answered): the 65.0 gate, the 0.25 centre offset and the radius 1.0 are program constants with no data word (Hit detection, step 2).
- **Attack windows at other play rates** (Resolved, High). No attack step changes the play rate. Only slow changes it during an attack, to 0.75, and only in Tor's attack sub-states 2 to 12, his air attacks and Roberta's swats (Hit detection, Windows at other play rates).

- **Shot target gates and arming state** (Resolved, High): projectile collision requires either the actor eligibility method to pass, or a guarding target while the arming counter is 0; it then requires the target's animation/skeleton data and at least one bone whose hurt-capsule length is positive, and rejects targets marked hidden by the invisible-kind predicate. The positive-length check is an early prerequisite over the same bone records that the later capsule loop tests individually; +0x40 is the capsule length, not a timer. This is separate from status-effect duration timers (Status timing). The random finder scans in global-list order and stores only its first ten passing the actor eligibility method (+0xCC), horizontal range and three-ray visibility; it chooses uniformly from those stored entries and does not replace them with later candidates. It does not require a positive-length capsule, so selection does not guarantee projectile collision. The arming counter is slot +0x7C (Roberta shot projectile).
- **Enemy-kind hittable tests** (Resolved, High; Medium for the meaning of the second actor): the six tests are listed under Hit detection, Hittable predicate. None is a status test: they are the script hold, the two dodges, the invisible-kind hide, the petpet kinds and the hit-reaction slide animations.

- **Melee contact mismatch.** Resolved: there is no action state 4 on this route. The value 4 is the recoil material word, and the reaction is Tor's attack sub-state 13 (SPEC-0048, Element recoil).
- **Mote charge display word** (Resolved in part, Medium-High; SPEC-0021): the 240 value is a HUD display timer of 4 s (SPEC-0023). A failed power-ready check sets four such timers (0x2DCD60, 0x2DCD64, 0x2DCD6C and 0x2DCD78) to 240 (Power-ready check).
- **Family A users** (Resolved, High for the emitted-particle association, object counts and emitter identity): Roberta Lava (161), Tor Water (163) and Tor Nova (172) each use one family-A object; Tor Rock (165) uses three stationary sub-shots. Each associated record is an emitted particle from a paired effect emitter; the particle supplies the collision radius and the age value used by the Family-A cutoff comparison against +0x14C. The generic particle updater also uses +0x14C as its curve-progression denominator, so this is not a universal lifespan label. Resolved (High): the association is not taken from whatever emitters overlap the object. Each family-A object binds one effect definition of its own at set-up, and the birth hook is installed only on the emitters of that definition. Roberta Lava 161 binds 415, Tor Water 163 binds 416, Tor Nova 172 binds 560, and each of the three Tor Rock 165 sub-shots binds 553. The other definitions started by these attacks (564 for Water, 562 for Rock) run as plain effects with no hook. Every particle born by a hooked emitter overwrites the single association slot, so the stored particle is the last one born. All emitters of these definitions fire their single burst on the same first running tick, and emitters are stepped newest-first, so the first-listed emitter of the definition is stepped last and supplies the final particle. Conditional disc-definition values are summarized below. Resolved (High): which definitions each power attack spawns: Tor Water (163) starts 416, which carries 564 as a nested definition; Tor Nova (172) starts 560; Tor Rock (165) starts 562, whose child is 553; Roberta's Lava (161) starts 415. No other definition is started by these attacks. Resolved (High for the emitter, Medium-High for the tick counts): the particle that feeds each object, with the radius and life it gives. The hit radius is the particle's current size-x value (the size-x curve of its emitter sampled at age divided by life, table below); the object ends on the first tick where the particle's age plus one tick (1/60 s) reaches its life.
  - Roberta Lava 161 (definition 415, one emitter, 4 particles): life 0.78 to 0.82 s (the last-born particle's draw), so the object lasts 47 to 49 ticks; radius 0.5 at birth rising to 25.0.
  - Tor Water 163 (definition 416, one emitter, 3 particles): life 0.7 s, 42 ticks; radius 0.5 rising to 15.0.
  - Tor Nova 172 (definition 560, three emitters): the stored particle comes from the first-listed emitter (5 particles, life 1.2 s, 72 ticks); radius 0 rising to 18.0. The other two emitters (life 0.2 s and 0.5 to 1.5 s) never supply the particle.
  - Tor Rock 165 sub-shots (definition 553, one emitter, 1 particle): life 0.6 s, 36 ticks each; radius 0 rising to 20.0.
  Test vectors: a Water object spawned on tick 0 has radius 0.5 on its first tick, 15.0 near its last, and ends when the particle's age reaches 41 ticks (age plus 1/60 s is then 0.7); a Nova object is harmless on its first tick (radius 0) and lasts 72 ticks; the three Rock sub-shots started on Rock ticks 1, 17 and 33 end about 36 ticks later, on ticks 37, 53 and 69, and each has a radius that starts at 0 and reaches 20.0. Resolved (High): each of the four radius curves is a straight line in the particle's age. The size row that applies to the x component is the first rate row for the whole life (the first threshold is 2.0, above any life fraction), so radius = start + rate x age / life with (start, rate) = (0.5, 24.5) for definition 415, (0.5, 14.5) for 416, (0, 18.0) for 560's first emitter and (0, 20.0) for 553; the life is that particle's own (0.78 to 0.82, 0.7, 1.2 and 0.6 s). Test vectors: Water at age 0.35 s: 0.5 + 14.5 x 0.5 = 7.75; Nova at 36 ticks: 9.0; Lava with life 0.80 at 24 ticks (0.4 s): 0.5 + 24.5 x 0.5 = 12.75. Tor Rock motion is answered in the next item.
The rows for definitions 564 and 562 and the second and third emitters of 560 are listed for reference only; the stored particle comes from the rows named under Family A users. The initializer sets particle +0x14C from emitter A+0x78 with a random offset bounded by A+0x7C; the table reports the resulting candidate range for the Family-A cutoff-comparison field. The raw size-x curve is the channel-2 value read at particle +0xB0. Curve endpoints are calculated continuously over normalized progress from 0 to 1; frame stepping can alter the last sample slightly.

| Candidate owner and effect | Definition node | Emission count / period | Candidate +0x14C range (s) | Raw size-x curve over progress |
| --- | ---: | --- | ---: | --- |
| Roberta Lava 161 (415) | 776 | 4 / one burst | [0.78, 0.82) | 0.50 rises to 25.0 |
| Tor Water 163 (416) | 1567 | 3 / one burst | 0.70 | 0.50 rises to 15.0 |
| Tor Water 163 (564) | 1568 | 10 / 0.10 s period | [0.70, 0.90) | 0.30 holds to progress 0.635, then falls to approximately 0 |
| Tor Nova 172 (560) | 1600 | 5 / one burst | 1.20 | 0 rises to 18.0 |
| Tor Nova 172 (560) | 1601 | 1 / one burst | 0.20 | 13.70 falls to 0 |
| Tor Nova 172 (560) | 1602 | 25 / one burst | [0.50, 1.50) | 0.40 rises to 1.50 at progress 0.20, then falls to approximately 0 |

- **Tor Rock 165 sub-shots** Resolved (High): three family A objects (filter 2) started at the release point on the Rock object's ticks 1, 17 and 33, each dealing trunc(F × P(Tor, 165)) per hit with take-hit flags (−1, 0, 2, 0) (Charm attack hits). They remain stationary at their launch point while active. Each sub-shot binds definition 553, whose single particle (life 0.6 s, so 36 ticks) supplies the hit radius, a straight line from 0 to 20.0 (radius = 20 x age / 0.6, 0.5556 per tick), and the life; each has its own hit list with 60-tick entries, so a target is hit at most once per sub-shot. The volume is the family-A vertical cylinder of height 4.0 centred on the release point. Rule and vectors: Family A users and Tor Rock 165, under Charm attack hits.
- **Right-stick aim mode** (Resolved, High): entry on right stick Y off centre in state 1, or in attack sub-states 1, 9 or 14 when the stored hero flag is 1; exit outside states 1 and 13; the reticle appears from the 61st tick (Right-stick aim mode). The flag is set from held R2/Circle during state 13, R2 wins when both are held, and neither held retains its prior value. The semantic limit is its last button choice; another consumer is not fully characterized (Medium-High).
- **Done tick at the 0.99 gate** (Resolved, High): the exact tick follows from the progress rule of SPEC-0005 (cursor plus stored fraction, finished flag at the last key) and the router's order (done test, then F + 1, then the press tests): done on tick T + L + 1 for a one-shot clip of L ticks requested on tick T. The vectors of Attack router use it. The order of the hero update and the animation pass inside one tick is settled (High; SPEC-0007, Order of work inside one tick, item 8): the update runs first, then the motion tick, then the animation pass, so a progress read made in the update sees the pass of the previous tick.
- **Tor arm-table point** (resolved in part): the point computed at 0.6 x reach ahead of the weapon anchor is passed to the hero's locomotion sub-object setter, which stores the vector at sub-object +0x50. A candidate-selection path reads the active hero's shared +0x50 vector as its search anchor and chooses the nearest eligible candidate by squared 3D distance. Because other setters can overwrite this shared field and the candidate path does not identify the writer, this does not prove that it reads the Tor arm-table value; that end-to-end dataflow remains open. No attack-motion or combat-targeting role is established. [High for the field consumer; open for the Tor-write-to-reader link]
- **Stale gesture mode** Resolved (High for the order and the rule; Medium-High for a pass that is busy and still reaches the selector). Rule for a rewrite: each hero input pass runs, in this order, the busy test, the gesture step (given the busy flag) and the common action selector. In a busy pass the gesture step zeroes its counters and state and leaves the mode word (hero +0x9A4) as it was. In a non-busy pass the step writes mode 0 in its Idle and first states before the selector runs, writes 1 while a flick is live and 2 or 3 in the two turn gestures (SPEC-0033 Behavior 52). The attack-start router has exactly four direct callers and no other entry (no data pointer to it): three in the action selector and one in the sidle handler. Test vector: mode 1 left by a flick, then one busy pass, then a non-busy pass with an attack press: the busy pass keeps mode 1; the non-busy pass writes 0 first, so the press starts the normal attack (sub-state 1 for Tor), not the flick lunge. Also: a busy pass in which the selector starts an attack (the selector runs on busy passes too, if the hero is in a state that lets it) starts the variant of the still-stored mode. Detail of the order: a busy pass resets the gesture machine's state and counters but preserves its mode word. The hero input update calls the gesture machine before the common action selector. On the first pass whose busy input is clear, the machine begins in Idle and writes mode 0 before the action selector runs. The selector's attack-start branches are downstream of this write, so they cannot consume the preserved value on that busy-to-nonbusy pass, regardless of which eligible input branch reaches them. In the action selector, its direct attack-start branches lie within the input/control path, after the applicable global-control and current-state checks. Depending on the branch, an accepted attack-input edge/result or an alternate accepted input condition sets state 13 and invokes the attack-start router; the input conditions are described under Attack input. The other known direct attack-start call is behind state 23, which shipped disc data never enters (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51). Thus stale-mode consumption is not supported for either known ordinary route on the first nonbusy pass. Mode 1–3 still selects variants when a live gesture has set it (Attack router; SPEC-0033 Behavior 52).

- **Tor Mote object placement (item 4 open).** Resolved:
  1. Resolved (High): Wind (167) and Nova (173) centres. the centre word (+0x80) is read back from the object's attached effect point each tick, after the group update sets the object's transform. Nova's transform is the hero anchor plus (0, 1.5, 0) at spawn and on every tick, so its centre follows the hero and does not stay at the spawn point. Wind's transform is the hero anchor's horizontal position with a height from the ground probe of SPEC-0018 started 8.0 units above the anchor; the probe returns the ground height under the anchor (or its miss value, -1.0e9, when there is no ground). At spawn the transform is the raw anchor, and the stored previous height is the anchor's height. Neither centre is fixed after spawn. Wind's height guard keeps the previous height whenever the new height differs from it by 1e8 or more, which happens only on a probe miss; so the Wind centre sits on the ground under the hero and holds its last height over a gap with no ground. Test vector: hero anchor (10, 5, 20) with ground height 2.0 under it: Wind's transform is (10, 2.0, 20) on the next tick; the same hero over a gap with no ground, previous height 2.0: the transform stays (10, 2.0, 20). Nova with the hero anchor (10, 5, 20): transform (10, 6.5, 20), recomputed from the hero each tick. The centre is read from the effect point attached to that transform.
  2. Anchor offsets (High; run-time words written by the start-up initialiser): (0, 2.0, 0) for Tor's Lava orbit (161) and for Roberta's Supernova orbit (173); (0, 1.5, 0) for Tor's Nova cylinder (173). Each of these three objects uses the hero anchor plus its offset at spawn and on every tick.
  3. Tor Lava orbit (High for the centre; Medium-High for the angle rule): the centre is the anchor raised by 2.0, and the orbit's offset is in Behavior above (Family B). The starting angle is 0 at the first activation and continues from the previous orbit's angle afterwards. The orbit is horizontal, so the query point is at the anchor height plus 2.0.
  4. Family A radii and lives (Resolved; High for the values below and for which emitter feeds each Mote, see Family A users): the spawn routines store no radius or count for Tor Water (163), Tor Nova (172) or the Tor Rock sub-shots (165). Their collision radius is the size of the emitted particle and their active state is the particle's age cutoff. The candidate effect definitions (SPEC-0034 section 2) carry these emitter values, given as the start size vector (x, y, z), the spawn ring radius, the particle life in seconds with its jitter, the particles per burst and the burst period:
     - definition 416 (Tor Water candidate): one emitter, size (0.5, 3.0, 0.5), no ring, life 0.7 with no jitter, 3 particles, one burst;
     - definition 564 (Tor Water candidate): one emitter, size (0.3, 0.3, 0.3), ring radius 1.0 filled at exactly the radius, life 0.8 with jitter 0.1, 10 particles every 0.1 s;
     - definition 560 (Tor Nova candidate): three emitters, each a single burst: size (0, 3.0, 0) with life 1.2 and 5 particles; size (13.704, 13.704, 13.704) with life 0.2 and 1 particle; size (0.4, 0.4, 0.4) with life 1.0 and jitter 0.5 and 25 particles;
     - definition 553 (Tor Rock candidate): one emitter, single burst, size (0, 2.0, 0), life 0.6, 1 particle;
     - definition 562 (Tor Rock candidate): two emitters, each a single burst: size (1.6, 1.6, 1.6) with life 3.0 and jitter 0.2 and 20 particles; size (4.0, 4.0, 4.0) with life 1.5 and jitter 0.02 and 1 particle.
     Test vector (Medium-Low, under the x-component rule): a particle born from definition 560's third emitter has collision radius 0.4 and life 1.0 with jitter 0.5, so its life lies between 0.5 and 1.5 s (30 to 90 ticks). Resolved: the particle stored for each Mote and its radius component (size x) are given under Family A users above; the vector here uses definition 560's third emitter only as an example of the size rule, and that emitter never supplies the stored particle. Each object's spawn point is stored at its attach point (+0x40, +0x120 and +0x3F0 respectively).
  5. Tor Shadow (169) and Sun (171) (High for the words; Medium for the motion): Shadow's radius is the first spawn argument, 10.0, stored at +0x54; its life is the second argument, 0.5 s, so 30 ticks, counted down each tick, and it ends on the tick the count reaches 0. Its centre is the point at +0x100, set to the spawn point and rewritten from its attached effect each tick; it does not follow the hero, since the driver passes it no position. Sun's radius is +0x400 = 10.0 (first argument); its countdown +0x408 is 0 at spawn (trunc(10 × 0.0)), so it becomes active (marker −1) on the next tick. Its centre is +0x410 (set at spawn to the hero anchor raised by 1.5 and re-read from its attached effect). Sun keeps no hit list (High): on its first active tick (the tick after the spawn, when the countdown 0 becomes the active marker) it tests every live hittable character once against the sphere of radius 10.0 around the centre, and each one inside takes one hit (take-hit, then confusion with the Tor duration, plus a hit effect at the target); the world breakables are walked once at the same moment (Attack sweeps, sweep E). No target is listed, none can be hit twice, and no later tick hits anything, so there is no slot count and no lifetime to give. The structure at +0x140 is not a hit list: it is a set of visual effect instances that the object rescales every tick by the progress value of its attached effect (+0x90) and clears when that effect ends. Lifetime (High for the rule, Medium-High for the tick count): the object stays live while any of four things lasts: its countdown, the emitters of its own base effect (definition 559: three single-burst emitters with particle lives 0.3, 0.5 and 2.0 plus or minus 1.0 s), the particle of its second effect (definition 566, life 0.7 s, 42 ticks) and the continuous ring effect it rescales (definition 568), which the object clears itself on the tick the second effect's particle expires. So the object ends when the longest-lived particle of definition 559 has died, which is the third emitter's 18 particles with lives uniform from 1.0 to 3.0 s: at most 3.0 s (180 ticks) after its first active tick and about 2.9 s (174 ticks) on average. Test vector: if the slowest of the 18 particles has life 2.4 s, the object ends 144 ticks after its first active tick, and the ring effect stops at tick 42. Test vector (hits): Tor casts Sun with enemies A and B at 4.0 and 12.0 units from the centre and a barrel at 3.0: on the first active tick A takes one hit and is confused and the barrel is tested; B is untouched; if A stays in range for the remaining ticks it is never hit again. The object has no combat role after its first active tick; a rewrite that does not simulate the particles may use 3.0 s as the lifetime (a rewrite choice).
  6. The 0.2 step (Resolved, High for the gate and the target): the position helpers add 0.2 times the unit vector toward a fixed object position, and only when a gate argument is non-zero. The Mote objects never take the step: the spawn dispatch and the per-tick driver of the Mote objects, which serves both heroes (the sites of Tor's and Roberta's objects alike), pass a zero gate at every call. The step is taken only by the hero's weapon-point routines (the weapon base and tip points), which pass a non-zero gate (Medium for which consumers use them: the swing test and the effect driver of SPEC-0034 section 3.17). The target is the camera object that the start-up code assigns (data address 0x32D060; its position word at +0x30 is the camera's eye), so those points move 0.2 toward the camera. It is not the hero's target pick. Test vector: hero position (10, 0, 5), facing (0, 0, 1), reach 1.5: a Mote object spawns at (10, 0, 6.5); with the camera eye at (10, 0, 15) the weapon tip point is (10, 0, 6.7).
- **Reflected shots and class-17 strike curse scale (R-0034; Resolved).** (1) Resolved: the four HUD timers are given in the HUD display timers entry of the layout section (C, G0, G1, and a separate Mote show countdown; D is not among them). (2) Resolved (High): the pool-shot block is the shield block b of this section, applied with the blocked flag set and the shot's element. With no block S is 0. The blocked value max(1, 0.25 D) is taken after the armour (D already includes it; SPEC-0029 Behavior 9). (3) Resolved (High): a reflected shot's charge is taken from the word-1 Mote: amount = round half away from zero of Q x s x 0.01, with Q = 1 and s as in the shield block (stat B for type 5, stat D for type 3, else 0). There is no minimum of 1, no magic payment, and the amount is taken only when a shield-family entry exists. The reflect test reads the word-1 Mote's element kind, and a shot of element 0 is never reflected (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9). (4) Resolved (High): a class-17 strike uses the attacker's enemy scale: 0.5 under curse, 1.5 under strength, 0.75 with both. The hero's 0.75 applies to hero attacks only (Enemy attack damage to a hero).
- **Roberta shot details (R-0041; Resolved).** (1) Resolved, High: the shot starts at the hero's posed attach point 0, the translation of that point in the current pass (Spawn point), not a fixed offset from the hero. (2) Resolved, Medium-High: each slot has a visual record in the effect host (six records of 0x1C0 bytes, from host offset 0x600, one per slot), whose effect id is looked up at launch. A full-release shot takes its id from a 14-entry boot table keyed by the Mote id (or the slot table when no Mote is equipped); a quick shot takes it from a boot table selected by the launch class and the slot (separate tables for classes 0, 1 and 2; class 3 has none). The table values are disc data the rewrite reads from the user's files; no ids are listed here. The shot's word at +0x64 is read by a second draw path, but no writer of that word was found, so that path draws nothing (Medium). (3) Resolved, Medium-High: Roberta's body entry is empty when she first joins (no body-family item with a source reaches hero 1's entry on the join path), and an empty body entry shows outfit 0, model resource 512. Each save writes both heroes' body entries (SPEC-0025, tails), so a save records each hero's outfit. (4) Resolved, Medium-High: Roberta's model faces +Z like Tor's (SPEC-0004, Coordinate conventions); idle clips give the root record zero travel. Idle clips do turn bone 0 by their own yaw values (see the first line of Open questions). (5) Resolved, High: the leap and landing steps are the ones in Roberta leap and landing, checked against the sub-state 8 and 13 routines (the counter, the 0.99 end gate, cue 17 when the counter reads 2, the 0.98 facing test, the 0.25 turn rate and the 10-tick cap). Answered: homing modes 0 and 1 aim at the first entry of the 15-entry auto-target list whose valid flag is set (aim rule above).
